# unitytk — Changelog

## 2026
- **2026-11-10 -- New feature Lods: one LODGroup per LOD group a delivery's `lods` record names (`Features/Lods/Editor/LodsImporter.cs`, the generated `LodsRecord.g.cs`; `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs` `Lods`; the package README's `lods` channel and Lods feature rows).** Unity's FBX import puts ONE LODGroup on a model's root, merging every `_LODn` mesh of the file by level index, and ignores the FBX's thresholds (measured, Unity 6000.3); the importer gives each group's node its own LODGroup at the heights the DCC set, and every LODGroup Unity made keeps only what no group claims (gone when that is nothing). Each level binds the way its group does: by exact name, else by namespace-stripped leaf (a tie is said and the level left out). A level the file lacks is said, the next one kept drawing its range and the last kept the group's cull; heights that miss a level are said and the group takes the default ladder (Unity refused the zeros it used to pad with). Without the record (or this package) Unity's own LODGroup stands: one asset per FBX works as before.
  - Test(s): `Features/Lods/Tests/LodsImporterTests.cs` (new: 9, all pass in the owned 6000.3 project); a Maya scene FBX with two groups imported with per-group LODGroups at 0.6/0.3/0 and 0.5/0.25/0 and no warning.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: `unity_package.compile` compiles the package's test assemblies too (`unitytk/unity_compile.py` new `tests` (default on), `TEST_FRAMEWORK`, `NUNIT`, `NETFX_SHIMS`, `_precompiled`; `operations.json`).** Every test assembly's constraints name the Test Framework, which the compile never defined, so all of them were left out: a green compile passed with a test file that did not compile (measured this round). Where the reference project holds the Test Framework, the editor build with the packages now defines `UNITY_INCLUDE_TESTS` and the framework's version define, and compiles the test assemblies against its `UnityEngine.TestRunner`/`UnityEditor.TestRunner`, NUnit from the project's `com.unity.ext.nunit` and the editor's .NET Framework facades NUnit needs. Without the Test Framework in the reference project they are left out and an info issue says so (`unity.compile.no_tests`; with no reference project, `unity.compile.no_packages` already does); `tests=False` is the faster loop. Cost measured on the shipped package: 37 s without the tests, 58 s with them (the variants run in parallel).
  - Test(s): `test_unity_compile.py` new `test_a_broken_test_file_fails_the_compile` (a temp copy whose `Kernel/Tests/ImportsTests.cs` does not compile: 0 errors with the tests left out, as before, 3 with them); `test_the_shipped_package_compiles` now bounds the run at 120 s.
- **2026-11-08 -- Tests: one shared test-support assembly, `M3trik.Unitytk.Testing` (`Kernel/Tests/Support/`, new: `FbxBinary`/`FbxNode`, `TwoTakeFbx`, `HorizonCodes`, `ImportScaleCheck`).** The binary-FBX serializer three test writers each carried (`Carrier/Tests/CarrierFbx.cs`, `Lightmaps/Tests/LightmapsFbx.cs`, `Materials/Tests/DeliveryFbx.cs`), the two-take ASCII delivery two importers' tests each carried (AnimationAudio, Shots), a packed horizon map's codes (`ShadowPlaneSampleTests`, and `test/models_dotnet`, which links the engine-free file) and the import-scale scaffold of the Lightmaps and ShadowPlane sample tests are written once; the feature and kernel test assemblies reference it.
- **2026-11-08 -- Review fixes: one wording for an unbound record name, an unset `GltfFrame` reads as the default, a probe's panorama found on disk by the media rule (`Kernel/Editor/RecordNamesLog.cs`, new; `Core/GltfFrame.cs`; `Kernel/Editor/MediaFiles.cs` new `OnDisk`; the importers of ArticulatedRig, Lightmaps, RenderEffects and ShadowPlane).** `RecordNamesLog.Message`/`Unbound` hold the tie and absent wording every importer said its own way (four phrasings). `default(GltfFrame)` skipped the constructor and landed every length at 0; its fields now read as `GltfFrame.Default`. The Lightmaps probe's source joined each folder to the record's raw name and threw on a null folder; `MediaFiles.OnDisk` reads the name alone, nearest first, before the file imports. `ArticulatedRigImporter`'s remarks point at `Imports.Frame` / `GltfFrame`.
  - Test(s): `GltfFrameTests.A_frame_left_unset_is_the_default_frame` (red: 0 for 1), `MediaFilesTests.A_file_on_disk_is_found_by_its_name_before_it_imports`, `ImportsTests` reading a real model importer, `MaterialsSampleTests` (each platform's override, independently stated; the import's glue for the active target).
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: `unity_project.install(source="git")` refuses a version the remote does not list (`unitytk/unity_host.py` new `_remote_lists`, `_verify_tag`, `TAG_TIMEOUT`; `install`).** It pinned `#v<version>` unchecked, so a version with no tag (a dev build, any before its release: today 0.7.0, the remote listing v0.6.0 alone) wrote a manifest line Unity cannot resolve. `git ls-remote --tags` (never prompting) now asks: a remote that answers without the tag refuses the install, naming the fix (a released unitytk, or `source="local"`); one that cannot be asked (no git, offline, no access, no answer in 20 s) leaves the line written with one warning saying the tag was not verified (`unity.install.unverified_tag` on the context, else the log). `"local"` links this checkout and has no remote to ask.
  - Test(s): `test_unity_host.py` new `test_a_git_install_of_a_tag_the_remote_does_not_list_is_refused`, `test_a_git_install_the_remote_cannot_confirm_proceeds_with_one_warning` (the remote stood in; red first), the git install test given a remote that lists the tag; 18 passed.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: `test_unity_cli.py` gives back the editor listing it fakes (`UnityCliTest.setUp`; new `UnityCliTestIsolationTest`).** Its stand-in's last listing (none) stayed in `UnityCli._editors`, the process-wide cache, so in `run_tests.py --all` every later module read "no Unity editor is installed": 7 errors in `test_unity_compile` and 2 failures in `test_unity_kernel_live`, which passed alone.
  - Test(s): `test_its_tests_leave_the_editor_listing_as_they_found_it` (runs the class, then reads the cache): red, then green; `test_unity_cli` then `test_unity_compile` in one run, 30 passed.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: the Materials import compresses its packed mask for the build target, as its source image is compressed (`Features/Materials/Editor/MaskTextures.cs` new `Compress`, `WithAlpha`; `MaterialsImporter.cs` `MakeMask`, `VERSION` 4).** Measured in an owned project on WebGL (ASTC): the mask sub-asset was DXT5 while every image beside it imported ASTC 6x6, since `Texture2D.Compress` picks the editor's own format and a texture a script makes is no importer's, so no platform setting recompresses it for the build. The mask now takes its source image's format for the import's build target (`context.selectedBuildTarget`, which also reimports the model on a platform switch), in that family's alpha form: ASTC as it is, ETC as ETC2 RGBA8, DXT as DXT5, BC7 as it is; an uncompressed source leaves it uncompressed. A source's own platform override and its compression quality are carried (Unity's 0-100); with no source to follow, the editor's own compression at high quality, as before. A desktop target with Unity's default settings still gets DXT5, now at the source's quality (Normal by default) rather than always high. Android's module is not installed here; WebGL's ASTC subtarget stood in for a mobile GPU.
  - Test(s): `MaterialsSampleTests` new `The_mask_is_compressed_as_the_build_targets_platform_compresses_its_source_image` (Android, WebGL, Standalone); the WebGL measurement before (DXT5) and after.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: the articulated-rig runtime suite's binding verifier calls the importer's `Wire` with its optional last argument (`test/test_articulated_rig_runtime.py`).** `MethodInfo.Invoke` takes every parameter, so the call without the optional one threw `TargetParameterCountException` and failed 09c-09g. With it running again, 09g held the retired rule (a rig whose roots hang under different nodes refused); it now holds articulation v2's (each root's chain poses in its own root's space: the rig imports, nothing reported), as `ArticulatedRigImporterTests` does.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: a render effect goes on the node its curve was written under, not on every node sharing its leaf (`Features/RenderEffects/Editor/RenderEffectsImporter.cs` new `Nodes`, used by `Assemble` and `Rebind`'s presence set; `VERSION` 2).** Measured: with two referenced nodes `a:box` (carrying the curve proxy `box__opacity`) and `b:box` (none), both got a Fade and `b:box` lost Unity's renderer-enabled curves. A track's node is now the parent of each of its curve proxies wherever the file carries one; a track with no proxy anywhere joins its node by the core's one name rule (`RecordNames`: the exact name, then the one node of its leaf), and a tie is left out and said. `Folded` keeps its meaning; a record-side tie (two tracks of one leaf) is still left out. The GLB writer's half of the rule is pythontk's.
  - Test(s): `RenderEffectsImporterTests` new `A_track_goes_on_its_proxys_parent_alone_not_every_node_sharing_its_leaf`, `A_track_with_no_proxy_joins_its_exact_name_else_a_unique_leaf_and_a_tie_is_said` (over `EffectsFbx.WriteSharedLeaves`, new): red with the leaf join (`b:box` wore a Fade, `d:lid` a Highlight), then green.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: the Lightmaps probe and the shadow planes scale with the model importer, through one conversion of glTF's frame (`Core/GltfFrame.cs`, new; `Kernel/Editor/Imports.cs` new `Imports.Frame`; `Features/Lightmaps/Editor/LightmapsImporter.cs` `Assemble`, `Probe`, `Point`; `Features/ShadowPlane/Editor/ShadowPlaneImporter.cs` `Import`, `Wire`, `FbxToUnity`; `Features/ArticulatedRig/Editor/ArticulatedRigImporter.cs` `Wire`, `Fill`, `Point`, `Turn`, `ImportScale` removed; `Features/ArticulatedRig/Model/ArticulationModel.cs` `Mirror`; `Features/Materials/Editor/GltfSurfaces.cs`).** Measured: at Scale Factor 2 the meshes doubled while the probe stayed at (-0.40, 1.00, -0.30) and every plane kept its radius, height and fade; with Convert Units off they were a hundred times too small. `GltfFrame` (engine-free) states the X mirror of a point, a direction and a rotation, the texture transform's UV origin, and what the import makes of a record's lengths: `Units` (a record's lengths in the file's own unit, labelled `unit_scale` metres: the Lightmaps probe, every length of a shadow plane, its `unit`) and `Length` (an in-frame record's: an FBX's own value x 0.01, the articulation record). `Imports.Frame(importer)` reads Convert Units, the file's unit and the Scale Factor into one; with Convert Units off Unity states no file unit (its `fileScale` reads 1, measured), so the record's label stands for it. Every importer reads its record through it; at Unity's defaults nothing moves.
  - Test(s): `LightmapsSampleTests` new `At_scale_factor_2_the_probe_scales_with_the_meshes`, `With_convert_units_off_the_probe_scales_with_the_meshes`; `ShadowPlaneSampleTests` new `At_scale_factor_2_the_planes_scale_with_the_meshes`, `With_convert_units_off_the_planes_scale_with_the_meshes` (the samples' FBX copied in and reimported): red (the probe at x1 for x2 and x100; Awning_Lamp_shadow's radius 0.626 for 1.253 and 62.65), then green; `test/core_dotnet/GltfFrameTests.cs` (new); `ImportsTests.With_no_importer_a_record_lands_in_the_default_frame`; the articulation tests at every unit unchanged.
- **2026-11-08 -- One name rule in the core: `RecordNames` (`Core/RecordNames.cs`, new; `Features/ShadowPlane/Editor/ShadowPlaneNames.cs`, `Features/ArticulatedRig/Editor/ArticulatedRigNames.cs`, `Features/Lightmaps/Editor/Receivers.cs` and their Names tests removed; `LightmapsImporter.Receivers`; the leaf copies in `Contracts/MaterialRecord.cs`, `RenderEffectsImporter`, `MaterialsImporter`, `CarrierFile.IsCarrier`).** Which node a record's name is: the exact name, then its leaf; objects sharing a name told apart by the entry's hierarchy; every overlapping set of entries settled together; a tie never guessed, its candidates named. A port of pythontk's `_Media.resolve_entries`, held to it by its golden cases (`ptk.Conformance.cases("record_names")`, pythontk's new provider). Three importers and four leaf helpers each kept a copy.
  - Test(s): `test/core_dotnet/RecordNamesTests.cs` (new: the golden cases, then the clauses the deleted Names tests held); the importers' tests unchanged; `dotnet test test/core_dotnet` 68 -> 77.
- **2026-11-08 -- The kernel's editor helpers: `OpContextEdits.Edited`, `AssetFolders`, `MediaFiles` (`Kernel/Editor/OpContext.cs`; `Kernel/Editor/AssetFolders.cs`, `MediaFiles.cs`, new; the actions of ArticulatedRig, Lightmaps, RenderEffects, ShadowPlane and Shots; the importers of Lightmaps, Materials and ShadowPlane).** `ctx.Edited(obj)` marks an edited object (a prefab instance's override recorded, its scene marked with no context) in place of six hand-rolled copies. `AssetFolders.SafeName` replaces one fixed set of characters (`\ / : * ? " < > |` and control characters) where `Path.GetInvalidFileNameChars` gave the running OS's, so a name an editor on Linux wrote could be one Windows refuses; `AssetFolders.Ensure` makes a folder and its parents. `MediaFiles.Nearest` finds a record's file in a model's media folders: the union of three drifted copies (one read a record naming a folder for its file name, two refused no folders).
  - Test(s): `Kernel/Tests/AssetFoldersTests.cs`, `MediaFilesTests.cs` (new), `OpContextTests.An_edited_object_is_marked_and_its_scene_saves_it_with_or_without_a_context`.
- **2026-11-08 -- Removed: `RunRules.WriteAtomic`, `UnitytkJson`, `OpenScenes` (`Core/RunRules.cs`, `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs`, `Kernel/Editor/OpenScenes.cs` and its test); `MaterialWriter`'s `HasTexture` and `HasProperty` are one `Has`.** `WriteAtomic` forwarded to `AtomicFile.Write` and `UnitytkJson` to `Json` (`Json.Parse(text, false)`, `Json.Number`); `OpenScenes.Scope`'s callers (Lightmaps' Apply, the RenderEffects action) were reworked earlier in the restructure without it; the preview-scene guard it held is back where scenes are listed (`LightmapsActions.OpenSets`/`OpenScenesList`, `ShadowPlaneActions.OpenPlanes`: no preview scene, so Prefab Mode's sets and planes are left alone; `LightmapsActionsTests.A_set_in_a_preview_scene_such_as_prefab_modes_is_left_alone`). Every caller, the test scripts included, reads the real API.
- **2026-11-08 -- The session server reads pythontk's job-queue layout, generated (`Kernel/Editor/QueueLayout.g.cs`, new, by `m3trik/scripts/sync_job_queue.py`; `Kernel/Editor/SessionServer.cs`).** Its file names and heartbeat states were retyped, so a change to `JobQueue`'s layout kept every gate green while the editor stopped seeing jobs; `check_csharp.py` now holds the generated file. `SceneCapture`'s doc comments name the operation and the framing in the contract's terms, not a producer's (`check_layers.py`'s two frozen rows dropped).
  - Test(s): `m3trik/test/test_sync_job_queue.py` new `test_unitytks_kernel_reads_the_layout_in_its_own_namespace_with_its_meta`, `test_the_session_server_retypes_none_of_the_layout` (red first), 5 passed.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: the shadow plane's fade floors its source ramp at 1e-4, as pythontk's `ShadowProjection.fade` and the viewer do (`Features/ShadowPlane/Model/ShadowPlaneModel.cs` `Fade`).** A ramp of 0 or less divided by zero and drew Inf or NaN.
  - Test(s): pythontk's new conformance case `fade_ramp_floor`: `dotnet test test/models_dotnet` red without the floor, then 31 passed.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fixed (pre-release review of the package's features): a prefab written while a render effect shows holds its materials as authored; the shadow cache's atlas is freed before scripts reload; two render_effects tracks of one leaf are a tie; the Shots importer declares its version; the Render Effects menu passes a model's object over and Prepare Materials saves only what it wrote (`Features/RenderEffects/Editor/RenderEffectsPreview.cs` new `PrefabSaves`, `RenderEffectsImporter.cs` `From`, `Delivery.tiedNodes`, `Delivery.tiedMaterials`, `Assemble`, `RenderEffectsActions.cs` `PrepareMaterials`; `Features/ShadowPlane/Runtime/ShadowPlaneCache.cs` new `Dispose`, `Editor/ShadowPlanePreview.cs` new `BeforeReload`; `Features/Shots/Editor/ShotsImporter.cs` `GetVersion` 1; `Tools/RenderEffects/RenderEffectsMenu.cs` new `PrepareSelected`; `Features/Lightmaps/Editor/LightmapsActions.cs` `Fill`; `Features/Carrier/Editor/CarrierImport.cs` and `Records.cs` gate on `Imports.IsDelivery`; `README.md` Render effects, Articulated rig).** Dragging a showing Fade or Highlight into the Project window (or applying an instance's overrides) wrote its in-memory twin into the prefab, a missing material: the preview hooked only Prefab Mode's save, and now gives every effect back before any prefab write, as ShadowPlane's and ArticulatedRig's do. The cache's `HideAndDontSave` atlas outlived each domain reload in no static, one orphaned per recompile. Two tracks whose names share a leaf (`a:box`, `b:box`) gave the node the last one silently; they are now left out and said, as every reader leaves a tie. A model asset in the menu's selection threw, undoing the rest. `AssetDatabase.SaveAssets` saved every dirty asset; a person's unsaved edit elsewhere is now theirs to save. Lightmaps' scene dirtying skips play mode as its siblings' do. One top-level type per file: `RecordsAudit.cs` is `Records.cs` (its `.meta` kept) and `RecordRefusal.cs`; `Shot` and `ShotField` leave `ShotList.cs` (the component's file and GUID kept) for files of their own; the naming test is its own file. The README calls the widening `SetLimitsFromPose(widen: true)`.
  - Test(s): red first, each for its reason: `RenderEffectsPreviewTests.A_prefab_written_while_an_effect_shows_holds_the_materials_as_authored` (the prefab's material path empty), `ShadowPlaneActionsTests.A_script_reload_frees_the_cache_atlas_and_the_next_caching_plane_makes_one`, `RenderEffectsImporterTests.Two_tracks_whose_nodes_share_a_leaf_are_a_tie_left_out_and_said`, `ShotsImporterTests.The_importer_declares_its_version_so_a_change_to_what_it_writes_reimports_the_models`, `RenderEffectsActionsTests.Prepare_materials_saves_what_it_wrote_and_no_other_asset`, and the new `Tools/RenderEffects/Tests` assembly's `RenderEffectsMenuTests` (ArgumentException); the extraction rules the deleted Python integration suite held, ported to `Features/Carrier/Tests/CarrierImportTests.cs` (new; `CarrierFbx.WriteEmbedding` writes a model embedding its files): a lone model's media land beside it marked, an extraction never writes over a file the model did not extract, a model that comes to share its folder moves its media into `<model>_Media/`, an unmarked file beside an FBX is never bound, a deleted extracted file reimports its model and is asked for again (green at once: the code held them). Edit Mode suites: URP 403/403 twice, Built-in 390 passed and 6 inconclusive (the URP-only tests); `dotnet test` 68 and 31 passed; `check_csharp`, `check_layers`, `sync_unity_samples --check` clean.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: the release receipt runs the Edit Mode tests on URP and Built-in before it posts (`test/run_tests.py` new `main`, `UnityReceipt.PIPELINES`, `UnityReceipt.edit_mode`, `refusal(edit_mode=)`; `test/test_editor_catalog.py` new `needs_dotnet`; `AGENTS.md`).** `--all --strict-env --post-status` ran the Python modules alone, so the `unity` status branch protection reads could be posted with no Edit Mode run; it now runs them on each pipeline's owned project after the suite passes and posts only when every one passed (`--all` alone still starts no editor: CI runs it). `--edit-mode --post-status` is refused instead of ignoring the flag. The core's `dotnet test` gate is an `EnvSkip`, so `--strict-env` fails on a missing SDK. `UNITYTK_MANUAL` leaves `AGENTS.md` (nothing read it).
  - Test(s): `test_run_tests.py` new `test_the_receipt_runs_edit_mode_on_urp_and_builtin_before_it_posts`, `test_a_failed_edit_mode_run_posts_nothing`, `test_a_failed_suite_posts_nothing_and_runs_no_editor`, `test_without_the_receipt_the_full_run_starts_no_editor`, `test_edit_mode_alone_cannot_post_the_receipt` (red: no `main`, Edit Mode never ran); `test_editor_catalog.py` new `DotnetGateTest`; 35 + 6 passed.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: `ProjectBuilder.test` ends a run past its timeout with all it started, and says why; `build` takes turns and removes only what it made (`unitytk/project_builder.py` new `_ProjectBuilderInternal`: `_locked`, `_pending`, `_discard`, `_log_tail`, `LOCK_POLL`, `LOCK_WAIT`, `REMOVE_TRIES`; `_make`, `_safe`, `_digest` moved there).** The editor runs through `AppLauncher.run(hidden=True)`: a job of its own, so the timeout ends the import workers too (a plain run killed the editor alone, and a worker holding the pipe stalled the wait). `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` becomes the documented `RuntimeError`, and both failure messages quote the log's last lines. A failed make deleted whatever was at the folder, a folder it never made included, never checked the removal, and two runs could build one project at once: a build now holds an OS lock beside the project (`<name>.lock`, let go with its process), refuses a folder that holds no project it made, marks a make in progress (`<name>.making`) so a killed run's half-made project is removed by the next build and one that could not be removed is never taken for a project.
  - Test(s): `test_project_builder.py` new `test_a_folder_that_holds_no_project_is_refused_and_left_alone`, `test_a_half_made_project_a_killed_build_left_is_made_again`, `test_a_leftover_that_could_not_be_removed_is_never_taken_for_a_project`, `test_two_builds_of_one_project_take_turns`, `test_a_run_past_its_timeout_ends_the_editors_tree_and_says_so`, `test_a_run_that_wrote_no_results_says_why_with_the_logs_end` (6 red first), `test_a_failed_make_takes_away_only_what_it_made`, `test_the_filter_is_unitys_test_filter`, `test_a_project_an_editor_has_open_is_never_tested`; 28 passed. Live: `run_tests.py --edit-mode -k Kernel` in the hidden batch editor, 64 of 64 passed, no editor left running.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: an interrupt while Unity's CLI runs stops it and the editor it started (`unitytk/unity_cli.py` `_UnityCliInternal._answer`).** Only the cancel and the timeout reached the stop; a Ctrl-C lands in `communicate`, where the wait is spent, and left both running. The module docstring drops the vendored-copy guard retired 2026-11-05.
  - Test(s): `test_unity_cli.py` new `test_an_interrupt_while_it_waits_stops_the_command_and_propagates` (red first); 16 passed.
- **2026-11-08 -- `UnityCompile`'s default reference project is the compile's own editor's (`unitytk/unity_compile.py` `_dev_project(version)`; `_build` moved to `_UnityCompileInternal`).** It read the newest editor's owned project whatever editor compiled, so the package half could build against another version's ScriptAssemblies.
  - Test(s): `test_unity_compile.py` new `test_the_default_reference_project_is_the_compiles_own_editors` (red first); 13 passed.
- **2026-11-08 -- Tests: the core's `AtomicFile` sweep and `UnitytkRecords` reader under `dotnet test` (`test/core_dotnet/AtomicFileTests.cs`, `UnitytkRecordsTests.cs`, new); the versions held equal (`test/test_package.py`); dead harness code removed (`test/base_test.py`, `test/test_base_test.py`); the lint gate covers the repo (`.github/workflows/tests.yml`).** `AtomicFile.Sweep` deletes a `.part` older than `StaleAfter` (one hour) and keeps a fresh one and another file's; `UnitytkRecords.Read` refuses non-JSON, another version and a failed shape whole, `VersionOf` and `TryGetProperty` as documented. `package.json`'s version must equal `unitytk.__version__`. `UnityEnv.create_project` skips (env) for a named editor the CLI does not list, which it made with the CLI's default editor and then ran in another (`UNLISTED_EDITOR`). Gone, with their tests, as nothing called them after the restructure: `UnityEnv.make_urp`, `URP_SETUP_CS`, `bundled_urp`, `NO_BUNDLED_URP`, `UserPropsProbe` (a drifted copy of the carrier rule), `SuiteOptions.MANUAL`/`manual`/`enabled`, `UnityTkTestCase.create_dummy_unity_project`. `ruff check .` is clean, so CI gates it in place of the package alone.
  - Test(s): `dotnet test test/core_dotnet` 40 -> 68 passed; `test_package.py` new `test_the_unity_package_and_the_python_package_carry_one_version`; `test_base_test.py` new `test_a_named_editor_the_cli_does_not_list_makes_no_project` (red first), 31 passed; `ruff check .` clean.
- **2026-11-08 -- The scene's carrier is the root-level `data_export`, on both of the package's readers (`Features/Carrier/Editor/CarrierFile.cs` new `CarrierFile.IsSceneCarrier`, `CarrierFile.AtRoot`; `Read` reads the Connections section after the Objects; `IsCarrier` takes Blender's `data_export.001` as pythontk's `is_carrier` does; `CarrierImport.Collect`; `README.md`).** Both readers took the first carrier named exactly `data_export` as the scene's own, so an assembly whose grouped module's `Module|data_export` came first in the file read that module's stale record -- measured 2026-11-08 on the workspace's Maya assembly twins (the Shots twin failed `Each_shot_entry_references_its_clip_with_its_description_and_section`). The file reader places each carrier by its `OO` link (wired under another Model: a module's); `Collect`, whose nodes Unity hands over before it parents the hierarchy, takes an own-named node as the scene's only where the file's root carrier holds the value too. pythontk `SceneStoreBase.is_scene_carrier` states the rule.
  - Test(s): red with each rule broken, then green: `Features/Carrier/Tests/RecordsTests.cs` new `A_carrier_under_a_group_is_its_modules_never_the_scenes` (GameObjects parented and as an import hands them over, and a file `CarrierFbx` (new) writes), `A_node_that_is_no_carrier_is_never_read`.

- **2026-11-08 -- `ProjectBuilder` builds a project of its own beside the owned one, and a sample's files can come from another delivery (`unitytk/project_builder.py` `folder(key=)`, `build(key=, replacements=)`, `import_samples(replacements=)`, new `NAME`, `KEY`).** A `key` (a short plain name) builds and refreshes `<NAME>-<key>` beside the owned project, so a run that must not share it -- the workspace's end-to-end suite, while the package's suites run -- has its own. `replacements` (`{sample: folder}`) delivers the folder's files in place of the sample's own of the same path, so the sample's tests check another producer's delivery of it; the stamp holds the files' digest, so a build without it imports the sample's own again, and a replacement naming no sample, or a file the sample does not hold, is refused before anything is copied.

- **2026-11-08 -- ArticulatedRig: a record lands as Unity's import frames the file, in every unit (`Features/ArticulatedRig/Editor/ArticulatedRigImporter.cs` new `ImportScale`, `Wire(unit=)`, `Fill(unit=)`, `VERSION` 3; `Model/ArticulationModel.cs` `IsIdentity`; regenerated `Model/ArticulationRecord.g.cs`).** The record states a length as glTF frames the file (the FBX's own value x 0.01, whatever its UnitScaleFactor), and Unity's FBX import converts the file's unit into every local (its `fileScale` with Convert Units on, times `globalScale`) -- measured on deliveries in cm, m and mm and at global scales 1 and 2: a joint whose file value is 24 imports at 0.24 from a centimetre file and at 24 from a metre one. So the importer scales every length it fills -- rest, anchor point, a slide's limits -- by that conversion x 100, read off the importer (1 for a centimetre file at the defaults): before, a metre file's rig snapped every joint to a hundredth of its place. Every model reimports (`VERSION` 3). `ArticulationModel.IsIdentity` reads the identity as the reference does: `t` exactly 0 and `q` the identity rotation either sign.

- **2026-11-08 -- ArticulatedRig fix pass (`Features/ArticulatedRig/`: `Runtime/ArticulatedRig.cs`, `Runtime/ArticulatedJoint.cs`, `Runtime/ArticulatedRigPointerGrab.cs`, `Model/ArticulationModel.cs`, `Editor/ArticulatedRigPreview.cs`, `Editor/ArticulatedRigActions.cs`, `Editor/ArticulatedRigImporter.cs`, `Editor/ArticulatedJointEditor.cs`, `Editor/ArticulatedRigEditor.cs`, `Editor/ArticulatedRigNames.cs`, `XRI/ArticulatedRigXRGrab.cs`, `XRI/ArticulatedRigXRSetup.cs`, `XRI/Editor/ArticulatedRigXRDoor.cs`; `Tools/ArticulatedRig/ArticulatedRigMenu.cs`; `README.md` Articulated rigs).** The edit-mode preview no longer takes a part's Transform: it holds and poses only the parts a value moves off their rest, yields a posed part the person moves (the move stays) until a value above it moves again, leaves a rig that follows its animation (and any part the Animation window animates) alone, counts each rig's open inspectors (the rig-to-joint selection hop, measured: Unity disables the old inspector, then enables the new one), and after a scene save or a prefab write poses again while an inspector is open. New action `ArticulatedRigActions.CaptureRest` (menu and joint inspector: Capture Rest From Transform): the rest that poses the part where it stands at its values, one undo step. Each root joint's chain now poses and grabs in its own space, the node its part hangs under (`ArticulatedRig.SpaceOf`, new), as every reader of the record does: the importer no longer refuses roots under different nodes (the rig goes on the nearest node above them all) nor two rigs on one node, so `ArticulatedRig` and `ArticulatedRigXRSetup` drop `[DisallowMultipleComponent]`; an off-chain part is placed in its parent joint part's frame (that part's own scale left out). `AddJoint` refuses a part another rig lists, and a root's rest is its local under its space. `ArticulationModel.Solve` has an overload solving into the caller's state on the model's own scratch (a warmed solve, pose and read allocate nothing; the rig's grab uses it), and the mirror is one table (`ArticulationModel.Mirror`, `MirrorFlips`, `MirrorLimits`, new) the importer and the tests share. The XR grab's `IsHolding` no longer counts a grab of a part above its own, so a hand that lost the rig steers and releases nothing. Set Limits From Pose's confirm lists the dry run's own narrowing changes (one predicate; its changes name `narrow` or `widen`). Slide values are labelled in the rig's units (metres for an unscaled space). Removed (no caller): `ArticulationModel.Joint.Slides`, `ArticulatedJoint.ChannelOf`, `ArticulatedRigImporter.Mirrored`, `ArticulatedRigActions.Narrowings`. Test(s): `ArticulatedRigPreviewTests` (a moved part keeps its move, a part at rest is never held, a yield ends when a value moves, shown again after a save, any open inspector shows it, a following rig left to the Animation window), `ArticulatedRigImporterTests` (the mirror pinned against the reference model over ry, tx, rx, rz, ty, one-sided limits, a turned rest and an anchor; split roots import, pose and grab; two rigs on one node), `ArticulatedRigTests` (split roots, a scaled rig, a flat model read while following, `resumeOnAnimatorChange`), `ArticulatedRigActionsTests` (`AddJoint`'s refusals, `CreateRig`'s undo, `CaptureRest`), `ArticulatedRigNamesTests` (new), `ArticulatedRigPointerGrabTests` (drag, scroll, `PlanePoint`), `ArticulatedRigXRTests` (only the holder steers); models_dotnet `ArticulationModelTests` (a warmed solve allocates nothing; the mirror table is the stress script's).

- **2026-11-08 -- ArticulatedRig on articulation v2, in Unity's terms: a joint component on each part, a rig that poses every part from the joint chain, actions, a menu, an inspector with handles and an edit-mode preview (`Features/ArticulatedRig/`: `Model/ArticulationModel.cs` (namespace `M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig`, the anchor term, `ScaleOf` removed); `Runtime/ArticulatedRigController.cs` renamed `Runtime/ArticulatedRig.cs` with its `.meta`, new `Runtime/ArticulatedJoint.cs`, `Runtime/ArticulationBoundary.cs` removed, `Runtime/ArticulatedRigPointerGrab.cs` (namespaced); `Editor/ArticulatedRigImporter.cs` rewritten, new `Editor/ArticulatedRigActions.cs`, `ArticulatedRigNames.cs`, `ArticulatedRigPreview.cs`, `ArticulatedRigEditor.cs`, `ArticulatedJointEditor.cs`, `IArticulatedRigXRDoor.cs`; `XRI/ArticulatedRigXRGrab.cs`, `ArticulatedRigXRSetup.cs` (namespace `M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig.XRI`), new `XRI/Editor/` (`M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig.XRI.Editor`: `ArticulatedRigXRDoor.cs`) and `XRI/Tests/`; `Tests/*` rewritten, `ArticulatedRigControllerTests.cs` renamed `ArticulatedRigTests.cs` with its `.meta`; new `Tools/ArticulatedRig/` (`M3trik.Unitytk.Tools.ArticulatedRig`: `ArticulatedRigMenu.cs`, and its tests); `test/models_dotnet/ArticulationModelTests.cs`; `test/test_articulated_rig_runtime.py` ported; `README.md` the `articulation` row, the component and importer row, Articulated rigs, Troubleshooting).** The record is articulation v2 (glTF's frame: metres, `(x, y, z, w)`, +Y up, an `anchor` on every joint); a v1 record is refused with its fix (re-export). The C# model ports the anchor term -- a part turns as `rest . anchor . pose(channels) . anchor^-1` -- into `Local`, `Read` and `Solve` (the turn's pivot, the wrist's centre and the reach measured pivot to pivot, as the reference does); an identity anchor keeps the old path; it gains allocation-free `Pose(state, into)`, `World(state, into)`, `Read(locals, hint, into)`, and `Joint.anchorT` / `anchorQ` / `Anchored`, `ChannelCode`, `IsIdentity`. Lengths are metres, so `ScaleOf` goes: Unity's FBX import already frames a file in metres (measured, below). The model is the same in any axes a mirror relates (pinned: a rig stated with X mirrored poses, reads and solves as the reference's mirror, ry, rz and tx negated), so the components hold Unity's own values and nothing converts at run time: `ArticulationBoundary` goes. `ArticulatedJoint` (on each part) holds its parent joint by reference, its rest in that joint's frame, its anchor (point and orientation in the rest frame; the pivot by default), its rotate order, its ordered channels (`Axis`, `hasMin`/`min`, `hasMax`/`max`, `weight`), the values `rx` ... `tz` as plain fields the Animation window keys, and `changed` (a `UnityEvent<Axis, float>` fired as a value moves). `ArticulatedRig` (on the rig's root; replaces `ArticulatedRigController`, whose record string goes: the runtime never reads the record) lists its joints and grab parts (`GrabPart`) by reference and poses every part each LateUpdate from the joint chain -- its parent joint's pose, never its scene parent's, so a flat model chains -- writing a part on the chain as its local transform and one off it (a flat model's) in the rig's space. Animation and posing: `followAnimation` (the importer's setting) reads the values off the parts the delivery's clips move; off, the values pose the parts (a Unity-made rig's clips key them); a pose -- a grab, `ApplyState`, a value a script sets over a followed clip -- holds over either until `ReturnToAnimation()` (or `resumeOnAnimatorChange`). The grab API stays (`BeginGrab`, `UpdateGrab`, `EndGrab`, `grabRotation`, `GrabPointWorld`, `FindGrab`, `EnsureGrabColliders`, `AddGrabColliders`), now `GrabbedPart` and `GrabbedJoint` (the joint), with `GetState` / `ReadState` / `ApplyState`, `ApplyNow`, `ReadPose`, `Rebuild`, `IsValid` / `Problem` and `TryGetRestFrame`. The importer fills the components by reference: joints settled together by name (exact, else the namespace-stripped leaf; a tie stays unbound and is reported, leaving its rig out), grab parts under their joint, the rig on the node its roots hang under (roots under different nodes refused and reported), every rest and anchor mirrored as Unity's import lands the file and the flipped channels' limits negated and swapped, the values where the file stands, grab colliders; stale components go; import version 2. `ArticulatedRigActions`: `CreateRig(root)`, `AddJoint(part, parentJoint, channels)` (at rest where the part stands, the anchor at its pivot), `SetLimitsFromPose(joint, widen)` (each value away from rest becomes its side's limit, or only widens one), `MakeXRGrabbable(rig)` (the XRI door's editor half, registered as the editor loads, adds `ArticulatedRigXRSetup`; without XRI it says so), each with an optional `OpContext`. Tools ▸ unitytk ▸ Articulated Rig: Create Rig, Add Hinge / Slide / Ball Joint, Set Limits From Pose (planned first; it asks before it narrows a limit), Widen Limits To Pose, Make XR Grabbable. The joint's inspector draws handles for the anchor (position, rotation) and the limits (an arc per turn, a span per slide); the rig's or a joint's inspector shows the rig posed from its values through `ArticulatedRigPreview`, which gives every part's transform back on close, save, scene close, prefab write, reload and Play. Found while testing: `AddJoint`'s listing on the rig did not undo -- Unity's record of a new component closes a record of the rig taken before it -- so the rig is asked after the component is made. ArticulationBody is prior art only, a later door (physics-driven articulations). Removed public names, with no moved-names row (no project uses unitytk): `ArticulatedRigController` and every member, `ArticulationBoundary`, `ArticulationModel.ScaleOf`; moved into namespaces: `ArticulationModel` (`M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig`), `ArticulatedRigPointerGrab` (same), `ArticulatedRigXRGrab` / `ArticulatedRigXRSetup` (`M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig.XRI`, field `controller` renamed `rig`, `Controller` renamed `Rig`).
  - Test(s): red first: `dotnet test` (models_dotnet) failed 4 of 16 on the anchored cases before the port (case 32, anchored, read off by 29.8); after it 22 of 22, the new ones -- `An_anchored_rig_poses_reads_and_solves_as_the_reference` (anchored, flat, anchored_tree), `The_flat_rig_chains_by_its_parents_with_every_rest_the_roots`, `An_identity_anchor_is_the_anchorless_path`, `A_rig_stated_in_unitys_axes_is_the_mirror_of_the_reference`, `A_joint_without_an_anchor_is_a_version_1_rigs_and_is_refused` -- each failing under a mutant of the port (the anchor dropped: 7 failed; the column's pivot, the bone's parent anchor, the wrist's centre: 5, 5, 4; the refusal's fix: 1). - **2026-11-08 -- The ShadowPlane fix pass (`Features/ShadowPlane/Editor/ShadowPlaneActions.cs`, `ShadowPlanePreview.cs`, `ShadowPlaneImporter.cs`, `Runtime/ShadowPlane.cs`, `Runtime/ShadowPlane.shader`, `Tools/ShadowPlane/ShadowPlaneMenu.cs`, their tests; `README.md` Shadows, Troubleshooting).** A placed model's planes stay current wherever it stands: the geometry signature on a plane (`horizonHash`) is no longer a millimetre-rounded digest of world-space float points, which a move of the instance flipped (measured: a 20,000-point mesh moved to (-410, 3, 978) and turned read stale, so Rebake Stale replaced a delivered map), but an exact digest of the meshes' triangles and counts (a mesh asset's own points too) plus the ground and each mesh's box, mean and placement in the plane's frame, carried in double through the local transforms below the contact's and the mesh's common ancestor and compared within 1e-4 of the target's extent; the ground is in it now (a moved ground is a stale map), and its size and spans are the ones the baker gave (asked for 100, it bakes and stamps 128, so it no longer rebakes every time). A rebake never writes over a map another plane in the open scenes holds (a duplicate's): it writes a new file, which the plan says and the menu's ask names. The meshes a shadow is of are those that draw: a `SkinnedMeshRenderer`'s in its current pose (`BakeMesh`; a rigged target cast nothing before) besides each `MeshRenderer`'s, LOD 0's alone of a `LODGroup`; a mesh on an inactive object, with no renderer or a disabled one, or in a later LOD is left out and named (`unity.shadow_plane.skipped`, info), and the importer takes a skinned mesh as an occluder too. The inspector's preview holds a plane's transform only while it places it, so with Follow Source off a move stays (it snapped back on deselect, unrecorded), and gives every plane back before a prefab is written (a prefab made from the scene object with the inspector open saved the preview's placement: measured). A plane's contact frame is read Z-up from an explicit `zUpFrames`, which the importer sets from the contact's own frame, never from the quad's mesh (a hand-added plane on Unity's Quad got a vertical footprint); how the quad lies is still its mesh's. The material's fade is 0 until a plane writes its own (measured: an unplaced plane marched the first plane's map in a frame at the origin, at full opacity). A rebake over the same file re-marches the cache (measured: the reimported map is the same `Texture2D`, so the cache kept the old march); every open plane bound to a reimported texture forgets its cached march. Rebake Stale reports progress per plane, Create per step, and the menu's Create and Bake per target, so the bar's Cancel stops them. A fallback plane (the pipeline's unlit shader) draws at the record's intensity, a constant alpha: no per-frame fade reaches it, and Built-in's `Unlit/Transparent` has no colour to carry one (HDRP keeps Unity's material, unfaded). The importer's version is 1, so every model reimports once. Measured and kept: `RenderSettings.sun` with Sun Source None is the scene's brightest enabled directional light (6000.3), so `Create` and the default source need no fallback of their own. Left: `EnsureFolder` / `Safe` stay in both features' actions, the only home both reference being the foundation.

New actions (`ShadowPlaneActions`, each with an optional `OpContext`): `Create` (a plane, its contact and its baked map under a target; the planes' shared material beside the scene), `BakeHorizon` (the target's meshes in the plane's frame through `ShadowHorizonBaker.Bake`, the rows reversed into a PNG beside the scene, `<scene>_ShadowPlanes/<plane>_horizon.png`, asked as a write and imported as data, the target's geometry hash stored in `horizonHash`), `RebakeStale` (every plane whose target's hash changed); the importer stamps the hash of the delivered geometry. The menu (Tools ▸ unitytk ▸ Shadow Plane: Create Shadow Plane, Bake Horizon, Rebake Stale Planes) plans first and asks before it writes over a map on disk. The inspector shows the plane placed in the Scene view while it is open, given back as it closes and before a save (`ShadowPlanePreview`), with handles for its radius and ground. `test/test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration.py` ported to the v3 component (the projected, silhouette and by-name cases deleted; the fade added). - **2026-11-07 -- A namespaced material is the one its record names, whatever Unity's import renames it (`Contracts/MaterialRecord.cs` `Find` generic, new `ImportedName`; `Features/RenderEffects/Editor/RenderEffectsImporter.cs` new `Worn`, its editor assembly referencing `M3trik.Unitytk.Contracts`).** Measured (6000.3.10f1, Windows): Unity's model import names a material the file calls `ns:Glow_mat` `ns_Glow_mat` -- each of `\ / : * ? < > |` as `_`, in the material's description too -- while a node keeps every character. The `materials` record names it `ns:Glow_mat`, as the file does, so `Find` matched it neither by name nor by the leaf after the last `:`: every material of a referenced asset kept Unity's own import, and a namespaced emissive group's Emission Track was never made (its weight curve rebound onto nothing). `Find` now joins by name, then by both names as Unity's import writes them (`ImportedName`), then by the leaf (an import that drops the namespace); each rule on both sides, a tie joining neither. It is generic over the dictionary's values, so the RenderEffects importer joins a renderer's material to a `render_effects` entry by the same rules (`Worn`, one lookup for the rebind and the assembly, where each had keyed the material's leaf). A foundation change: `MaterialRecord.Find`'s signature (callers unchanged) and one new public name, `MaterialRecord.ImportedName`.
  - Test(s): `MaterialsImporterTests.A_namespaced_material_is_written_from_its_entry_whatever_unitys_import_renames_it` (red first: emission 0 for 1.878) and `RenderEffectsImporterTests.A_namespaced_delivery_joins_each_target_to_its_curve_by_its_leaf` (red first: no track under the carrier; `EffectsFbx.Write` gains a namespace prefix, `NamespacedRecord` new); `UnityCompile.compile()` 0 errors in all four builds.

The prior art of `Highlight`, `EmissionTrack` and `RenderEffectsActions` cites the open standard (glTF `KHR_animation_pointer`, `KHR_materials_emissive_strength`) and Unity's Animation window, not a producer or the viewer. `README.md`: the Edit Mode behaviour, the folded node, the prefab-asset refusal; the record row, the Emission Track row and the sample row in delivery terms; the troubleshooting rows (the opacity row in delivery terms; the old controller's `colorProperty` row replaced by the Edit Mode one). `package.json`'s description lists render effects (fades, highlights, visibility, animated emission) and materials, no longer opacity fades and emissive groups. No public name added, renamed or removed; no serialized field added.

occlusion R when packed with them), `KHR_materials_emissive_strength`, `KHR_materials_unlit`, `KHR_texture_transform`; a v1 record is refused with its fix (re-export). **The one material writer** (a foundation change, approved): `MaterialWriter.Write(material, LitSurface, host)` puts a glTF material on the pipeline's own Lit (its Unlit for an unlit one) through the host's row of a declared table, `LitTable` (`BuiltIn`: `Standard` and the four `Unlit/*`; - **2026-11-05 -- Animation Audio: whoever owns the animation owns its cues (Rev G phase 4.2; `Features/AnimationAudio/` from `Features/AudioEvent/`: `Runtime/AnimationAudioPlayer.cs` from `AudioEventController.cs`, `Editor/AnimationAudioImporter.cs` from `AudioEventImporter.cs`, `Editor/AnimationAudioActions.cs` from `AudioEvents.cs`, each with its `.meta`; `Tools/AudioEvent/` removed; `Core/PersistedStrings.cs`, `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs`; `Samples~/AnimationAudio/` new, `package.json`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`; `test/test_standalone_enduser.py`; `test/test_multi_section_audio_integration.py` removed).** The `audio` record is v3 (`{version: 3, cues: [{clip, sound, time, offset, stop}], media?}`: per clip, in seconds from its start, the sound by the delivered file's name, its offset into the sound and its stop), regenerated `AudioRecord.g.cs`; v1 and v2 are refused with the re-export fix.
  - **The importer** (`M3trik.Unitytk.AnimationAudio.AnimationAudioImporter`) writes each cue into the clip the model importer cuts from its take as two plain `AnimationEvent`s: `PlayAudio` at its time (object: the sound's `AudioClip`; float: the offset into the sound) and `StopAudio` at its stop (the same clip). It writes them into the imported clip object (`OnPostprocessAnimation`, `AnimationUtility.SetAnimationEvents`), never into the model importer's clip list (the `.meta`): Unity's own rule for an imported asset, so the Events tab stays the project's, for its own additions, and every import writes the delivered cues afresh. Events-tab events arrive on the clip first and stay beside the delivered ones. Cues join clips by take name, as `ShotsImporter` joins shots: a renamed clip plays its take's cues, and a take split in the Animation tab gives each part the cues inside it, re-timed from its start (one begun before a part starts at its start, that much further into the sound; one running past a part's end stops there); a shotless file's cues (`clip: ""`) play in its one take. The sound resolves by its file name and the record's `media` digest (the FBX's embedded copy, extracted beside the model once, or a same-named library clip whose bytes hash alike, never another model's extraction); a cue whose sound is not found yet is left out and named. A model whose clips cue sounds (delivered or Events tab) gets an `AnimationAudioPlayer` on its root, its `AudioSource` 3D. `GetVersion()` (2): a changed rule reimports the models it wrote.
  - **The player** (`AnimationAudioPlayer`; Add Component ▸ Audio ▸ Animation Audio Player) plays delivered and Unity-authored events alike: `PlayAudio(AnimationEvent)` starts the event's object (an `AudioClip` from its float's seconds in; any other `AudioResource`, an Audio Random Container, from its start), each sound on a source of its own copying the player's own `AudioSource` (which plays nothing: it holds the settings), so sounds overlap and each stops alone; `StopAudio(AnimationEvent)` stops it where the clip firing it started it (a cut may deliver the next clip's start before the last one's stop). A sound also stops when the clip that started it stops playing, since an interrupted clip fires no more events: once a frame the player asks the Animator's states (current and next, every layer) whether that clip still plays, or a legacy Animation whether its state is enabled. Justified in its prior art: the Animator's state is what Unity says plays; a Playables graph (Timeline, an Animator with no controller) shows no clips there, so a sound it started plays to its stop event or its end.
  - **The actions** (`AnimationAudioActions`): `AddCue(clip, sound, time, stop?, offset, ctx)` cues a sound in a clip with no DCC behind it -- a model's clip in its Events tab (normalized times; a file write, then a reimport), a clip of the project's own in the clip (one undo step) -- and plans nothing when the clip cues it there already; `AddPlayer(target, ctx)` gives an object a player.
  - **Removed** (no moved-names rows: no project uses unitytk): `AudioEventController` (with `clips`, `OnAudioEvent(string)`, its by-name lookup), `AudioEventImporter` (with `Place`, `ParseManifest`, `ManifestEntry`), `AudioEvents` (`FixSceneObjects`, `ReimportModels`, `ReimportToBindCode`), the menu Tools ▸ unitytk ▸ Audio Events, `PersistedStrings.AudioEventFunction` (now `PlayAudioFunction` / `StopAudioFunction`), the assemblies `M3trik.Unitytk.AudioEvent[.Editor/.Tests]` and `M3trik.Unitytk.Tools.AudioEvent`. The two integration suites over the retired per-object audio fixture lose their audio: `test_multi_section_audio_integration` goes (its premise was the retired pipeline), and `test_standalone_enduser` keeps the fixture's opacity fades.
  - **The sample.** `Samples~/AnimationAudio/AnimationAudio.fbx` (70 KB; the workspace's `sync_unity_samples.py`, recipe `unity_samples/animation_audio.py`): a box over two shots, Approach (1-24) and Exit (25-48) at 24 fps, and a synthetic half-second chime keyed on frame 18, across the cut, embedded in the FBX. Its demo recipe `Editor/AnimationAudioDemoRecipe.Build(folder)` (Tools ▸ unitytk ▸ Samples ▸ Animation Audio Demo) builds `AnimationAudioDemo.controller` (the two clips in turn; the model's Rig set to Generic first, as a controller asks) and `AnimationAudioDemo.unity`. The samples' `.meta` files state the ModelImporter's version (24200): a bare `.meta` imported as a pre-version file, a Legacy rig and External materials (measured, 6000.3).
  - Test(s): `AnimationAudioImporterTests` (10; a real import of a two-take ASCII FBX 7.4 with a `data_export` audio record: each cue in its take's clip at its seconds with its AudioClip, a sound across the cut stopped at it and played on from its offset, one trimmed at the head; through a reimport, never in the Events tab; an Events-tab event kept beside the delivered; a renamed clip; a split clip re-timed; a v2 record refused; a missing sound named; and the placement over Unity's clip and take types), `AnimationAudioPlayerTests` (7: the offset and the stop event, overlapping sounds, an offset past the end, the function names, a sound stopped when its clip's Animator state leaves, a stop scoped to its clip, an Animator's own events reaching the player), `AnimationAudioActionsTests` (6: the three kernel checks for both actions, refusals, a model's Events tab), `AnimationAudioSampleTests` (4, over the imported sample). Red first: with the importer's write of the events removed and the player's clip watch answering "still plays", 11 of 27 failed (`Values differ at index [0]`, `actual is ...[0]`; `A_sound_stops_when_the_clip_that_started_it_stops_playing`); Edit Mode in the owned URP project: Edit Mode 175: 174 passed, 0 failed, 1 inconclusive (the Built-in-only Lightmaps case in the URP project).

A shotless animated scene's record (`shots: []`) imports no shots.

- **2026-11-05 -- Shots: a shot is a clip, and its data rides with it (Rev G phase 4.1; `Features/Shots/` from `Features/ShotMetadata/`, `Runtime/ShotList.cs` new from `ShotMetadataController.cs`, `Editor/ShotsImporter.cs` from `ShotMetadataImporter.cs`, `Editor/ShotsActions.cs` new; `Contracts/ISequenceWindow.cs` removed; `Features/AudioEvent/Editor/AudioEventImporter.cs`, `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs`, `Core/ImportOrder.cs`, `README.md`; `docs/parts/contract.md`).** The source writes one FBX take per shot, named after it and spanning its range, and the `animation` record (v2) keeps per shot only what a clip cannot hold: its description and metadata. So the feature holds no frames, windows or player.
  - **The component.** `M3trik.Unitytk.Shots.ShotList` (assemblies `M3trik.Unitytk.Shots`, `.Editor`, `.Tests`; Add Component ▸ unitytk ▸ Shots) holds `entries`. It is not named `Shots`, like its namespace: inside any `M3trik.Unitytk.*` namespace that name resolves to the namespace (CS0118). Each `Shot` has a clip REFERENCE, a description and its metadata, one `ShotField` per key with the value as JSON text, so any JSON value keeps its type. That form is weighed in the component's prior art: Unity serializes neither a Dictionary nor an arbitrary JSON value, and `[SerializeReference]` is barred. `Entry(clip)` gives a clip's shot (null when none) and `Shot.TryGetMetadata(key, out json)` a value. An Animator, an Animation or a Timeline plays the clips.
  - **The importer** reads the v2 record and rewrites the model's `ShotList` at every import, so the delivered entries are the source's. A v1 record is refused with its fix (re-export), and the model is left with no stale component. Each shot joins every clip cut from its take (`ModelImporterClipAnimation.takeName`, ignoring case), never a clip's name. A clip renamed in the Animation tab still plays its shot, and a take split there gives each part the shot's entry. A shot whose take the file lacks is warned (re-export), and so is one no clip is cut from (add one in the Animation tab). A model that imports no animation says so once.
  - **The action.** `ShotsActions.AddEntry(target, clip, description, ctx)` gives a clip a shot in a project with no DCC behind it. It adds a `ShotList` when the target has none. A clip that already has an entry is left alone. An imported model, an instance of one and a prefab whose list a model delivers are refused, the fix in the message (add a `ShotList` to an object of your own: the delivered entries are the source's): an entry added on an instance is a prefab override Unity matches by list position, so the next import that changes the delivered list would misalign it.
  - **Audio events per clip.** `ISequenceWindow` / `PureSequenceWindow` retire, since there is no whole-timeline clip. With them go AudioEvent's sequence-clip placement and both importers' import ranks (no contract declares `[ImportRank]` now). A clip plays the events of the take it is cut from: those the `audio` record scopes to that take, and every unscoped one. Each lands at the take's start plus `frame / fps` (the record's clock), less where the clip starts in Unity's own take info: `importedTakeInfos`' start, and the clip's first frame at the take's sample rate (`AudioEventImporter.Place`). So a renamed, trimmed or split clip keeps its events at their moments, and an event more than half a frame outside a clip is left out.
  - **Removed** (no moved-names rows, Rev G section 8: no project uses unitytk): `ShotMetadataController` (with `GetShot`, `TryGetSequenceWindow`, `fps`, `clipMode`, `sequenceClip`, `sequenceFirstFrame`), `ShotRecord`, `ShotMetadataImporter`, `ISequenceWindow`, `PureSequenceWindow`, the assemblies `M3trik.Unitytk.ShotMetadata[.Editor/.Tests]`.
  - Test(s): `ShotListTests` (3: the lookup by clip, not by name; a destroyed clip; metadata by exact key) and `TheComponentIsNamedPlainlyTests` (a reader in another `M3trik.Unitytk.*` namespace names `ShotList`; red first as `Shots`: CS0118), `ShotsActionsTests` (6: one Ctrl+Z, the plan equals the run, a second run plans nothing, no context, refusals), `ShotsImporterTests` (8: `An_imported_model_and_its_instance_are_refused_an_entry`, red first: the instance took the entry; the join over Unity's clip and take types, renamed and split; no take vs no clip; and a real import of a two-take ASCII FBX written by the test. That import shows the component on the model references the model's own clip sub-assets through a reimport, the take keeps the shot's place on the timeline, its clip starts there, a renamed clip still joins, a missing take is warned, a model importing no animation says so, and a v1 record is refused leaving no shots), new `AudioEventImporterTests` (6: a shot's events on its own clip on the record's clock, renamed, split, unscoped, outside the clip, unlisted clip). `AudioEventsTests` loses its two sequence-window tests, `ImportOrderTests` no longer needs a ranked contract, and `MayaSurfaceMaterialImporterTests` holds the rewriter past the highest ranked order. The package's 154 Edit Mode tests: 153 pass and 1 is inconclusive (the Built-in-only lightmap test, in the URP project). `test/core_dotnet` (40) and `test/models_dotnet` (9) pass, and so does `python -m pytest test` (233 passed, 11 skipped by environment). `UnityCompile.compile()` has 0 errors, and `check_layers.py`, `check_csharp.py` and `sync_scene_records.py --check` are clean.
- **2026-11-04 -- Fixed (review of the restructure): one project layer, a licence wall read only from its own signs, temporary projects leave the Hub, Unity's CLI is robust and cancellable, and the tests name no producer (`unitytk/unity_project.py` new; `unity_cli.py`, `unity_host.py`, `project_builder.py`, `unity_compile.py`; `test/`).**
  - **One project layer.** `UnityProject` holds what a Unity project folder is (the package line, the manifest read and written, the editor that holds it and the refusal to write one it holds, the URP path-depth guard, a failed run's diagnosis); `UnityHost` and `ProjectBuilder` both extend it and neither imports the other.
  - **The licence wall.** `diagnose` reads compile errors first and calls a run unlicensed only on exit code 198 or Unity's own licence messages: every editor log has `[Licensing::` lines, so a crash or a compile error read as no licence and a suite skipped instead of failing.
  - **The Hub.** `UnityCli.projects` and `unregister`: a released test project leaves the Hub's list (`TempProjects.forget`), as does a build cut short; the owned projects stay listed.
  - **Unity's CLI.** Each command runs with no stdin and no console window, a timeout raises `RuntimeError`, a `cancel` callable stops the CLI and what it started, `editors()` is read once per process (`refresh=True` re-reads) within its own 15 s bound, and no relative path is tried when `LOCALAPPDATA` is unset. `UnityCompile` takes an editor's own executable with no CLI installed.
  - **The owned project.** Its first build says so before it starts, reports progress and takes a cancel (the half-made folder goes); a folder too deep for URP's imports is refused with its fix (`UNITYTK_PROJECTS`); `ProjectBuilder.test(pipeline=)` runs the Built-in project too, and its verdict is the results file's (0 of 0 fails, an inconclusive is counted, not failed).
  - **Text.** The newer-record warning says to update the package's manifest version; the README, the part docs and the tests say what exists.
  - **Producer knowledge.** The test harness no longer sandboxes a mayapy child (none is started) and no test names a producer; the check that the editor operations agree with the other hosts' twins moves to the workspace (`m3trik/test/test_sync_workspace.py`). The layers baseline pays off 35 entries.
  - Test(s): `test_unity_host` (the licence wall from a real log, the logged and cancellable first build), `test_unity_cli` (the child's flags, timeout, cancel, cache, `projects`/`unregister`, and `RealCliTest` against the installed CLI), `test_project_builder` (the verdict, progress, cancel, the depth guard, the shared layer), `test_temp_projects` (3), `test_unity_compile`, `test_run_tests.test_git_runs_in_this_repo`.

The repo's `.editorconfig` states the C# style the C# pack names.** The pack's rule (`.claude/rules/csharp.md`: "the .NET coding conventions in `.editorconfig`") named a file the repo did not have. It now holds what the code already does: LF, four-space indent, Allman braces, block-scoped namespaces (C# 9), usings outside the namespace, `var`, PascalCase types, members and constants, camelCase public fields, parameters and locals, `_camelCase` private fields; all suggestions, applied at review, except the Unity analyzers' destroyed-object checks (UNT0007, UNT0008, UNT0029), which warn. `RenderEffectsController`'s two private fallback lists, the only private fields off that rule, are `_fallbackColorProperties` and `_fallbackEmissionProperties`.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. Fixed (review of the MayaSurface split): the untagged-subshader test is named for what it answers, the import order refuses a rank that would reach the material rewriter, the Maya surface importer's hot paths ask nothing they need not, its once-a-session warning names no model, one stand-in host serves every feature's tests, and the release window counts from 0.4.0 (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Kernel/Runtime/{RenderPipelineHosts,PureRenderPipelineHost}.cs`, `Kernel/URP/UniversalRenderPipelineHost.cs`, `Core/ImportOrder.cs`, `Features/MayaSurface/Editor/{MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter,MayaSurfaceTextureRoles}.cs`, the kernel's and features' tests, `test/core_dotnet/ImportOrderTests.cs`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`).**
  - **The untagged subshader.** `RenderPipelineHosts.HasUntaggedPass` became `DrawnThroughUntaggedSubshader` (unreleased, so no `MOVED_NAMES` row): since the ShadowPlane review it answers whether a scriptable pipeline draws the shader through a subshader tagged for no pipeline, and so is false of a Built-in lit shader even asked for `ForwardBase`. Its doc lists the Built-in lighting modes it refuses and says URP draws their objects as an error in the editor and a development build alone (URP 17.3's `RenderingUtils` error draw is `[Conditional("DEVELOPMENT_BUILD"), Conditional("UNITY_EDITOR")]`), not at all in a release player.
  - **Import order.** `ImportOrder.AfterMaterialDescriptions` (100) ran after every ranked importer only while every contract's rank stayed below 50, which nothing held. `ImportOrder.MaxRank` (new, 49) is the highest rank, and a contract declaring a rank outside 0 to `MaxRank` is refused with the range in the message.
  - **Hot paths.** `MayaSurfaceTextureRoles` asked the media provider and read two channels' toggles on every texture's first import before it checked whether a shaded material named the file. It now asks `MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.Named` (internal) first, so most textures ask nothing. The importer asked the media provider for the model's folders once per material; it asks once per model import.
  - **The once-a-session warning** (no host serves the pipeline) spoke for every model but named the first one's path; it names none. Its guard is the pipeline's name, not a flag, so a session that switches to another pipeline no host serves is told of that one too, as intended.
  - **One stand-in host.** `PureRenderPipelineHost` (new, `Kernel/Runtime`, beside the interface as every contract's `Pure*` stand-in is) is a host a test sets up as the pipeline it plays (its names and shaders, what it draws, its blend switch's answer; URP's names by default), serving no pipeline but one it is given and keeping each blend switch and render it is asked for. The kernel's, ShadowPlane's, MayaSurface's and EmissiveGroup's tests use it instead of four fakes of their own, so a member the interface gains is answered once.
  - **Release window.** The three 0.7.0 removal bullets (ShotMetadata, ArticulatedRig, ShadowPlane) and the ShadowPlane review bullet said 0.7.0 ships on or after 2026-11-19. CODE_STANDARD §5 counts the 30 days from the notice's first shipment, unitytk 0.4.0 (2026-10-24, `0e9953f`), so the date would have been 2026-11-23. No project uses unitytk, so no window applies and 0.7.0 is held for none.
  - Test(s): `RenderPipelineHostsTests` +2 (each Built-in lighting mode, `FORWARDBASE` included, disqualifies an untagged subshader, asked for the unlit modes or its own; the stand-in host serves no pipeline unless given one, answers as set up and keeps each blend switch); `MayaSurfaceMaterialImporterTests` (a pipeline no host serves: the warning names no model and comes once for two models); `test/core_dotnet` +1 (`ImportOrderTests`: a rank past `MaxRank`, or below 0, is refused naming the range), 45 in all; the package's 127 Edit Mode tests all pass in DevProject; `test_deploy_templates` (67), `test_base_test` (62), `test_surface_snapshot` (4), `test_unity_compile` (11), `test_editor_catalog` (5), `test_package` (2), `test_unity_kernel_live` (2), `test_unity_host` (18) and `test_maya_surface_materials_integration` (12) pass in Unity 6000.3 and Maya, no environment skip; `unity_package.compile` 0 errors, 0 warnings across all four builds; `check_layers`, `check_csharp`, `check_api_surface --semver` (nothing unaccounted) and `sync_scene_records --check` clean.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. The monolith dissolves: the assembly `M3trik.Unitytk` is gone. Its settings page is the kernel's, over a registry of the channels the features declare, each keyed by a persisted string; its carrier readers are the carrier feature's (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Features/Carrier/Editor/{CarrierImport,CarrierFile,UnitytkImportConfigurator}.cs`, `Kernel/Editor/{SettingsPage,SettingsChannelAttribute}.cs`, new or moved; `UnitytkSettings.cs` and `M3trik.Unitytk.asmdef` removed; `Core/PersistedStrings.cs`, `Core/SettingsFile.cs`, `Kernel/Editor/Settings.cs`, `Kernel/Tests/SettingsTests.cs`, each feature's importer, `Contracts/MaterialRecord.cs`, `README.md`; `unitytk/template_deployer.py`, `unitytk/__init__.py`, `pyproject.toml`; `docs/parts/{feature,foundation}.md`; the tests named below).** The base architecture's last step: nothing is left at the package root but `package.json`, `README.md` and `LICENSE.md`.
  - **The carrier.** `UnitytkSettings.cs` moved with its `.meta` to `Features/Carrier/Editor/CarrierImport.cs`, so the file keeps its GUID under the type it kept. `CarrierFile` and `UnitytkImportConfigurator` (the batch-end postprocessor that applies the import settings a channel asks for) have files of their own, all three in `M3trik.Unitytk.Carrier.Editor` with their global names kept, which verifiers and project scripts call. That assembly no longer references `M3trik.Unitytk`. `IsOwnMedia` (an ordinal folder compare) and `InOwnMediaFolder` (case ignored) are unchanged. Their console lines go through the kernel's `ImportLog` (`[unitytk] <model>: ...`), so the kernel-bypass baseline loses its last 7 calls (1 line, 6 warnings) and its last prior-art miss: the C# baselines hold 0 for unitytk. A refused record's warning names its importer's tag and the refusal (`[unitytk] [<Tag>] '<model>' carries ...`). The Maya surface importer adds a line saying what follows a refused materials record: the file's own materials are read.
  - **Settings.** The kernel's settings registry is the one place each channel is named. Its key is a `PersistedStrings` constant, its saved spelling unchanged and held by the surface snapshot (`OpacitySetting` `opacity`, `HighlightSetting` `highlight`, `AudioSetting` `audio`, `ShotsSetting` `shots`, `LightmapsSetting` `lightmaps`, `ShadowsSetting` `shadows`, `EmissiveSetting` `emissive`, `ArticulationSetting` `articulation`, `MayaSurfaceSetting` `maya_surface`, `MaterialsSetting` `materials`, and `AllowedFbxSetting` `allowed_fbx`, which `SettingsFile.AllowedFbxKey` now reads). A channel a record rides is keyed by the record's key, so its constant names the `UnitytkRecords` one rather than spelling it again. Each importer's channel constant reads its key, and the feature declares the channel once beside its importer, `[assembly: SettingsChannel(<Importer>.Channel, "<Label>", "<tip>")]` (new, in the kernel). `Settings.Channels` (new) lists the declarations by label, and the settings page draws a toggle per declaration. Every channel is on while the file does not set it, the file's own contract, so a declaration states no default. The record reads that used a channel constant as the record's key (lightmaps, shadows, materials) name the `UnitytkRecords` key instead.
  - **The page** (`M3trik.Unitytk.Kernel.SettingsPage`, internal) is Project Settings ▸ unitytk, written through `Settings.Set`. It is now found by every channel's key and label, and it shows a settings file that does not parse. Tools ▸ unitytk ▸ Settings keeps its path. That item only opens the page, so it stays beside it in the kernel: a tool is a menu over a feature's actions. The toggles are drawn by label (Articulated Rigs, Audio Events, Deliverable Materials, Emissive Groups, Highlights, Lightmaps, Maya Surface Materials, Opacity Fades, Shadow Planes, Shot Metadata).
  - **Removed**, each with a `MOVED_NAMES` row: `ImportGate` and `ImportGate.ShouldProcess` (`Settings.ShouldImport`); `UnitytkSettings` (`Settings`), its `IsChannelEnabled` (`Settings.ChannelOn`), `SetChannelEnabled` and `SetAllowedFbx` (`Settings.Set`) and `AllowedFbx` (`SettingsFile.Strings(SettingsFile.AllowedFbxKey)`); `UnitytkChannel` (`Settings.Channels`) and each of its members (the `PersistedStrings` key that names it). The page's and menu's classes were internal. `MaterialRecord.Entries(string json, string where, out string refusal)` stays though no importer calls it: it is how a script holding the record's text reads it, as a player's does with `LightmapMetadataController.materialsJson`, where no records provider exists.
  - **Deployer.** A 0.6.0 (or 0.0.15) project's `UnitytkSettings.cs` moves by a `_SPLIT_0_7` row to the carrier's `CarrierImport.cs`, its `.meta` with it. `TemplateDeployer._RETIRED` (new) names the files no file of the bundle replaces, and `deploy_package` deletes each with its `.meta`; today that is the root `M3trik.Unitytk.asmdef`, which left in place would compile an empty assembly. `status` counts a retired file as one more to clear (`legacy`, never `up_to_date`), `package_changes` plans its delete, Install says it removed it, and `_present` reads it as no content. The core component is the foundation, the carrier, and `package.json`, `README.md` and `LICENSE.md`, and the core's summary says so. Proved on the real package: `git archive v0.6.0` deployed by 0.6.0's own deployer into a scratch project, this tree deployed over it, then compared with a fresh deploy. Both hold the same 468 files, contents and GUIDs. `status` reads up to date, a plan finds nothing to change, and every 0.6.0 file's GUID is still in the project but the retired asmdef's. A per-component uninstall (`render_effects`, `maya_surface`) leaves the two trees equal again (410 files), and a whole uninstall removes the folder.
  - **Layers.** `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.m3trik.layers.csharp]` drops the monolith's `"."` rank; every assembly is in a declared part, and `check_layers` holds 0 C# exceptions.
  - **Hand-staged suites** stage the carrier's `UnitytkImportConfigurator.cs` where they staged `UnitytkSettings.cs`; `TemplateSources.closure` carries the kernel and carrier the importers read through. With `UnitytkChannel` gone, a staged set no longer reaches the Maya surface types.
  - **Known break for project asmdefs:** the assembly `M3trik.Unitytk` is gone. A project asmdef that referenced it (by name or GUID `4b3f599317fd4e599156dc44da75d243`) references what it uses: `M3trik.Unitytk.Carrier.Editor` for `CarrierImport`, `CarrierFile` or `UnitytkImportConfigurator`; `M3trik.Unitytk.Kernel` for `Settings` (what `ImportGate` and `UnitytkSettings` were); `M3trik.Unitytk.Core` for `PersistedStrings` and `SettingsFile` (what `UnitytkChannel` named); `M3trik.Unitytk.Contracts` for `MaterialRecord`. A script that called `ImportGate.ShouldProcess(path, UnitytkChannel.Audio)` calls `Settings.ShouldImport(path, PersistedStrings.AudioSetting)`; one that called `UnitytkSettings.SetChannelEnabled(UnitytkChannel.X, on)` calls `Settings.Set(PersistedStrings.XSetting, on)`.
  - Test(s): `SettingsTests` +1 (each declared channel is keyed once by a persisted string, the labels are unique and drawn in order, the page is found by each key, and the kernel declares none); `MayaSurfaceMaterialImporterTests` (a refused record's fallback is said); the package's 125 Edit Mode tests all pass in DevProject; `test_deploy_templates` (67: the 0.6.0 move now places a root asmdef and holds its delete in status, the plan and the deploy, ending with nothing planned; the flat layout's monolith file moves to the carrier and its asmdef goes; the carrier's readers are called by the carrier alone; the gate and the channel list are gone; the core's root holds the manifest, README and licence alone), `test_base_test` (62), `test_surface_snapshot` (4: the persisted strings rewritten with the eleven keys, a record's channel read through its `UnitytkRecords` key), `test_unity_compile` (11), `test_editor_catalog` (5), `test_package` (2), `test_unity_kernel_live` (2), `test_unity_host` (18), the hand-staged `test_render_effects_e2e` (10), `test_render_opacity_e2e` (3) and `test_shared_material_standalone` (1), and `test_maya_surface_materials_integration` (12) pass in Unity 6000.3 and Maya; environment skips: `test_keyed_visibility` (1) and `test_standalone_enduser` (4), whose fixtures need `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS`; `test/core_dotnet` (44), `test/models_dotnet` (9); `unity_package.compile` 0 errors, 0 warnings across all four builds; `check_layers`, `check_csharp` (0 frozen), `check_api_surface --semver` (minor, nothing unaccounted) and `sync_scene_records --check` clean.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. Fixed (review of the EmissiveGroup and RenderEffects splits): Built-in's blend switch needs all four of its properties, the emissive mask advice looks its shader up only for a mask and says when it is missing, the Render Effects actions class is `RenderEffectsActions`, and the behaviour the splits changed is stated and tested (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Kernel/Runtime/BuiltInRenderPipelineHost.cs`, `Kernel/Editor/OpContext.cs`, `Kernel/Tests/`, `Features/EmissiveGroup/`, `Features/RenderEffects/` (`Editor/RenderEffectsActions.cs`, `Tests/RenderEffectsActionsTests.cs`, renamed), `Features/Carrier/Tests/RecordsTests.cs`, `Tools/RenderEffects/RenderEffectsMenu.cs`, `unitytk/__init__.py`, `README.md`, `docs/parts/feature.md`).**
  - **Built-in's blend switch.** `BuiltInRenderPipelineHost.SetBlended` switched on `_Mode` alone. A custom Built-in shader with a `_Mode` and its blend written in its passes therefore took `_Mode` 2, `_ALPHABLEND_ON`, the Transparent tag and queue 3000 in the model's material, with no "no blend switch" warning, and the fade popped at zero. The switch now needs `_Mode`, `_SrcBlend`, `_DstBlend` and `_ZWrite`, as RenderEffects' own check did before the split, and leaves any other material unchanged, as the interface says.
  - **Emissive groups.** A mask texture's first-import settings ask `Settings.ChannelOn(Channel, assetPath)`, so a settings file that does not parse is warned against the texture. `Shader.Find("m3trik/EmissiveGroupsLit")` ran on every model import. It now runs only for a channels-encoding record that names a mask (`Attach` takes a `Func<Shader>`). A shader that is not found (its runtime part missing, or its first import still to come) is reported as missing, no longer as "the URP render pipeline does not draw it". The request for animated custom properties is `EmissiveGroupImporter.AskImportSettings` (internal), which takes the import-settings contract.
  - **Render Effects.** The actions class `M3trik.Unitytk.RenderEffects.RenderEffects` had its namespace's name, so code in a `M3trik.Unitytk.*` namespace could not call it as `RenderEffects.FixSceneObjects`. It is `RenderEffectsActions` (unreleased). The `RenderEffectsTools` row of `MOVED_NAMES` points at `RenderEffectsActions.FixSceneObjects`, and the menu path is unchanged. The split changed two behaviours, now stated in the action's doc. `FixSceneObjects` gives a controller to a renderer under an inactive object too: the menu before it asked `GetComponentInParent<Animator>()`, which skips inactive objects, so an object keyed to appear later got none. It skips assets, hidden objects and a prefab stage's objects. The importer's node recording and curve rebinding are `RenderEffectsImporter.Record` and `Rebind` (internal), each taking the import-settings contract.
  - **The kernel.** `OpenScenes.Scope` leaves out preview scenes, so `Lightmaps.Apply` and `FixSceneObjects` with no scene named no longer edit an open prefab stage (before the split, the lightmaps' own scope included one). `OpContext.Issue` defaults to severity `error` and `ctx.Raise` to `warning`, and both XML docs now say so.
