- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. MayaSurface split out of the monolith: an editor and a test assembly, one type per file; the importer reads through the contracts and asks the render-pipeline host for its shaders, which gains the lit shader; the media contract says which files lie in a model's folder of its own; the import order names Unity's description preprocessors (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Features/MayaSurface/{Editor,Tests}/`, `test/core_dotnet/ImportOrderTests.cs`, new; `Kernel/Runtime/IRenderPipelineHost.cs`, `BuiltInRenderPipelineHost.cs`, `Kernel/URP/UniversalRenderPipelineHost.cs`, `Contracts/Editor/IModelMedia.cs`, `Features/Carrier/Editor/CarrierRecords.cs`, `Core/ImportOrder.cs`, `UnitytkSettings.cs`).** The base architecture's seventh feature split, and the last: no feature calls the monolith now.
  - **Assemblies.** `M3trik.Unitytk.MayaSurface.Editor` (editor only, as the whole feature always was) holds the 1,102-line `MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.cs` split one type per file: `MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter` (moved with its `.meta`), `MayaSurfaceTextureRoles`, `MayaSurfaceShaders` (the writers), `MayaSurface` (a record-less surface's reading), `MayaSurfaceMaps`, `MayaSurfaceMap`, `MayaSurfaceAlpha`, `MayaSurfacePacking` and `MayaSurfaceTextureRole`, global names kept. It references neither the monolith nor a peer feature. No `Model/` part: the host-free rules (an entry's values as a shader reads them, a record-less surface's reading) have a reference in pythontk's `MaterialFamilies`, but no `ptk.Conformance` cases to hold a port to, so they stay in the editor part.
  - **The importer** reads the materials record off the file through the carrier (`IRecordSource.Record`, then `Read` at `MaterialRecord.CurrentVersion`: a refusal is the provider's warning) and its maps through `IModelMedia` (`MediaFolders`, `RequestEmbeddedFiles`, `InOwnMediaFolder`); asks `Settings.ShouldImport` for its two channels (`MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.Channel`, `materials`, the record's key; `SurfaceChannel`, `maya_surface`) and `Settings.ChannelOn` for a map's first-import role; declares `[assembly: ReadsRecord(UnitytkRecords.Materials, typeof(MaterialRecord))]` beside the lightmaps' declaration of the same record (the records audit reads each key once); and reports through `ImportLog`: the kernel-bypass baseline loses its 1 console line and 10 warnings. Its summary line reads `[unitytk] <model>: shaded N material(s): ...` (was `[MayaSurface] '<model>': ...`). A writer's loss names the model too.
  - **The order.** `ImportOrder.AfterMaterialDescriptions` (new, 100) is the order of an importer that rewrites the materials Unity's own description preprocessors made (Built-in at -990, URP at -980, -970 and -960), after every importer a contract's rank orders: the importer's bare `ORDER = 100`, named in the core. `IRecordSource` declares no rank (the record is read off the file, not from an importer that runs first), so no order is read from it.
  - **The render pipeline.** The writers ask `RenderPipelineHosts.Current` instead of the pipeline asset's type name: Built-in's targets where it is `RenderPipelineHosts.BuiltIn` (its `LitShader`, Autodesk Interactive, the four `Unlit/*` shaders, Standard's cutout), a scriptable pipeline's otherwise (its `LitShader` written by URP Lit's properties, its `UnlitShader`, the pipeline asset's Autodesk Interactive graphs). The base colour and map go by the host's properties, and Built-in's Fade and Opaque and a URP surface's blend through its `SetBlended`, which writes what the writers wrote by hand (URP's switch was written from this importer). A pipeline no host serves (HDRP) keeps Unity's mapping, said once per session, naming it (`NameOf`).
    - `IRenderPipelineHost.LitShader` (new): `Standard` on Built-in, `Universal Render Pipeline/Lit` on URP. A host a project implements must add it.
  - **The media contract.** `IModelMedia.InOwnMediaFolder(model, file)` (new; the carrier's `CarrierImport.InOwnMediaFolder`, the stand-in's false) is whether a file lies in the model's folder of its own, where every extraction first imports its files: where a map takes its role. `IsOwnMedia` is not that test: it also takes a file the model extracted beside itself, and compares the folder's case. A provider a project implements must add it.
  - **Removed**, each with a `MOVED_NAMES` row: `MayaSurfaceShaders.Pipeline` (with `BuiltIn`, `Universal`, `Other`) and `MayaSurfaceShaders.Current()` (ask `RenderPipelineHosts.Current`, `BuiltIn`, `For`). `MayaSurfaceShaders.Pick` and `PickArnold` take the host instead of the enum: `Pick(entry, maps, IRenderPipelineHost host, out label, string assetPath = null)`, `PickArnold(IRenderPipelineHost host, out label)`. The importer's internal `_roles` is read through `RoleOf`.
  - **Deployer.** A 0.6.0 project's importer moves by its `_SPLIT_0_7` row; no 0.0.15 row names it.
  - **Hand-staged suites.** `TemplateSources.closure` reached the importer through the word `MayaSurface` in `UnitytkSettings.cs`'s channel list; it reaches `MayaSurface.cs` and the types it names now, never the importer, so a hand-staged set no longer shades its materials on import.
  - **Known break for project asmdefs:** one that references `M3trik.Unitytk` to reach the MayaSurface types must reference `M3trik.Unitytk.MayaSurface.Editor`; a script that called `MayaSurfaceShaders.Current()` or a `Pick` taking the enum passes `RenderPipelineHosts.Current`.

- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. Fixed (review of the ShadowPlane split): URP's host no longer counts a Built-in lit shader as drawn for a helper pass beside it, the importer's tests cover an atlas, a URP-like host and the texture gate, and stale text goes (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Kernel/Runtime/RenderPipelineHosts.cs`, `Kernel/URP/`, `Kernel/Tests/`, `Features/ShadowPlane/`, `Features/LightmapMetadata/Editor/LightmapProbes.cs`, `Features/MayaSurface/MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.cs`, `README.md`).**
  - **URP's `Draws`.** `RenderPipelineHosts.HasUntaggedPass` was true of a subshader tagged for no pipeline with any one pass of the modes asked, so a Built-in lit shader (`ForwardBase` passes, which URP draws with its error shader) holding one untagged helper pass read as drawn by URP. A subshader holding one of the Built-in pipeline's own lighting passes (`Always`, `ForwardBase`, `ForwardAdd`, `Deferred`, `PrepassBase`, `PrepassFinal`, `Vertex`, `VertexLM`, `VertexLMRGBM`) no longer counts, whichever modes are asked. The review asked that every pass be one URP draws or a utility pass; that would refuse the package's own `m3trik/ShadowPlane`, whose `ShadowPlaneCache` pass has a `LightMode` no camera draws (a command buffer renders it), so such a pass neither counts nor disqualifies. URP's `UntaggedLightModes` says it copies `DrawObjectsPass`'s shader tags (Unity 6.3 / URP 17.3).
  - **The HDRP message** no longer claims each plane "draws its silhouette as imported", which nothing measured: the planes keep the material Unity imported, which may draw opaque.
  - **Stale text.** The Maya surface importer's header and `LightmapProbes.FbxToUnity`'s comment named `ShadowPlaneController` for the importer's behaviour (`ShadowPlaneImporter`). The README's row for a shadow plane on the unlit shader adds the cause "the pipeline's host does not draw the shader", and HDRP's case. `ShadowPlaneModel.FarPoint` says a zero direction is noon here, where the reference raises `ValueError`. The three entries below that remove `[Obsolete]` members (ShotMetadata, ArticulatedRig, ShadowPlane) say they were deprecated 2026-10-20 and first shipped in 0.4.0 (2026-10-24), so CODE_STANDARD §5's window would have run to 2026-11-23. No project uses unitytk, so none applies.
  - Test(s): `ShadowPlaneImporterTests` +4 (an atlased plane's tile rides `_Rect` on a normalised mesh on the shared material, and its UVs stay in the tile on the fallback; a URP-like host's unlit shader, base map, colour and blend switch; a horizon map pinned on its first import only while the channel is on), its tag test renamed for what it reads; `RenderPipelineHostsTests` +1 (a `ForwardBase` pass disqualifies a subshader, a shadow caster and a command buffer's pass do not; `HasUntaggedPass(Standard, "ForwardBase")` is now false); `UniversalRenderPipelineHostTests` +2 (URP's host draws `m3trik/ShadowPlane`, and not a Built-in lit shader for its helper pass); the package's 100 Edit Mode tests all pass in DevProject; `test_shadow_plane_integration` (4), `test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration` (15), `test_unity_kernel_live` (2), `test_deploy_templates` (67), `test_base_test` (62), `test_surface_snapshot` (4), `test_unity_compile` (11), `test_editor_catalog` (5), `test_package` (2) in Unity 6000.3 and Maya, no environment skip; `test/core_dotnet` (41), `test/models_dotnet` (9); `unity_package.compile` 0 errors, 0 warnings across all four builds; `check_layers`, `check_csharp`, `check_api_surface --semver` (nothing unaccounted) and `sync_scene_records --check` clean.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. RenderEffects split out of the monolith: a runtime, editor and test assembly; its menu item is an action held to the kernel's checks; the importer asks the render-pipeline host for the blend switch and the carrier for the model's import settings; the kernel scopes an action's scenes and marks a scene an edit with no context changes (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Features/RenderEffects/{Runtime,Editor,Tests}/`, `Tools/RenderEffects/`, `Kernel/Editor/OpenScenes.cs`, new; `Kernel/Editor/OpContext.cs`, `Features/LightmapMetadata/Editor/{Lightmaps,ApplyRun}.cs`, `Features/LightmapMetadata/URP/Editor/DeliverableLook.cs`).** The base architecture's sixth feature split.
  - **Assemblies.**
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.RenderEffects` (every platform) holds `RenderEffectsController`; it moved with its `.meta`: GUID, serialized fields (`colorProperty` keeps its `_BaseColor` default) and global name stay, and the surface snapshot holds. Its property-block writes are unchanged, read-modify-write beside `EmissiveGroupController`'s and `ShadowPlaneController`'s.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.RenderEffects.Editor` holds `RenderEffectsImporter` (global name kept) and the actions, `M3trik.Unitytk.RenderEffects.RenderEffectsActions`.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.Tools.RenderEffects` holds the menu.
  - **The importer** asks `Settings.ShouldImport` per channel (`RenderEffectsImporter.OpacityChannel`, `HighlightChannel`), the carrier's `IModelImportSettings` for animated custom properties, unresampled curves and the detection pass, and reports through `ImportLog`: the kernel-bypass baseline loses its 4 console lines, 3 warnings and the `Undo` call.
  - **The render pipeline.** A fading renderer's material is switched by `RenderPipelineHosts.Current.SetBlended` (URP's switch was written from this importer's URP branch: the same properties, keywords, depth pass and queue), asked first of a scratch copy whether the shader has a switch. A pipeline no host serves (HDRP) keeps every material as Unity imported it, said once per model; the importer no longer reaches HDRP's `HDMaterial.ValidateMaterial` by reflection, nor sets HDRP's property names. `Reset()` picks the host's `BaseColorProperty` where the material holds it.
  - **The action.** `RenderEffectsActions.FixSceneObjects(string[] scenes = null, OpContext ctx = null)` gives each Renderer under an Animator in the open scenes (or those named, by path) that has none a `RenderEffectsController`, asking `ctx.ShouldCreate` and adding through `ctx.AddComponent`; it returns how many it added. `Tools/RenderEffects/RenderEffectsMenu.cs` runs it through `OpContext.RunMenu` on the same path, **Tools ▸ unitytk ▸ Render Effects ▸ Fix Scene Objects**: one undo step, progress with Cancel.
  - **The kernel.**
    - `OpenScenes.Scope(scenes)` (new): an action's open scenes by path (any separator, any case), every loaded scene when it names none, a closed one refused before anything runs. `Lightmaps.Apply`'s own scope moved down to it, its second user.
    - `OpContextEdits.AddComponent` and `Destroy` with no context mark the scene they change, so it saves the edit (undo marks it when a context runs): `AudioEvents.FixSceneObjects()` called with none added components a scene did not save. The lightmaps' and the URP look's own marks after a delete are gone.
  - **Moved names** (`MOVED_NAMES`): `RenderEffectsTools` (its menu method) is `M3trik.Unitytk.RenderEffects.RenderEffectsActions.FixSceneObjects`.
  - **Deployer.** A 0.6.0 project's controller moves by its `_SPLIT_0_7` row; the 0.0.15 row names the new path. A hand-staged set (`TemplateSources.copy_flat`) names the component and its importer.
  - **Hand-staged suites.** `TemplateSources.closure` reached the URP door's files through the word `Lightmaps` in `UnitytkSettings.cs`'s channel list (the lightmaps' actions, whose look a door provides) and copied them into a project without URP, so every suite that stages the scripts by hand failed to compile there since the LightmapMetadata split. A door (`TemplateSources.is_door`: not auto-referenced, compiled only with its package) is no part of a set now; its contract's stand-in serves.
  - **Known break for project asmdefs:** one that references `M3trik.Unitytk` to reach `RenderEffectsController` must reference `M3trik.Unitytk.RenderEffects` (and `.Editor` for the importer); a script that called the menu's class calls `M3trik.Unitytk.RenderEffects.RenderEffectsActions.FixSceneObjects()`.
  - Test(s): the feature's Edit Mode tests (`RenderEffectsActionsTests` 5: one Ctrl+Z restores the scene, the plan names the run's change, a second run plans nothing, a run with no context leaves its scene to save, a closed scene is refused; `RenderEffectsImporterTests` 4: the host's blend in place and never on a material no fade needs, a shader with no switch said and left alone, a pipeline no host serves keeps Unity's materials, a stepped visibility becomes its fade; `RenderEffectsControllerTests` 1: opacity rides the shared block beside another component's value), `OpenScenesTests` 2, `OpContextTests` +1; the package's 93 Edit Mode tests all pass in DevProject; `test_render_effects_e2e` (10), `test_render_opacity_e2e` (3), `test_render_opacity_controller` (2), `test_blender_render_effects_e2e` (6), `test_bool_to_fade_curve` (22), `test_fade_window_preservation` (2), `test_shared_material_standalone` (1), `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` (12), `test_deploy_templates` (67), `test_base_test` (62), `test_surface_snapshot` (4), `test_unity_compile` (11), `test_editor_catalog` (5), `test_package` (2) pass in Unity 6000.3, Maya and Blender; environment skips: `test_keyed_visibility` (1), `test_multi_section_audio_integration` (4) and `test_standalone_enduser` (4), whose fixtures need `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS`; `test/core_dotnet` (41), `test/models_dotnet` (9); `unity_package.compile` 0 errors, 0 warnings across all four builds; `check_layers`, `check_csharp`, `check_api_surface --semver` (nothing unaccounted) and `sync_scene_records --check` clean.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. EmissiveGroup split out of the monolith: a runtime, editor and test assembly; the importer reads through the contracts, and a new one says which import settings a channel needs of a model; the kernel reads a texture's channel and names a pipeline for every feature (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Features/EmissiveGroup/{Runtime,Editor,Tests}/`, `Contracts/Editor/IModelImportSettings.cs`, new; `Features/Carrier/Editor/CarrierRecords.cs`, `Kernel/Editor/Settings.cs`, `Kernel/Runtime/RenderPipelineHosts.cs`, `Features/ShadowPlane/Editor/ShadowPlaneImporter.cs`, `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs`).** The base architecture's fifth feature split.
  - **Assemblies.**
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.EmissiveGroup` (every platform) holds `EmissiveGroupController`, the `EmissiveGroup` record it keeps (now in a file of its own), `EmissiveGroupsLit.shader` and `EmissiveGroups.hlsl`.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.EmissiveGroup.Editor` holds `EmissiveGroupImporter` and the generated `EmissiveGroupsRecord.g.cs` beside it (its channel line in `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs` names the importer).
    - The controller, shader and include moved with their `.meta`: GUIDs, serialized fields and global names stay, and the surface snapshot holds.
  - **The importer** reads through the carrier (`IRecordSource`), asks `Settings.ShouldImport(assetPath, EmissiveGroupImporter.Channel)` (`emissive`, the settings key; a mask texture's first-import settings ask `Settings.ChannelOn`), declares `[assembly: ReadsRecord(UnitytkRecords.EmissiveGroups, typeof(EmissiveGroupsRecord))]` and reports through `ImportLog`: the kernel-bypass baseline loses its 3 console lines. A stale controller's removal is said as `removed the stale Emissive Group Controller`.
  - **The render pipeline.** `EmissiveGroupsLit` is URP's alone, and the importer never rewires a material to it: where the record's channels encoding names a mask, the import log says to bind it on `EmissiveGroupsLit` only where `RenderPipelineHosts.Current` draws the shader; elsewhere it warns, naming the pipeline, that the model keeps Unity's materials (graft `EmissiveGroups.hlsl` into a shader of the project's).
  - **The import-settings contract.** `IModelImportSettings` (`Contracts/Editor`, new) is what a channel asks of a model's importer, applied after the batch with one reimport of that model: `RequestAnimatedCustomProperties`, `RequestSparseCurves` and `IsDetectionPass`. Neither the media contract (what a model embeds and where its files lie) nor the records' (what it carries) holds it. The carrier provides it over `CarrierImport`, whose batch-end configurator applies it with the media's extraction; `PureModelImportSettings` keeps what was asked and applies nothing. Keyed weights ask it for animated custom properties; RenderEffects' split reads it too.
  - **The kernel.**
    - `Settings.ChannelOn(channel, assetPath = null)` (new) is a channel's toggle alone, for a texture's first-import settings, warning once of a settings file that does not parse as `ShouldImport` does. ShadowPlane's texture pinning read `Settings.File.Bool` and so lost that warning: a texture-only import with a torn settings file silently took the last good values. It asks `ChannelOn` now.
    - `RenderPipelineHosts.NameOf(host)` (new) names a host's pipeline in a message, or the pipeline drawing now by its asset's type and name where no host serves it. ShadowPlane's own copy of the rule is gone.
  - **Deployer.** A 0.6.0 project's EmissiveGroup files move by `_SPLIT_0_7` rows; the 0.0.15 rows name the new paths.
  - **Known break for project asmdefs:** one that references `M3trik.Unitytk` to reach `EmissiveGroupController` or `EmissiveGroup` must reference `M3trik.Unitytk.EmissiveGroup` (and `.Editor` for the importer).
  - Test(s): the feature's Edit Mode tests (`EmissiveGroupImporterTests` 7: the current record fills the controller in slot order and deletes the Blender proxy, keyed weights from either transport rebind, another version is refused and a stale controller goes, the channel off leaves the model alone, the mask advice follows the host, a mask is named by its suffix, the record declared; `EmissiveGroupControllerTests` 2: weights by name, and the weights ride the shared property block beside another component's value), `RecordsTests` +1 (a model is on a detection pass only while a setting asked of it is off, the stand-in against the carrier), `SettingsTests` +1, `RenderPipelineHostsTests` +1; the package's 80 Edit Mode tests all pass in DevProject; `test_emissive_groups_integration` (4), `test_controller_runtime_integration` (4), `test_shadow_plane_integration` (4), `test_deploy_templates` (67), `test_base_test` (61), `test_surface_snapshot` (4), `test_unity_compile` (11), `test_editor_catalog` (5), `test_package` (2) in Unity 6000.3 and Maya, no environment skip; `test/core_dotnet` (41), `test/models_dotnet` (9); `unity_package.compile` 0 errors, 0 warnings across all four builds; `check_layers`, `check_csharp`, `check_api_surface --semver` (nothing unaccounted) and `sync_scene_records --check` clean.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. Fixed (review of the render-pipeline host and the LightmapMetadata split): an action called with no context says what it found, URP's half of Apply is held to the kernel's checks, and six smaller defects (`Kernel/Editor/OpContext.cs`, `Features/LightmapMetadata/`, `Features/AudioEvent/Editor/AudioEvents.cs`, `Features/Carrier/Editor/RecordsAudit.cs`, `Kernel/URP/Tests/`, `test/test_surface_snapshot.py`).**
  - **Problems with no context.** The actions reported a problem as `ctx?.Issue(...)`, so `Lightmaps.Apply()` from a script or `unity.csharp.exec` printed none of the warnings `ApplySceneLightmaps()` printed. `OpContextEdits.Raise(code, message, severity = "warning", location, fix)` (new) records the issue on a context and, with none, writes the Console line a menu's run prints, titled `unitytk` (`OpContext.RunMenu`'s report and it share one formatter). `AudioEvents.FixSceneObjects`, `Records.Audit`, `Lightmaps.Apply` and the URP door's look raise through it; m3trik's `check_csharp.py` refuses `?.Issue(`.
  - **URP's half of Apply.** The URP integration project now runs Apply as the kernel's checks do: the dry run plans exactly what the live run does (the shading, the probe's prefiltered atlas, the materials, the tone-map pass, the Volume and its profile), and a second run plans nothing. `UniversalRenderPipelineHost`'s `Serves`, `Draws` and `SetBlended` on URP Lit (transparent and back to opaque) are Edit Mode tests in the door's test assembly, which now references URP.
  - **Auto Generate is gone.** Apply switched `Lightmapping.giWorkflowMode` off a project's Auto Generate lighting; on Unity 6000.3, the package's floor, the mode is always on demand and its setter keeps nothing (measured: neither the setter nor a `LightingSettings` given `autoGenerate = true` keeps it), so the switch, its issue code `Lightmaps.AutoGenerateCode` and the README's claim are deleted rather than given a scene to dirty.
  - **Smaller.** The stale-records warning is `LightmapMetadataController`'s `OnEnable` alone again: `OnValidate` binds quietly, so an inspector edit, an undo or a domain reload no longer repeats it. Apply says the tone map it installed through `ctx.Log` (three.js ACESFilmic, the exposure, the renderers, the Volume), as it did before the split. `Made<T>` sits in a file of its own. A test of the host registry no longer leaks its second fake when an assertion fails. Stale names in comments (`MaterialRecord.cs`; `UnitytkSettings.cs`'s channel list, which now names each split feature's importer) and the LightmapMetadata entry's known break (`MaterialRecord`, `MapAbsence`, `MaterialColor` moved assembly) are corrected.
  - **The surface snapshot** records each component's GUID and fields, no longer its path: a file moves by design, and the GUID is what saved data names.
  - Test(s): `OpContextTests` +1 (a problem raised with and without a context, and as a menu says it), `LightmapMetadataControllerTests` (new, 1: an edit that reaches `OnValidate` says nothing of stale records; enabling does), `UniversalRenderPipelineHostTests` +3; the package's 68 Edit Mode tests all pass in DevProject; `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` (12, the URP plan included), `test_audio_events_integration` (4), `test_unity_kernel_live` (2), `test_surface_snapshot` (4), `test_deploy_templates` (67), `test_base_test` (61), `test_unity_compile` (11), `test_editor_catalog` (5), `test_package` (2) in Unity 6000.3 and Maya, no environment skip; `test/core_dotnet` (41), `test/models_dotnet` (9); `unity_package.compile` 0 errors, 0 warnings across all four builds; `check_layers`, `check_csharp`, `check_api_surface --semver` (nothing unaccounted), `sync_scene_records --check` clean.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. ShadowPlane split out of the monolith: its model under `dotnet test`, a runtime, editor and test assembly; the importer asks the render-pipeline host, which gains the base map's property and, on URP, draws a legacy unlit pass; the media contract gains `MediaFolders`; the overdue `[Obsolete]` record types and members are gone (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Features/ShadowPlane/{Model,Runtime,Editor,Tests}/`, `Kernel/URP/Tests/`, `test/models_dotnet/ShadowPlaneModelTests.cs`, new; `Kernel/Runtime/`, `Kernel/URP/UniversalRenderPipelineHost.cs`, `Contracts/Editor/IModelMedia.cs`, `Features/Carrier/Editor/CarrierRecords.cs`, `Features/LightmapMetadata/Editor/Lightmaps.cs`).** The base architecture's fourth feature split.
  - **Assemblies.**
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.ShadowPlane.Model` (no engine references) holds `ShadowPlaneModel`, pythontk's `ShadowProjection.model` and `ShadowModel.placement`, global name and signatures kept. `FarPoint` (new) is the point a sun is written as (`ShadowProjection.far_point`), which `EvaluateDirectional` calls.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.ShadowPlane` (every platform) holds `ShadowPlaneController`, `ShadowPlaneNames` and `ShadowPlaneCache` (each now in a file of its own), `ShadowPlane.shader` and `ShadowPlaneHorizon.hlsl`.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.ShadowPlane.Editor` holds `ShadowPlaneImporter` and the generated `ShadowRecord.g.cs` beside it (its channel line in `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs` names the importer).
    - The controller, the shader and its include moved with their `.meta`: GUIDs, serialized fields (but the two removed below) and global names stay, and the surface snapshot holds.
  - **The model is held to pythontk's golden cases** (`ptk.Conformance.cases("shadow_projection")`, the Unity suite's seed 3) under `dotnet test` in `test/models_dotnet`: every term of the model from the case's source point and, for a sun, from its direction; the far point; the canvas's placement; each within the document's tolerances.
  - **The importer** reads through the carrier (`IRecordSource`, `IModelMedia`), asks `Settings.ShouldImport(assetPath, ShadowPlaneImporter.Channel)` (a texture's first-import pinning asks the channel's toggle alone, `Settings.File.Bool`: the allowlist is a model's), declares `[assembly: ReadsRecord(UnitytkRecords.Shadows, typeof(ShadowRecord))]` and reports through `ImportLog`: the kernel-bypass baseline loses its 9 console warnings.
  - **The render pipeline.** The importer asks `RenderPipelineHosts.Current` instead of the pipeline asset's type name: one shared material per rig type on `m3trik/ShadowPlane` where the host draws it (`Draws`), else each plane on the host's unlit, blended shader (`UnlitShader(true)`, `SetBlended`, `BaseMapProperty`, `BaseColorProperty`).
    - A pipeline no host serves (HDRP) keeps Unity's imported material, said once per model in the import log; the importer used to rewire it to `HDRP/Unlit` by property names. Its planes still follow their source.
    - A plane on the fallback keeps its in-tile UVs: only the shared material reads `_Rect`, so an atlased plane on the fallback showed the whole atlas.
    - `IRenderPipelineHost.BaseMapProperty` (new): `_MainTex` on Built-in, `_BaseMap` on URP. A host a project implements must add it.
    - URP's `Draws` also counts a subshader tagged for no pipeline whose pass URP draws (no `LightMode`, `SRPDefaultUnlit`, `UniversalForward`, `UniversalForwardOnly`), through `RenderPipelineHosts.HasUntaggedPass` (new; modes compared ignoring case, as Unity reports Built-in's upper-cased). The package shader is legacy CG that URP draws as `SRPDefaultUnlit`: by the tag alone a URP project would have lost the shared material and the horizon march. `Kernel/URP/Tests/` holds the door's first Edit Mode tests (compiled only with URP and the Test Framework).
  - **The media contract.** `IModelMedia.MediaFolders(assetPath)` (new; the carrier's `CarrierImport.MediaFolders`, the stand-in's one folder) is where a model's files are looked for, nearest first; `Lightmaps.MediaDirs` reads it instead of deriving it again. A provider a project implements must add it.
  - **Removed**, due in 0.5.0 (deprecated 2026-10-20, first shipped in 0.4.0 on 2026-10-24: no deprecation window applies: no project uses unitytk), each with a `MOVED_NAMES` row: `ShadowPlaneRecord`, `ShadowPlaneAtlas` and `ShadowPlaneHorizon` (the record reads through `ShadowRecord.Shape.Plane`, `PlaneAtlas`, `PlaneHorizon`); `ShadowPlaneController.horizonMapping` and `horizonEncoding` (the record's version states both); the six-argument `ShadowPlaneController.HorizonRefusal` (call `HorizonRefusal(size, spans, heightScale, bounds)`). A saved plane drops the two fields on its next save.
  - **Deployer.** A 0.6.0 project's ShadowPlane files move by `_SPLIT_0_7` rows; the 0.0.15 rows name the new paths.
  - **Known break for project asmdefs:** one that references `M3trik.Unitytk` to reach the ShadowPlane types must reference `M3trik.Unitytk.ShadowPlane` (or `.Model`, `.Editor`).
  - Test(s): the feature's Edit Mode tests (`ShadowPlaneImporterTests` 7: the current record builds the plane on the shared material, another version is refused and a stale controller goes, the channel off leaves the model alone, the host's unlit fallback, a pipeline no host serves keeps Unity's material, the shader drawn in Built-in and through URP's unlit pass, the record declared; `ShadowPlaneControllerTests` 3; `ShadowPlaneNamesTests` 2), `UniversalRenderPipelineHostTests` 2, `RenderPipelineHostsTests` 5, `RecordsTests` 4; the package's 63 all pass in DevProject; `test/models_dotnet` (9: the model over pythontk's 25 golden cases; a far point off by 1e-7 fails two) and `test/core_dotnet` (41); `test_shadow_plane_integration` (4), `test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration` (15), `test_blender_shadow_plane_integration` (8), `test_embedded_dependencies_integration` (5), `test_controller_runtime_integration` (4), `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` (12) and `test_unity_kernel_live` (2) in Unity 6000.3, Maya and Blender, no environment skip; `test_deploy_templates` (67), `test_surface_snapshot`, `test_unity_compile`, `test_editor_catalog`, `test_package`, `test_base_test`; `unity_package.compile` 0 errors, 0 warnings across all four builds; `check_layers`, `check_csharp`, `check_api_surface --semver` (nothing unaccounted), `sync_scene_records --check` and `sync_shadow_shaders --check` clean.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. LightmapMetadata split out of the monolith: a runtime, editor and test assembly, a URP door for the tone-map pass and its install, and its menu items as actions; the materials record moves to the core's contracts (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Features/LightmapMetadata/{Runtime,Editor,Tests,URP,URP/Editor}/`, `Tools/LightmapMetadata/`, new; `Contracts/MaterialRecord.cs`, `MaterialRecord.g.cs`, `MapAbsence.g.cs`, moved; `Contracts/MaterialColor.cs`, new).** The base architecture's third feature split, and the first to read the render-pipeline host.
  - **Assemblies.**
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.LightmapMetadata` (every platform) holds `LightmapMetadataController`, the records it keeps (`LightmappedObject`, `LightmapProbeRecord`, `LightmapHighlightCopy`, `LightmapBinding`, `DeliverableRecipe`, `DeliverableShadedRenderer`, each now in a file of its own), `DeliverableShading`, `DeliverablePmrem`, the shaders and the generated `RenderingPolicy.g.cs`.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.LightmapMetadata.Editor` holds `LightmapMetadataImporter`, the generated `LightmapRecord.g.cs` and `HandoffBlock.g.cs` beside it, and the actions.
    - Files moved with their `.meta`: GUIDs, serialized fields and global names stay, and the surface snapshot holds. A type that moved assembly but kept its name needs nothing of a scene or an Assembly-CSharp script.
  - **The importer** reads through the carrier (`IRecordSource`, `IModelMedia`), asks `Settings.ShouldImport(assetPath, LightmapMetadataImporter.Channel)` (and the materials channel), declares `[assembly: ReadsRecord(...)]` for the lightmaps, handoff and materials records, and reports through `ImportLog`. A duplicate record name with no hierarchy is now said at import; the controller no longer logs it from an editor-only block.
  - **Actions.** `Lightmaps.Apply(string[] scenes = null, IRecordSource records = null, OpContext ctx = null)` is Apply Scene Lightmaps; `Lightmaps.RemoveLook(IDeliverableLook look = null, OpContext ctx = null)` is Remove Deliverable Look. `Tools/LightmapMetadata/LightmapMetadataMenu.cs` runs both through `OpContext.RunMenu`, on the same menu paths.
    - Each scene edit asks `ctx.ShouldChange` first (which records the undo); each texture, material, renderer asset and profile written asks `ShouldWrite`, once per file per run; a probe and the look's Volume are made or deleted through `OpContextEdits`. Fourteen `Undo` calls and the console warnings are gone: each warning is an issue with a stable code (`Lightmaps.*Code`).
    - Each part converges: a second run plans nothing (bindings, probe placement, cubemap and atlas, materials, the look are compared before being changed).
    - `scenes` limits a run to open scenes by path; `records` is the provider the shading reads each model's materials record through (`MaterialRecord.Entries(Shape.Payload)`, new).
    - A renderer's lightmap index is runtime state that undo does not restore, so the controller follows its record on undo (`OnValidate`): one Ctrl+Z after Apply takes the lightmaps off the renderers too.
  - **The render-pipeline host.** Apply asks `RenderPipelineHosts.Current` whether the pipeline draws Deliverable Lit (`Draws`) instead of comparing the pipeline asset's type name.
  - **The URP door**, two assemblies, not auto-referenced and compiled only with URP 17.3.0 or later.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.LightmapMetadata.URP` holds `DeliverableToneMapFeature`, which renderer assets save by its GUID.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.LightmapMetadata.URP.Editor` provides `IDeliverableLook`, the seam the editor part declares (`PureDeliverableLook` its stand-in), with the install the `#if UNITYTK_URP` block held. The Volume profile's Tonemapping override is now saved inside the profile; before, it was gone once the editor reloaded.
    - The monolith no longer references URP: the layers baseline loses its two entries, and the kernel-bypass baseline 49.
  - **The materials record** (`MaterialRecord`, `MapAbsence`) and `MaterialColor` sit in `M3trik.Unitytk.Contracts`, which the monolith now references, global names kept: two features read them.
  - **Moved names** (`MOVED_NAMES`): `LightmapMetadataApplier` (`ApplySceneLightmaps`, `PROBE_OBJECT_NAME`) is `M3trik.Unitytk.LightmapMetadata.Lightmaps` (`Apply`, `ProbeObjectName`); `DeliverableLook` is the door's, reached through `IDeliverableLook` (`Lightmaps.LookVolumeName`, `ToneMapFeatureName`), its `Uninstall` the `RemoveLook` action; `DeliverableShading.Convert` and `SourceMap` are Apply's own.
  - **Deployer.** A 0.6.0 project's LightmapMetadata files and its root materials files move by `_SPLIT_0_7` rows; the 0.0.15 and 0.4.0 rows name the new paths.
  - **Known break for project asmdefs:** one that references `M3trik.Unitytk` to reach the LightmapMetadata types must reference `M3trik.Unitytk.LightmapMetadata` (or `.Editor`), and one that reaches `MaterialRecord`, `MapAbsence` or `MaterialColor` must reference `M3trik.Unitytk.Contracts`, the assembly they moved to from `M3trik.Unitytk`; a script that called `LightmapMetadataApplier.ApplySceneLightmaps()` calls `M3trik.Unitytk.LightmapMetadata.Lightmaps.Apply()`.

- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. The render-pipeline host: the kernel's runtime half answers what each pipeline spells its own way, with a Built-in default and a URP door; `unity.scene.view` renders the way the active pipeline documents (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Kernel/{Runtime,URP}/`, new; `Kernel/Editor/SceneCapture.cs`).** The base architecture's host for Unity, as Maya and Blender are tentacle's.
  - **The contract.** `IRenderPipelineHost` (`M3trik.Unitytk.Kernel.Runtime`, every platform) answers the blend switch (`SetBlended`), the base-colour property, the unlit shader, whether a shader draws under the pipeline (`Draws`, by its subshaders' `RenderPipeline` tag), and how to render a camera to a texture (`Render`). `BuiltInRenderPipelineHost` is the default.
  - **The registry.** `RenderPipelineHosts.Current` is the host of the pipeline drawing now; null for one no door serves (HDRP). A door registers its host with `RenderPipelineHosts.Register`, from `[RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod]` in a player and `[InitializeOnLoadMethod]` in the editor.
  - **The URP door**, `M3trik.Unitytk.Kernel.URP`, is not auto-referenced and compiles only with URP 17.3.0 or later. It carries `[AlwaysLinkAssembly]`, so stripping keeps its registration. Its blend switch is URP's material inspector's; it renders through `RenderPipeline.SubmitRenderRequest`, the route URP's manual documents.
  - **`unity.scene.view`** renders through the host: `Camera.Render` on Built-in, a render request on URP, Built-in's way for a pipeline no host serves.
  - Features still detect the pipeline themselves; each moves to the host as it splits.
  - Test(s): `RenderPipelineHostsTests` (4, Edit Mode; the package's 35 all pass in DevProject); `test_unity_kernel_live` (2); a URP project's `unity.scene.view` renders its cube through the request (URP accepts it), checked once by hand; `test_deploy_templates`, `test_surface_snapshot`, `test_unity_compile`, `test_editor_catalog`, `test_package`, `test_base_test`; `unity_package.compile` 0 errors.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. Fixed (review of the night's commits): uninstall takes every old copy, every install refuses an editor older than 6000.3, and fourteen smaller defects (`template_deployer.py`, `unity_host.py`, `launcher.py`, `unity_tests.py`, the kernel, the contracts, the AudioEvent feature).**
  - **Uninstall.** On a 0.6.0-layout project, uninstalling a component left half of it: of the two earlier paths a file can sit at, it took only the last one mapped. The controller then stayed without the record it reads, and the project stopped compiling. Every old path now goes.
  - **The floor.** `TemplateDeployer.deploy_package`, so the DCC panels and extapps too, refuses a project older than Unity 6000.3, naming the upgrade. On such an editor the package's URP, Input System and XRI code compiled out silently; only `UnityHost.install` had refused. `UnityHost` now delegates to `TemplateDeployer.require_floor` and `UNITY_FLOOR`.
  - **The host.** `kernel_source` judges an embedded copy current by its content, as the sandbox does, not by its version alone.
  - **The licence.**
    - A keyring entry stored before pythontk kept the username (`keyring_user`) is never sent as `-username`; the warning says how to store it again.
    - `UnityTests.run` signs in as every other batch launch does, and mirrors a project's embedded packages.
  - **C#.**
    - Each record source reads a node by one rule, `UnitytkRecords.TryGetProperty` (ignoring case, refusing an empty value). The stand-in drifted from the carrier, and a fixture now holds both to it.
    - `Settings.ShouldImport` warns once per settings problem, not once per imported node.
    - A real provider that cannot serve fails its need, naming it, rather than letting the stand-in serve.
    - A killed write's `.part` file is swept on the next write.
    - The settings page shows a write the file refuses instead of throwing in `OnGUI`.
    - Both Audio Event actions report progress, so a menu run shows its bar and Cancel. `ReimportModels` says why a second run plans again.
  - **Namespaces.** The new `AudioEvents` actions, the Audio Events menu and the new test classes sit in their assemblies' namespaces before they publish (`M3trik.Unitytk.AudioEvent.AudioEvents`). The moved-names rows point there and at `TemplateDeployer.components`.
  - **Order.** A split feature lists its controller first again.
  - **Snapshot.** The surface snapshot also holds the shaders' and include files' GUIDs, which materials name: all seven are unchanged since 0.6.0.
  - **Known break for project asmdefs:** an assembly definition in a project that references `M3trik.Unitytk` to reach AudioEventController, ShotMetadataController or the ArticulatedRig types must now reference those features' assemblies. Scenes and Assembly-CSharp scripts are unaffected (GUIDs and global names are unchanged).

- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. ArticulatedRig split out of the monolith: a model, runtime, editor and test assembly and two doors, XRI and Input System; the model held to its golden cases under `dotnet test` (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Features/ArticulatedRig/{Model,Runtime,Editor,Tests,XRI,InputSystem}/` and `test/models_dotnet/`, new).** The base architecture's second feature split.
  - **Assemblies.**
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig.Model` (`noEngineReferences`) holds `ArticulationModel`, pythontk's model ported, and the articulation record (`ArticulationRecord.cs` and its generated `ArticulationRecord.g.cs`).
    - The record sits with the model, not the importer, because a player reads it too: the component holds the record and binds it at runtime. `UnitytkRecords`' channel line names `ArticulationRecord`, which `sync_scene_records.py` writes beside.
    - `M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig` holds `ArticulatedRigController`, `ArticulatedRigPointerGrab` and `ArticulationBoundary`. `M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig.Editor` holds `ArticulatedRigImporter`.
    - The components keep their files, GUIDs, serialized fields and global names, each moved with its `.meta`, so saved scenes and project scripts are untouched.
  - **The importer** reads through the carrier (`IRecordSource`), asks `Settings.ShouldImport(assetPath, ArticulatedRigImporter.Channel)`, declares `[assembly: ReadsRecord(UnitytkRecords.Articulation, typeof(ArticulationRecord))]` and reports through `ImportLog`. It reads no import-ordered contract, so its order stays Unity's default. The kernel-bypass baseline shrinks by one.
  - **Doors.**
    - The XR adapter is the XRI door, `M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig.XRI` (was `M3trik.Unitytk.XR`). It is not auto-referenced, and it references the runtime part instead of the monolith.
    - The pointer grabber's Input System code is the Input System door, `M3trik.Unitytk.ArticulatedRig.InputSystem`, behind a seam the runtime part declares: `IPointerInput`, registered with `ArticulatedRigPointerGrab.UsePointer`.
    - The door registers the Input System's mouse as the player loads, where that handler is active. With nothing registered, the legacy Input Manager is read; a touch or a custom pointer registers its own.
    - The monolith no longer references `Unity.InputSystem`. The layers baseline shrinks by three and the prior-art baseline by one.
  - **`dotnet test`.** `test/models_dotnet` compiles the core and every feature's `Model/`. It holds `ArticulationModel` to `ptk.Conformance.cases("articulation")` (pose, world, read, solve, scale), generated by pythontk at test time (`UNITYTK_PYTHON`, else `python`). `TestArticulationModelDotnet` runs it from the suite; the Unity-side conformance run stays.
  - **Removed:** the overdue `[Obsolete]` `ArticulationJson`, and `ArticulationRecord.SUPPORTED_ARTICULATION_VERSION`, `Parse`, `IsSupported`, `Read` and `RefusalMessage` (deprecated 2026-10-20, due 0.5.0, first shipped in 0.4.0 on 2026-10-24: no deprecation window applies: no project uses unitytk).
  - **Moved:** `ArticulationRecord.AddGrabColliders` is now `ArticulatedRigController.AddGrabColliders`, since the record is engine-free; it has a `MOVED_NAMES` row.
  - **Deployer.**
    - A 0.6.0 project's ArticulatedRig files move into the parts by `_SPLIT_0_7` rows. The XR folder and its asmdef move to the XRI door, and the 0.0.15 rows target the new paths.
    - A split feature's files are its parts' and doors' alone. Before, each door file read as moved from a path no release had (`ArticulatedRig/XRI/...`).
  - Test(s):
    - the feature's Edit Mode tests (`ArticulatedRigControllerTests` 5, `ArticulatedRigImporterTests` 4, `ArticulatedRigPointerGrabTests` 2); the package's 30 all pass in DevProject;
    - `test/models_dotnet` (5) and `test/core_dotnet` (41) under `dotnet test`;
    - `test_articulated_rig_runtime` in Unity 6000.3, the XRI class included; its 4 live-export tests env-skip without `ARTICULATED_RIG_LIVE_FBX`;
    - `test_controller_runtime_integration`, `test_surface_snapshot`, `test_deploy_templates`, `test_base_test`, `test_unity_compile`, `test_editor_catalog` and `test_package`;
    - `unity_package.compile` 0 errors, 0 warnings across editor, player and packages;
    - `check_layers`, `check_csharp`, `check_api_surface --semver` (nothing unaccounted) and `sync_scene_records --check` clean. `run_tests.py --post-status` posts the `unity` commit status on HEAD after a full local pass (`test/run_tests.py` `UnityReceipt`, new).** The base architecture's receipt gate. CI has no Unity or licence, so the suite that needs them runs locally, and branch protection requiring the `unity` status before main moves makes that run name the exact commit that merges.
  - Before the run, the flag refuses anything but `--all --strict-env` with no `-k`, on a clean tree.
  - After a pass, it posts `success` through `gh api` (`repos/<owner>/<repo>/statuses/<sha>`, context `unity`), with fixes when `gh` is missing or the post fails.
  - Nothing posts without the flag. Requiring the status in branch protection is the maintainer's switch.
  - Test(s): `UnityReceiptTest` (3), with git and gh mocked.

- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. AudioEvent and ShotMetadata split out of the monolith, over the first contracts and the record carrier feature; their menu items become actions; the records audit (`Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/`: `Contracts/`, `Features/Carrier/`, `Features/AudioEvent/{Runtime,Editor,Tests}/`, `Features/ShotMetadata/{Runtime,Editor,Tests}/`, `Tools/AudioEvent/`, all new; `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs`, `ImportOrder.cs`, `ReadsRecordAttribute.cs`, new).** The base architecture's first feature split.
  - **Assemblies.** Each feature is a runtime and an editor assembly, plus its tests: `M3trik.Unitytk.AudioEvent[.Editor]`, `M3trik.Unitytk.ShotMetadata[.Editor]`.
    - The components keep their files, GUIDs, serialized fields and global names, so saved scenes and prefabs, and project scripts, are untouched. The surface snapshot holds live.
    - Two verifiers that looked the types up as `AudioEventController, M3trik.Unitytk` name the new assemblies.
  - **Contracts.** `ISequenceWindow` (runtime) is the shot windows, which ShotMetadataController implements. AudioEvent's importer reads it with `GetComponent<ISequenceWindow>()`; before, it reflected over the controller's class name.
    - `IRecordSource` and `IModelMedia` (editor) are the records and the embedded media an importer reads.
    - Each ships with a `Pure*` stand-in.
  - **The carrier feature.** `M3trik.Unitytk.Carrier.Editor`'s `CarrierRecords` provides both editor contracts over today's `CarrierImport`/`CarrierFile`. Those stay in the monolith until the last feature splits.
    - `Records.Audit` is new. It lists every record a project's models carry at a version this unitytk does not read, each as an issue naming the file, for the files to re-export before a pin moves.
    - Readers declare their records once: `[assembly: ReadsRecord(key, typeof(<Shape>))]`.
    - The carrier ships with the core, since every importer reads through it.
  - **The import order** is declared once, on the contract (`[ImportRank(0)]`). An importer's `GetPostprocessOrder` is `ImportOrder.Provider` or `.Reader(typeof(ISequenceWindow))`, no longer a bare 0 and 1.
  - **The record reader moves into the core.** `UnitytkRecords` and `UnitytkJson`, global names kept, are in `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs`. Its channel lines name the split importers, which `sync_scene_records.py` follows.
  - **The import gate is one rule**, `SettingsFile.Admits`. The kernel's `Settings.ShouldImport(assetPath, channel)` and today's `ImportGate` both read it.
  - **Actions.** `AudioEvents.FixSceneObjects(ctx)` and `AudioEvents.ReimportModels(paths, ctx)` were the menu's code. The menu, `Tools/AudioEvent/AudioEventMenu.cs` (same paths), runs them through `OpContext.RunMenu`.
    - Their Edit Mode tests run the kernel's three checks: one Ctrl+Z undoes the run, the plan names every change, and a second run plans nothing.
    - The split importers report through `ImportLog`, so the kernel-bypass baseline shrinks by 13.
    - `AudioEventTools` gets a moved-names row (`AudioEvents`).
  - **Removed:** `ShotRecord.section` and `ShotRecord.objects`, which 0.5.0's CHANGELOG had listed as removed (deprecated 2026-10-20, first shipped in 0.4.0 on 2026-10-24: no deprecation window applies: no project uses unitytk).
  - **Test assemblies** compile only where the Test Framework is installed (a `versionDefines` on `com.unity.test-framework`). A project without it, which an embedded deploy creates, failed to compile the kernel's tests.
  - **Deployer.**
    - `Tools/<Feature>/` deploys with its feature.
    - `Features/Carrier/` ships with the core.
    - A 0.6.0 project's files move into the split folders with their GUIDs, by explicit rows (`_SPLIT_0_7`).
    - The test suites that copy scripts by hand now copy what the named files need (`TemplateSources.closure`): the contracts, the kernel, and each contract's real provider.
  - Test(s):
    - the package's Edit Mode tests, 19 in DevProject;
    - `test_audio_events_integration`, `test_shot_metadata_integration`, `test_shots_audio_sidebyside_integration`, `test_clip_name_contract_integration`, `test_embedded_dependencies_integration`, `test_blender_shot_metadata_integration`, `test_render_opacity_e2e` and `test_unity_kernel_live`, all passing with Maya, Blender and Unity 6000.3;
    - `test_deploy_templates`, `test_base_test` and `test_surface_snapshot`;

- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. unitytk's C# is searched live: `CSHARP_SOURCES` tells `ptk.ApiSurface` where the package's C# lives (`unitytk/__init__.py`).** The base architecture's decision 15.
  - `api.search` and `api.describe` return the package's public C# (host `unity`, run through `unity.csharp.exec`). `check_api_surface.py` judges its release delta. pythontk's reader reads the declaration as data, never importing unitytk.
  - `MOVED_NAMES`, new, holds 0.7.0's moved public names: `TEMPLATE_PACKAGE`, `CORE_FOLDERS` and `AudioEventTools`.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. `unity_project.install` moves a project to this version, embedded or pinned from OpenUPM, and plans every file it changes (`unity_host.py`; `template_deployer.py` `package_changes`, new; `operations.json`).**
  - `UnityHost.install(project, source="embedded"|"openupm", ctx)`:
    - `"embedded"`, the default until the package is published, writes each package file that is not as bundled, each missing `.meta`, and an earlier layout's moves.
    - `"openupm"` writes OpenUPM's scoped registry (`package.openupm.com`, scope `com.m3trik`) and this version into `Packages/manifest.json`, keeping every other entry. It deletes the embedded copy, which Unity would resolve over the pin.
  - A dry run lists every file written and deleted. Run again with nothing to change, it plans nothing.
  - It refuses a project whose editor is older than 6000.3.
  - `TemplateDeployer.package_changes(project)`, new, is the deploy's plan.
  - `UnityHost.kernel_source(project)`, new, says how a project gets this kernel: a current embedded copy, a `file:` link to this unitytk's own package folder (DevProject), or an OpenUPM pin on this version. The host refuses every other project, with the install as the fix.
  - Test(s): `test_unity_host` (5 new, 1 rewritten).
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. A batch launch signs in with the licence stored in `ptk.Credentials`, and the licence wall is its own error naming the fix (`launcher.py`, `unity_host.py`; `UnityLicenseError`, new).**
  - The licence lives under `"unity"` (the Unity ID's email and password) and `"unity.serial"`: `ptk.Credentials.set_credential("unity", "<email>", "<password>")`. On CI, `UNITY_USER`, `UNITY_PASSWORD` and `UNITY_SERIAL_KEY`.
  - Batch launches pass `-username`, `-password` and `-serial`. The command line unitytk logs, or puts in an error, masks them.
  - `create_project` raises `UnityLicenseError` at the licence wall. The env-gated suites create projects through `base_test.UnityEnv.create_project`, which takes the wall as an env skip.
  - Test(s): `test_launcher.TestLicensedLaunch` (4), `test_unity_host` (1), `test_base_test` (1); fake credential stores only.

  - A dry run plans the project, the copy into `Assets/` and the launch, and makes nothing.
  - Test(s): `test_launcher` (1), `test_asset_delivery` (2).
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. `DevProject`, the development host, and the Unity Test Framework run (`DevProject/`, `unitytk/unity_tests.py`, `Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/Kernel/Tests/`, all new; `unity_compile.py`).**
  - **`DevProject`** references the package by local path and lists it under `testables`. It carries URP 17.3.0, the Input System 1.18.0, XRI 3.3.1 and the Test Framework 1.6.0, the minimums Unity 6.3 ships. Prototypes go in its `Assets/Sandbox/`, which nothing references.
  - **The repository holds only its source.** Its `Library/` (1.7 GB) would sit in a cloud-synced tree, so the editor never opens it in place.
  - **`UnityTests`, new, works on a copy** in the per-user cache:
    - `working_copy()` mirrors the source into it, without the sandbox and with the manifest's `file:` paths made absolute;
    - the copy keeps its `Library/`, so a run reimports only what changed;
    - `run()` launches Unity's `-runTests` there, without `-quit`, which would end the run before its tests finish;
    - `parse()` reads the NUnit 3 results.
  - **The fast loop compiles the whole matrix.** `unity_package.compile`, when it compiles the package itself, uses the copy as its reference project. The URP, Input System and XRI builds (editor and player, the XR door included) now run there: 0 errors in about 5 s.
  - **The kernel's first Edit Mode tests**, `M3trik.Unitytk.Kernel.Tests`:
    - a menu run is one undo step;
    - a cancel undoes its edits;
    - a dry run plans without changing anything;
    - the edits work with no context;
    - the provider registry serves a test contract's real provider over its stand-in;
    - the settings action plans, writes and then converges.
  - Test(s): `test_unity_tests` (3). The Unity half runs the package's 9 Edit Mode tests in DevProject on 6000.3, all passing.

  - `m3trik/scripts/check_layers.py` holds the package's assembly references to the declared order:
    - down only, never across peer features;
    - an outside package only from a door (`autoReferenced: false`, gated by `defineConstraints`).
  - `check_csharp.py` holds three more rules:
    - editor code above the kernel calls no `Undo`, progress bar or `Debug.Log` directly;
    - each part assembly records its `Prior art:`;
    - every `*.g.cs` is what its generator writes.
  - Today's monolith is frozen in m3trik's baselines: 5 references, 101 bypass calls, 2 prior-art misses. Only new violations fail.
  - A Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook refuses a hand edit to a `*.g.cs`, naming the generator to run.
  - Test(s): `test_generated_files_hook` (4); m3trik's `test_check_layers` and `test_check_csharp`.

Slice 2: the Unity package moves to `Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/`, laid out as the base architecture's parts, with a committed `package.json` (`"unity": "6000.3"`, MIT) and `LICENSE.md` (`Packages/`, new; `template_deployer.py`, `unity_compile.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `MANIFEST.in`).**
  - **Layout.**
    - `Core/` was `Kernel/Core/`; `Kernel/Editor/` keeps its name.
    - Each feature folder moves under `Features/` (`Features/AudioEvent/`, `Features/ArticulatedRig/XR/`...).
    - The monolith assembly stays at the package root and still compiles the features until each splits out.
    - Every file and folder moved with its `.meta`. The surface snapshot holds, live in 6000.3: no GUID or serialized field changed.
  - **The map** for the package is `Packages/AGENTS.md` (with its `CLAUDE.md` stub), beside the folder that ships, so no rule ships to a project.
  - **`package.json` is committed** and deployed as a file: version 0.7.0, `"unity": "6000.3"` (was a generated `2021.3`), `license: MIT`. The deployer no longer generates it.
  - **The wheel** carries the package as `unitytk/templates/` (`[tool.setuptools.package-dir]`; `**` globs need setuptools 62.3). `TemplateDeployer.source_dir()`, new, finds the checkout's `Packages/` folder, else the wheel's copy. It replaces `importlib.resources` and the `TEMPLATE_PACKAGE` constant, which was never a root export. A wheel built from the tree deploys a complete, up-to-date package from an install.

    - each file moves with its `.meta`, so the project keeps its GUIDs;
    - the emptied old folders go;

  - **`Core/AtomicFile.cs`, new, holds the atomic write.** `RunRules.WriteAtomic` forwards to it. The settings file now needs only `Json`, `AtomicFile` and `PersistedStrings`, not the run rules, so a hand-copied set stays small.
  - `PersistedStrings.SettingsFile` is now `SettingsPath`, unreleased, so it no longer reads as the `SettingsFile` class.
  - Paths followed in the same change:
    - `sync_shadow_shaders.py`'s mirror;
    - the `dotnet test` project;
    - the suites' hand-staged sets, which now also carry `Core/AtomicFile.cs`, `PersistedStrings.cs` and `SettingsFile.cs`;
    - the docs' links.
  - Test(s): `test_a_0_6_package_moves_into_features_and_core` (new). 198 tests across the deploy, base, catalog, host, package, compile, surface-snapshot, temp-project, launcher and live kernel suites; `dotnet test` 41; `unity_package.compile` 0 errors; every generator `--check` in sync.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. The surface snapshot: script GUIDs, serialized fields and persisted strings held to a committed record before any file moves (`test/test_surface_snapshot.py`, `test/data/surface_snapshot.json`, `test/data/surface/Surface.prefab` and `Surface.unity`, all new; `templates/Kernel/Core/PersistedStrings.cs`, new).**
  - Two checks need no editor. Each component script's GUID must still belong to a script in the package, so a file has to move with its `.meta`. Every script the committed prefab and scene reference must still resolve.
  - In a Unity editor the test starts, each component must still serialize each recorded field, unless the field names it in `[FormerlySerializedAs]`. The committed prefab and scene must load with no missing script.
  - The surface grows by addition: a new component, field or persisted string fails until it is recorded (`UNITYTK_UPDATE_SNAPSHOT=1`).
  - `PersistedStrings` declares, once, the strings saved files hold:
    - the `data_export` carrier;
    - the `_Media` suffix;
    - the `unitytk.extracted-by:` mark;
    - the `Library/unitytk/` memo;
    - `OnAudioEvent`;
    - the settings file.
    Today's constants now point at it.
  - Seen while building it: Unity 6.3 writes each component's `Assembly::Type` in `m_EditorClassIdentifier`. The script GUID is still what binds a component.
  - Test(s): `test_surface_snapshot`, 3 tests, the Unity half in 6000.3 (8 components; the XR setup joins once a project with XRI runs it).
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. Slice 1, the settings write: a change on disk is read at once, a torn write leaves the last good settings in force, and a save keeps every key it does not know, written atomically (`templates/Kernel/Core/SettingsFile.cs`, `Kernel/Editor/Settings.cs`, both new; `UnitytkSettings.cs`).** Fixes the three settings defects the base architecture names:
  - **Unknown keys were lost on save.** `JsonUtility` wrote only the fields one class declared, so a save dropped any key another version or tool had written.
  - **A torn write turned every channel on.** `File.WriteAllText` was not atomic, and a file that failed to parse fell back to the defaults. The write is now atomic, and a file that fails to parse leaves the last good settings in force, with one warning.
  - **The two-second cache wrote over a newer file.** A toggle saved the cached copy. Each read now follows the file as soon as its size or write time changes, and each write reads the file fresh.
  - The file is found from the project (`Application.dataPath`), not the process's working folder, which a launcher sets to its run folder.
  - `M3trik.Unitytk.Kernel.Settings.Set(key, value, ctx)` is the action that changes a setting. Its dry run lists the write, and a value the file already holds writes nothing.
  - `UnitytkSettings.Key(channel)` names each channel's key.
  - Test(s): `SettingsFileTests` (`dotnet test`: the three defects, each red before); the live kernel test plans, writes and re-plans `audio` in Unity 6000.3, keeping a key it does not know.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. The kernel's action API: `OpContext.RunMenu`, the edits it records for undo, the provider registry and the import log (`templates/Kernel/Editor/OpContext.cs`; new `Provided.cs`, `ProvidesAttribute.cs`, `ImportLog.cs`, `Core/ProviderRule.cs`).** The base architecture's decision 14 in code. These are the pieces a feature's actions and importers use instead of calling Unity's `Undo`, progress bars or `Debug.Log` themselves.
  - `OpContext.RunMenu(title, ctx => ...)` runs an action the way a menu item or window button does:
    - one undo group, named for the item;
    - a progress bar with Cancel;
    - the issues in the Console, each with its asset selected on click.
    - A cancel or a failure undoes the run's scene edits. The Console names the files it wrote, which stay.
  - A live `ShouldChange` on a Unity object records the object for undo first. The `OpContextEdits` extensions (`Created`, `Destroy`, `AddComponent`, `Log`) also work on a null context.
  - An action's need is a contract-typed parameter defaulting to `Provided.One<T>()`. A provider marks itself `[Provides(typeof(T))]`, and a contract's stand-in sets `StandIn = true`.
  - The rule for which provider serves a contract is `ProviderRule`, in the engine-free core: one real provider, else the stand-in; two real providers are an error naming both.
  - `unity.project.doctor` reports each provider problem as a `provider` issue.
  - Importers report through `ImportLog`, whose lines name the asset.
  - Test(s): `test_a_menu_run_is_one_undo_step_and_play_mode_is_an_issue` (live, Unity 6000.3): one undo step undoes a rename and a move, and a cancel undoes its edits. `ProviderRuleTests` (`dotnet test`).
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. Slice 0's last faults: play mode, a stale result, a held lock (`templates/Kernel/Core/RunRules.cs`, `ISceneFiles.cs`, `PureSceneFiles.cs`; `Editor/UnitySceneFiles.cs`).**
  - **Play mode.** A call that opens scenes, or a live run that may change or save them, is refused in play mode. Its `unity.play_mode` issue's fix is the code that exits play mode. Code, reads and plans still run, with the issue as a warning, and nothing is marked changed.
  - **A stale result.** A run folder's earlier `outcome.json` is deleted before the run starts, so a killed run leaves no outcome, never an earlier run's.
  - **A held lock.** A call on a project an editor holds fails through the runner's round trip, saying to ask the person, and starts no editor.
  - `ISceneFiles` gains `Playing()`.
  - Test(s): `RunRulesTests` (36 under `dotnet test`); `test_a_call_on_a_project_an_editor_holds_fails_with_the_fix_and_launches_nothing`; the live test enters play mode, sees the issue, applies its fix and leaves.
- **2026-11-04 -- 0.7.0. The rails: the map carries the placement table, the never list, stop-and-ask and the working loop (`AGENTS.md`), and the package's anatomy section describes the target layout (`templates/README.md`).** The base architecture's rails-first decision (12): code written from now on is built under them. The Unity map rides in the root map until the package moves to `Packages/` (slice 2).
- **2026-11-03 -- 0.6.0. The operation catalogs stay LF on a Windows checkout (`.gitattributes`, new).** `test_editor_catalog` compares `editor_operations.json`'s bytes with Python's `json.dumps` output (the editor's C# writer matches it), and a `core.autocrlf=true` checkout, as on GitHub's Windows runners, turned them CRLF; both catalogs are pinned `text eol=lf`. `template_deployer.py` drops a `pythontk` import nothing used (ruff F401).
  - Test(s): a `core.autocrlf=true` clone checks both catalogs out LF (CRLF before); `test_editor_catalog`; `ruff check unitytk` clean.
- **2026-11-03 -- 0.6.0. Only the kernel declares operations: `[Operation]`, `Effect` and `OperationRegistry` are internal (`templates/Kernel/Editor/OperationAttribute.cs`, `Effect.cs`, `OperationRegistry.cs`).** The base architecture's decision 14: Unity follows the Maya and Blender rule, so the kernel's console, scene view and doctor are the only operations, and a feature's actions are public methods that code reaches through `unity.csharp.exec`. Made internal before 0.6.0 publishes, so the attribute never becomes public surface.
  - Test(s): `unity_package.compile`: editor and player builds of every assembly, 0 errors.
- **2026-11-03 -- 0.6.0 floors pythontk 0.20.0 (`pyproject.toml`).** The `unity` operation host is built on pythontk's operations engine (`BatchOperationHost`, `SessionOperationHost`, `OperationCatalog` / `OperationProvider`), which first ships in 0.20.0; under the 0.17.0 floor an install beside an older pythontk fails on the first `python -m pythontk ops` call.
- **2026-11-03 -- 0.6.0. Fixed (review of slice 0): the kernel's run rules move to the engine-free core and are tested without an editor; no project an editor has open is written or opened; a code run never saves scenes; an interrupted run is `partial` (`templates/Kernel/Core/` new `RunRules`, `ISceneFiles`, `PureSceneFiles`, `IOperationTarget`, `Invocation`, `OperationInterruptedException`, the exceptions moved from `Kernel/Editor/`; `Kernel/Editor/` new `UnitySceneFiles`, `RunFolder`, `CSharpConsole`, `OperationRegistry`, `SessionServer`, `InstanceLock`, `ProjectDoctor`; `Json.cs`; `unity_host.py`; `template_deployer.py`; `__init__.py`).**
  - pythontk's two rules of the day, mirrored:
    - a `code` run never saves scenes (code saves what it saves itself);
    - held work is set whenever a live run changed something, and pythontk's new refusal text is used word for word.
  - `UnityHost`:
    - A path in no project keys to the sandbox, whose kernel refuses it, so a typo starts no second editor. `serve` refuses a session with no project.
    - Before any write or launch, a project an editor has open is refused (Unity's `Temp/UnityLockfile` held, or `Library/EditorInstance.json` naming a running Unity). The message says to ask the person to close it, never to close it yourself.
    - A launch waits up to 30 s for an editor this tooling just stopped to let the project go.
    - The sandbox is redeployed only when its package is not this unitytk's, file for file.
  - Interrupted, compile and number handling:
    - A code run interrupted after a cancel is `partial` (what it changed stays); a cancel at a checkpoint or while compiling stays `cancelled`.
    - A compiler that could not run (missing, crashed, timed out, no temp folder) is a `failed` `CompileErrorException`, like a compile error.
    - An integer reads as `long` (`decimal` past it), so `9007199254740993` keeps its last digit. `UnitytkJson` keeps doubles for the records.
    - An operation's integer argument out of range is refused, never cast.
    - A float is written as the shortest text that reads back the same.
  - The kernel uses the project from `Application.dataPath`, never the process's working folder.
  - Hardening:
    - `WriteAtomic` leaves no `.part` file when the replace fails.
    - The session server looks at its queue about 40 times a second.
    - Its lock wait starts at the first try.
    - A late heartbeat never writes `serving` over `stopped`.
    - A lease with no `until` has lapsed.
    - `Alive` survives a pid it may not query.
    - All scene paths are checked before any opens.
    - POSIX lock: close-on-exec, and no variadic `open`.
    - `Thread.Abort` missing degrades to the caller's kill.
  - Upgrade notes:
    - `_RELOCATED` moves an old copy of the two exceptions, so it never defines a second type.
    - Removing the last component reports that the core and kernel remain.
  - Test(s):
    - `test/core_dotnet`: 28 tests (new `RunRulesTests`, `PureSceneFilesTests`, `JsonTests` for exact integers and float text).
    - `test_unity_host.py`: new `test_a_path_in_no_project_keys_to_the_sandbox_and_a_session_names_its_project`, `test_a_project_an_editor_holds_is_never_written_or_opened`, `test_the_sandbox_is_redeployed_only_when_its_package_is_not_this_unitytks`.
    - New live test `test_unity_kernel_live.py`: `UnityHost` + `SessionOperationHost` + exec, then `scene.view` on a scene the code saved. It skips without an editor.
    - In Unity 6000.3.10f1, launched by the test run: the typo, open-project, held-work, interrupt, compile-error, integer-range and restart checks all passed.
- **2026-11-03 -- `API_INDEX.md`, `API_REGISTRY.md`/`.json` and `API_CHANGES.md` are no longer committed: the API is searched live (`api.search`) (`AGENTS.md`; CI's `api-retirements` job, `publish.yml`).** The retirement-window check is CI's `API retirements on time`. The delta since a release (`m3trik/scripts/check_api_surface.py unitytk --changes` / `--semver`) needs a `v<version>` release tag, which unitytk's publish makes after each upload from 0.6.0 on (`publish.yml` "Tag the release", annotated as push.ps1 tags the cascade's); before a tag exists both report that there is no baseline.
- **2026-11-03 -- The `unity` host keeps one editor per project, the sandbox included, and its C# operations are registered (`unity_host.py` new `UnityHost.instance`, `pyproject.toml` entry point `unitytk_editor`; `templates/Kernel/Editor/RunFolder.cs`, `CSharpConsole.cs`, new `CompileErrorException.cs`).** Found by the end-to-end run over MCP: a call without scenes started the sandbox's editor, and a call naming a scene inside the sandbox started a second one, which Unity refused (a project opens once); the sandbox's key is now its own folder. `editor_operations.json` is registered under `pythontk.operations`, so `unity.csharp.exec`, `unity.scene.view` and `unity.project.doctor` list and run from `python -m pythontk ops` and MCP. `RunFolder` follows pythontk's rule: a code run marks the open scenes changed before it runs (a save the code makes clears it; marked after, an exec that saved every scene left every later call that opened scenes refused), and a snippet that does not compile raises `CompileErrorException`, a `failed` call with nothing changed.
  - Test(s): `test_unity_host.py` new `test_a_call_without_scenes_and_one_naming_a_sandbox_scene_share_one_editor`; verified in Unity 6000.3.10f1 through both MCP servers.
- **2026-11-03 -- New: the editor kernel, slice 0 of the Unity operation protocol (`templates/Kernel/Core/`, assembly `M3trik.Unitytk.Core`, no engine reference; `templates/Kernel/Editor/`, assembly `M3trik.Unitytk.Kernel`; `editor_operations.json`, new; `template_deployer.py` `CORE_FOLDERS`; `templates/UnitytkSettings.cs`, `templates/M3trik.Unitytk.asmdef`).** The editor now answers pythontk's operation calls.
  - An `[Operation]` static method is an operation. `OperationRegistry` finds them through `TypeCache`, binds the JSON arguments to their parameters, and `OpContext` is the context.
  - `RunFolder` answers a pythontk run folder with pythontk's statuses and scene rules.
  - `BatchServer.Run` answers one call per editor (`-executeMethod`).
  - `SessionServer` makes a kept editor serve a pythontk `JobQueue` folder (`-unitytkServe <folder> [-unitytkOwner <pid>]`). It re-arms after each domain reload and ends on the `stop` file, a lapsed lease, or its owner's exit.
  - `Catalog` writes the catalog, as Python writes JSON.
  - The operations:
    - `unity.csharp.exec`, effect `code`. It compiles a snippet with the editor's own Roslyn; `Vars` keeps values between calls; a cancelled snippet is interrupted 2 s after the cancel.
    - `unity.scene.view`, the twin of `maya.scene.view` and `blender.scene.view`.
    - `unity.project.doctor`.
  - The contract types are generated from pythontk's payloads (`m3trik/scripts/sync_operation_contract.py`, new). `UnitytkJson` forwards to the core's `Json`, so a hand-copied set that carries `UnitytkSettings.cs` carries `Kernel/Core/Json.cs` too.
  - `Kernel/` deploys with every selection. Removing the last component now keeps the core, because its kernel serves on its own.
  - Measured on 6000.3.10f1, built-in pipeline:

    | Measure | Result |
    |:--|:--|
    | Launch to serving | 4.4–7.2 s |
    | Warm `csharp.exec` | 0.84–1.23 s (mostly the compiler's start) |
    | A script edit compiling and reloading in place | 3.4–7.4 s |
    | One call per editor | 5.5–11 s |
    | Exit after `stop` | 0.5 s |
    | Exit after the lease lapses | 1.6 s |
    | Exit after the owner is gone | 1.2–1.5 s |
  - Test(s):
    - New `test_editor_catalog.py` (5).
    - New `test/core_dotnet` (`dotnet test`, 8).
    - New `m3trik/test/test_sync_operation_contract.py` (5).
    - `test_deploy_templates.py`: new `test_kernel_folder_is_core`; `test_removing_the_last_component_keeps_the_core` replaces `..._takes_the_package`; the kernel is in its file lists.
    - `base_test.TemplateSources.missing_records` names the core types a staged set reads, and `test_base_test.py` pins it; the flat-copied sets carry `Kernel/Core/Json.cs`.
    - In a real editor:
      - per call: exec, a compile error, a dry-run refusal, a scene saved after an edit, `scene.view` and `doctor`;
      - in a session: exec value and output, `Vars`, a compile error, `scene.view`, `doctor`, a cancel while compiling and of a running loop, a script edited between calls, `stop`, the lease, the owner's exit, and a leftover running job failed at start.
- **2026-11-03 -- Fixed (review): `UnityCompile` resolves `GUID:` references, honours `excludePlatforms`, stops a hung compiler and cancels between assemblies (`unity_compile.py`).** A reference written by GUID (the editor's default) was skipped, so its dependent reported false CS0246 errors; an assembly excluded from the editor still built there; a `csc` that hung blocked forever.
  - Test(s): `test_unity_compile.py` new `test_a_reference_by_guid_resolves_as_one_by_name`, `test_an_assembly_excluded_from_the_editor_builds_for_the_player_alone`, `test_a_cancel_stops_it_between_assemblies` (red first).
- **2026-11-03 -- New: the fast loop, `UnityCompile` and the operation `unity_package.compile`; unitytk registers its operations (`unity_compile.py`, new; `operations.py` + `operations.json`, new; `pyproject.toml`'s `pythontk.operations` entry point; `__init__.py`).** The package's C# is compiled with an installed editor's own compiler (its bundled Roslyn on its bundled .NET runtime) against its own engine assemblies, no editor running: every `.asmdef` under the source is one assembly, built in dependency order, its `versionDefines` and `defineConstraints` honoured, as an editor build and a player build, and with the URP, Input System and XR Interaction Toolkit packages when a reference project an editor imported holds them. Each compiler error is an issue naming its file, line and code. Measured on 6000.3: the 24 template files compile clean in both builds in 2.4 s (an editor's own compile is tens of seconds before the first error). Decision 12 of the base architecture puts this loop before slice 0.
  - Test(s): new `test_unity_compile.py` (8: version symbols, assembly order and nesting, define constraints; with an editor: a clean package in both builds, an error as an issue with file, line and code, unguarded editor code failing the player build alone, an unmet constraint left out, the shipped package).

That entry's Test(s) line now says what ran.

- **2026-11-01 - Fix: one carrier rule on every channel -- the first of the scene's own carriers wins, and a node that is no carrier never counts (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` `CarrierImport.Collect`, `CarrierFile.IsCarrier`; `templates/README.md`).** `Collect`, the user-property path of the lightmaps, handoff, audio, shots, shadows, emissive and articulation importers, had two faults:
  - Of two own carriers in one file it kept the last, where pythontk (`FbxMedia._applied_channels`) and `CarrierFile` keep the first. It now keeps the first own value.
  - It took any node carrying a property of a channel's name, an artist's custom attribute included. It now reads carriers alone, as the materials channel did.
  - `CarrierFile.IsCarrier` now mirrors pythontk's committed `SceneStoreBase.is_carrier` exactly (the name, or the name and digits alone), so Blender's `data_export.001` is no carrier. It had followed an uncommitted pythontk edit.
