    - `test_maya_surface_materials_integration.py` `test_01c`: an artist node's lightmaps and audio, and Blender's dotted copy in FBX 7.4 and 7.7, against the committed rule.
    - All three red first on HEAD's templates (Unity took `Crate_Lightmap.exr` for `Second_Lightmap.exr`, the artist's `Artist_Lightmap.exr`, and the dotted copy's record).
    - Green, fresh batch Unity 6000.3.10f1: `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` 12, `test_audio_events_integration` 4, `test_shot_metadata_integration` 6, `test_shots_audio_sidebyside_integration` 4, `test_shadow_plane_integration` 4, `test_emissive_groups_integration` 4, `test_articulated_rig_runtime` 32 (4 env-skipped: no `ARTICULATED_RIG_LIVE_FBX`), `test_embedded_dependencies_integration` 5, `test_maya_surface_materials_integration` 12. `test_deploy_templates`, `test_launcher`, `test_package`, `test_asset_delivery` and `test_base_test`: 172 passed.
- **2026-10-31 - Fix (review of the morning's change): URP's Autodesk Interactive graphs take the alpha the record states; `CarrierFile` keeps the first own carrier and stops at Objects; the texture roles have a postprocessor of their own (`templates/MayaSurface/MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.cs` `UniversalAutodeskInteractive`, `MayaSurfaceMaps.Of`, new `MayaSurfaceTextureRoles`; `templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` `CarrierFile`, `CarrierImport.Collect` back to its one form; `test/base_test.py` new `CarrierFbx.binary`; `test/test_asset_delivery.py`).**
  - URP's graphs take the alpha from ONE source, never the product glTF states (measured on URP 17's graphs): Transparent takes the colour map's A or `_Opacity`; Masked takes the colour map's A or an `_OcclusionMap` mask. `_UseOpacityMap` was on whenever a base map was bound. So a MASK entry with a roughness map never cut, sampling a white mask, and a BLEND entry at opacity 0.5 drew opaque. The writer now takes the colour map's A where it is the whole alpha, else the factor, and says what is lost.
  - Of two own carriers, `CarrierFile` keeps the first, as pythontk does.
  - The walk ends with the Objects section: a file cut short after Objects keeps its records, and Connections / Takes are never read.
  - The texture roles move to `MayaSurfaceTextureRoles`, so a later change to the material mapping reimports the models alone. Textures reimport once with this change, because their postprocessor set changes.
  - A StingrayPBS's tiling is read off its own `uv_scale` / `uv_offset`, the names the description carries (measured, 6000.3), else `uvTransform`. The importer had read `uvTransform` alone, so the tiling never applied (measured red: 1, 1).
  - A model in a package goes through `FileUtil.GetPhysicalPath`, and a model whose file cannot be found is said rather than read as recordless. The reviewed regression (S2, by reading) did not reproduce: on 6000.3 a model in a local package outside the project read its record at HEAD.

    - a model in a local package outside the project;
    - a tiled StingrayPBS;
    - the openPBRSurface map pinned to the model's own copy.
    - Red first on HEAD's templates: the reader fixtures (HEAD kept the second own carrier), the Stingray tiling (1, 1) and both URP alpha cases. The package case passed at HEAD.
    - Green, fresh batch Unity 6000.3.10f1: `test_maya_surface_materials_integration` 12, `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` 10, `test_embedded_dependencies_integration` 5, `test_shadow_plane_integration` 4 -- 31 passed, 0 env-skipped. `test_deploy_templates`, `test_asset_delivery` and `test_base_test`: 153 passed.

A map the record names that the project does not hold reads as absent, as every reader of the record reads it: matte (`MapAbsence`, roughness 1.0; the importer took 0.5).

- **2026-10-31 -- Fix (second review of the same day's change): a legacy model binds its own copy first, no other model reuses an older release's leftover, extraction survives a failure, MayaSurface roles stay in the model's own folder, and an .m4a digest no longer leaves an event unbound (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` new `IsReusableBy` / `InOwnMediaFolder`, `AudioEvent/AudioEventController.cs` new `UsableDigest`, `MayaSurface/MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.cs`, `templates/README.md`).** Five defects the re-review found in the morning's rules:
  - With no digest, a legacy lone model's unmarked copy was no longer its own, so it bound the first same-named clip in `FindAssets` order, which is not promised. The no-digest bind now takes its own copy, then a usable clip in its own media folder, then one elsewhere.
  - A new model with matching bytes reused an older release's unmarked file beside another FBX, which the README says may be deleted (measured: Gamma bound `Assets/Alpha/door.wav`). `IsReusableBy` is now the one reuse rule: the model's own copy, or a clip that is neither another model's extraction nor an unmarked file beside another FBX.
  - `ExtractBeside` settles file by file: an IO failure keeps that file in `<model>_Media/` and says so, each file is marked as it moves, `ApplyPending` catches per asset, and `SameBytes` compares lengths, then a streamed SHA-256.
  - A same-named texture dropped beside a shared-folder model took the model's role (measured: a loose `ss_normal.png` imported as NormalMap). Roles now match the model's own media folder only.
  - Unity imports `.wav`/`.mp3`/`.ogg`/`.aif`/`.aiff`/`.flac` as AudioClips but not `.m4a` (measured, 6000.3.10f1), and a model whose own clip was an `.m4a` bound nothing. A digest whose own copy is not an AudioClip now reads as absent, so the event binds by name. The record still states the embedded bytes.
  - Test(s): `test_embedded_dependencies_integration.py` new `TestLegacyLoneModelsBindTheirOwn` and the content suite's `.m4a` and crate-bytes checks; `test_maya_surface_materials_integration.py` `test_06` (a loose texture stays Default). Red against `82deb31`: the `.m4a` case, Gamma's reuse and the loose normal map; the legacy-bind case did not reproduce (the search order happened to favour the near clip). Green, fresh batch editors: 95 tests across the seven Unity suites.
- **2026-10-31 - Fix: an extraction decides where it lands by the exact names it writes; a same-named clip is reused only when no extraction rewrites it; a model's own is its own (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` `CarrierImport` new `ExtractBeside`, `IsExtractedMedia`, `InMediaFolders`, `HoldsCurrent` own-only, removed the `RequestEmbeddedFiles` name ledger and `ForeignBeside`; `templates/AudioEvent/AudioEventController.cs` `ResolveClip`; `templates/MayaSurface/MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.cs` `NoteMaps`, `OnPreprocessTexture`; `templates/README.md`).** Review of the same day's change. (1) The guard checked only the names a channel gave, but `ExtractTextures` writes every embedded file -- a material's own map too -- so a lone model still wrote over a library's `wood.png` (GUID kept), and a marked `door.mp3` hid a foreign `door.wav`. A lone model's media now land first in `<model>_Media/` and are imported there; when an exact name that landed names a file beside the model it did not extract, they stay there, else each moves beside it (a new one marked its own, one it extracted before given the new bytes in place, GUID and settings kept). (2) MayaSurface keyed a map's role by where it would land, decided before the redirect: a legacy lone model's re-export imported its normal map as colour. Roles are keyed by model and file name and found wherever that model's media land (`InMediaFolders`). (3) Room C reused room A's own extraction, which A's re-export rewrites in place: C played A's new sound. A same-named clip is reused only when no extraction rewrites it -- never one marked as another model's, or in another model's `<model>_Media/` -- else the model extracts its own. This narrows the decision's "anywhere" (new evidence beats an old decision; flagged to the maintainer). (4) A newer same-named file beside a lone model counted as its own (`HoldsCurrent`, and the no-digest bind): both now require the model's own copy, so a file from before the digests extracts its own into `<model>_Media/` beside a library clip, which keeps its bytes and GUID. A file extracted before 2026-10-31 carries no mark, so the model's next extraction goes into `<model>_Media/`.

- **2026-10-31 - Fix: an extraction never writes over a file the model did not extract; beside a lone model, such a same-named file sends its media into `<model>_Media/` (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` `CarrierImport.MediaFolder`, `RequestEmbeddedFiles`, `ApplyPendingImportSettings`, new `CarrierImport.IsOwnMedia`; `templates/AudioEvent/AudioEventController.cs` `ResolveClip`; `templates/README.md`).** `ModelImporter.ExtractTextures` writes every embedded file in place, keeping the GUID of a file already there: a lone model in a folder holding a library's `door.wav` (or another model's) gave that file its own bytes, so everything bound to it played the new sound and the original was gone -- reachable since the content rule extracts whenever no same-named clip matches (measured red: the library clip's SHA-256 changed, its GUID kept). Which files are its own is state in the `.meta`: each file a model extracts beside itself is marked in its importer's `userData` (`unitytk.extracted-by:<model GUID>`), so its re-export replaces its own copies in place, GUID kept, and every checkout and a rebuilt Library agree. A file extracted before this carries no mark, so the first re-export that would overwrite one extracts into `<model>_Media/` instead. A file written over that no channel names is warned about, never claimed. The audio importer binds an unmatched copy only when it is the model's own (`IsOwnMedia`): an edited own copy stays bound.

- **2026-10-20 - Every record is read through its generated typed reader, the one way (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` new `UnitytkJson`, `UnitytkRecords.Read` / `VersionOf`, `CarrierImport.Read`; the importers of `animation`, `audio`, `emissive_groups`, `lightmaps`, `handoff`, `materials`, `shadows` and `articulation`; generated beside them by m3trik's `sync_scene_records.py`: `AnimationRecord.g.cs`, `AudioRecord.g.cs`, `EmissiveGroupsRecord.g.cs`, `LightmapRecord.g.cs`, `HandoffBlock.g.cs`, `ShadowRecord.g.cs`; `templates/README.md`).** No importer parses a record or spells a version of its own: each reads with `UnitytkRecords.Read(key, json, <Shape>.CurrentVersion, <Shape>.Shape.Payload.Read, ...)` -- the core's one JSON reader (`UnitytkJson`, in every build), the version gate, then the record's shape as pythontk declares it. Five importers read their records with `JsonUtility` under a hand-typed `SUPPORTED_*VERSION`, and two templates each carried a JSON reader of their own.
  - A record that fails its declared shape is refused whole, as the GLB build refuses it: a key it requires left out, a null where it admits none, a nested value or list item that is no object, a value of another type or outside its choices. The warning says why ("... fails its declared shape (Plane: missing required 'canvas') ...") and to re-export. The defaults a hand-written reader filled in for a missing field go with it: a shadow plane's `follow_source` true, `max_stretch` 6, `intensity` 1, rig type `projected` and `unit_scale` 1; a lightmap entry's `uvIndex` 1.
  - `emissive_groups` v2: its version under `version` (v1 stated it as `schema`), read from `EmissiveGroupsRecord`.
  - Audio events are placed on the record's own clock: an event plays `frame / fps` seconds into its clip at the `audio` record's `fps`, not at the imported clip's `frameRate` (the rate Unity sampled the take at); a record stating no usable `fps` is refused. On a Shots + Full Sequence file's sequence clip an event sits at its shot's start plus its own `frame / fps`.
  - The hand-off is read through `HandoffBlock` at `HandoffBlock.CurrentVersion` (no longer `RenderingPolicy.HandoffVersion`); `ReadRecipe(null)` is the viewer's defaults. `ReadProbe(json)` reads the whole record, and `ReadProbe(LightmapRecord.Shape.Payload)` the probe of one already read. `MaterialRecord.Entries(json, where, out refusal)`.
  - The leaner shapes -- a field no reader read is gone from pythontk's declaration, so from the record: a lightmap entry states no `intensity` (the multiplier its texels already carry), a shot no `section` or `objects`, a horizon block no `mapping`, `encoding` or `levels` (the record's version states the one representation and encoding), the emissive record no `resolution`. `LightmappedObject.intensity` is no longer public: it stays serialized and hidden, never set by an import, so `RecordsHash` reads a scene bound before 2026-10-20 as it hashed it and the scene stays bound. A horizon plane marches on the footprint, spans, height scale and bounds alone (`HorizonRefusal(size, spans, heightScale, bounds)`), its `horizonLevels` log2 of the footprint. `recordIntensity` states the record's rule: the plane's opacity never exceeds it.
  - A shadow plane draws at `alpha = shadow x min(its material's opacity, the record's intensity)` -- the standard's rule, the WebXR viewer's (`ShadowPlane.shader` new instanced `_RecordIntensity`, written by `ShadowPlaneController` from `recordIntensity`; 1, uncapped, for a plane with no controller). An FBX plane whose material states its opacity draws as before, its baked keys never above its intensity; one whose material states none (alpha 1) draws at the record's intensity, where it drew at full opacity. The pipeline fallback (HDRP, or the shader missing) applies no per-plane block, the cap included.
  - Breaking: the lightmap controller's per-object entry is `LightmappedObject` (was `LightmapRecord`, now the generated record type's name; Unity serializes it by field, so saved scenes and prefabs keep their data, and `RecordsHash` hashes the same form). Gone, internal or new this cycle: `LightmapManifestJson`, `LightmapPayload`, `ShadowPlanePayload`, `EmissiveGroupsPayload`, `ShotMetaPayload`, the audio importer's payload types, `MaterialRecord.Version`, `CarrierImport.IsCurrent`; and `ArticulationRecord.Rigs` / `Grabs`, obsolete since before 0.1.0, their announced removal.
  - `[Obsolete]`, removed in unitytk 0.5.0 (deprecated 2026-10-20): `ArticulationJson` (use `UnitytkJson`); `ArticulationRecord.SUPPORTED_ARTICULATION_VERSION` (use `ArticulationRecord.CurrentVersion`); `ArticulationRecord.Parse`, `IsSupported`, `Read(payload, where, context)` and `RefusalMessage` (use `UnitytkRecords.Read`); `ShadowPlaneRecord`, `ShadowPlaneAtlas` and `ShadowPlaneHorizon` (use `ShadowRecord.Shape`); `ShotRecord.section` and `objects` (empty for a current import; a project script reading them still compiles); `ShadowPlaneController.horizonMapping` and `horizonEncoding` (read by nothing) and the `HorizonRefusal(encoding, mapping, ...)` overload (use `HorizonRefusal(size, spans, heightScale, bounds)`).
  - Test(s): Roslyn matrix -- core plus each component alone, the embedded-deps selection and everything; editor and player; Built-in and URP -- 40/40 builds, 0 errors, 0 warnings. `test_deploy_templates.py` 60/60: the generated readers pinned with their importers' components, new `test_every_record_is_read_through_its_generated_shape`; with the other pure suites 216/216. `test_lightmap_metadata_integration.py`: its 16 probe cases are whole current records (another version, an entry and a box that fail the shape among them); the recipe fixture is `HandoffRecord.block`'s hand-off of a lightmapped file with a probe (pythontk publishes only the settings a file's content uses: a fixture holding nothing lost its probe level -- red, `probeIntensity` 0.3183099 for 0.25); a hand-off that fails its shape is refused. The shot suites read no section, the horizon fallback cases no encoding or mapping. `test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration.py` new `test_04b_the_record_intensity_caps_the_opacity` (red first: an opacity of 1 under an intensity of 0.8 drew 1.0): rendered, 0.4 under 0.8 draws 0.4 and 1 under 0.8 draws 0.8; the three shadow-plane suites (Maya, Blender, runtime) 27 tests, 0 failures, 0 skips. `test_articulated_rig_runtime.py` `test_06_record_validation_matches_reference` (red: the shape's `FormatException` escaped the model's `ArgumentException` contract): `ArticulationModel`'s mapping constructors refuse a rig that fails the shape with an `ArgumentException`, and the parity cases are record-shaped rigs. Fresh Maya 2025 / Blender 5.1 / Unity 6000.3.10f1 batch runs: the 13 suites on the readers (lightmap, emissive, shot, Blender shot, audio, articulated rig, Maya surface, shadow plane in Maya, Blender and at runtime, embedded dependencies, shots and audio side by side, the clip-name contract) 103 tests, 0 failures, 4 environment skips (`ARTICULATED_RIG_LIVE_FBX` unset); after the leaner shapes, the 9 they touch (shot, Blender shot, lightmap, the three shadow-plane suites, emissive, embedded dependencies, shots and audio side by side) 58 tests, 0 failures, 0 skips. None left a run in `test/temp_tests` (75 runs, 12.79 GB, before and after). `sync_scene_records.py --check`: in sync, 22 records, 8 read by Unity.
- **2026-10-20 - A Unity suite's scratch run goes with the suite (`test/base_test.py` `TempProjects.allocate(prefix, owner)`, new `TempProjects.release`; the 24 suites that allocate; `test/test_temp_projects.py` new `SuiteLifetimeTest`).** Every integration suite kept its run under `test/temp_tests/` on purpose, and only the 3-day age sweep reclaimed any: 75 runs and 12.8 GB on 2026-10-20 (a `lightmap_meta_integ_*` or `maya_surface_integ_*` run is 1.65 GB), which filled O: and truncated a production module to 0 bytes. Allocated with its class, a run is now removed when the class is done -- `addClassCleanup`, so after `tearDownClass` and after a `setUpClass` that raised, pass or fail, a crashed or timed-out batch Unity included, under the runner and pytest alike -- retried while a process still holds part of it. `UNITYTK_PRESERVE=1` keeps it for a post-mortem and says so. The 23 per-suite teardowns that only kept the run, or deleted it each its own way (`FADE_WINDOW_PRESERVE` folds into `UNITYTK_PRESERVE`), are gone; the age sweep still reclaims what a killed, held or preserved run leaves.

Real runs on fresh Maya 2025 / Blender 5.1 / Unity 6000.3.10f1 batch instances -- lightmap_metadata + clip_name_contract, then audio_events -- made 4 run folders and removed every one, passing class or failing: `temp_tests` held 75 entries / 12,785,542,357 bytes before and after.
- **2026-10-20 - unitytk reads the deliverable's records under their current keys and versions alone (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` new `UnitytkRecords`, `MaterialColor`; `LightmapMetadata/LightmapMetadataController.cs`, `MaterialRecord.cs`, `DeliverableShading.cs`; `ShotMetadata/ShotMetadataController.cs`; `AudioEvent/AudioEventController.cs`; `ShadowPlane/ShadowPlaneController.cs`; `EmissiveGroup/EmissiveGroupController.cs`; `ArticulatedRig/ArticulatedRigController.cs`; `MayaSurface/MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.cs`; `templates/README.md`).** pythontk renamed four records' carrier keys -- `shot_metadata` -> `animation` (v1), `audio_manifest` -> `audio` (v2), `lightmap_metadata` -> `lightmaps` (v2: one `unit_scale` for the record, the probe's box `{min, max}`, no direction on a point light), `shadow_metadata` -> `shadows` (v2) -- and every reader now implements today's standard only: a file written before it is re-exported, never read through a branch for its shape.
  - One declaration per key: `UnitytkRecords`, in the core and runtime-visible -- the constant's literal is the key's one spelling, which m3trik's `sync_scene_records.py` reads (and holds to pythontk) off the line naming its importer. Every importer, `ArticulationRecord.Channel` and the settings page read the constant; none spells a key.
  - One version rule, pythontk's `RecordSpec.is_current`: each importer accepts exactly the version pythontk declares (`animation` 1, `audio` 2, `lightmaps` 2, `shadows` 2, `emissive_groups` 2, `articulation` 1, `materials` 1, the `handoff` recipe 3). A record of any other version, or stating none, is not read, with one warning (`UnitytkRecords.Refusal`): re-export the file, or for a newer one update the templates.
  - Gone with the old standard: the lightmaps v1 reading (the probe's own `unit_scale`, the `[[min], [max]]` box -- `ReadProbe` now refuses a probe without the record's `unit_scale`, or a box of another shape), the audio v1 wire string and the `audio_trigger` curve cleanup, the shadows v1 payload, the articulation record read as v1 when it states no version, and the shot-window guard for files written before shots carried their ranges.
  - A material colour is encoded in one place, the core's `MaterialColor.Encode`, for both components that write one: a LightmapMetadata deploy without MayaSurface failed to compile on `MayaSurfaceShaders.Encode`. The records' JSON reader is runtime code (now the core's `UnitytkJson`): a player build failed to compile on `MaterialRecord.cs`, which read with an editor-only one.
  - `[Obsolete]`, removed in unitytk 0.5.0 (deprecated 2026-10-20): `CarrierImport.WarnIfNewer` (use `CarrierImport.Read`), `MayaSurfaceShaders.Encode` (use `MaterialColor.Encode`), `ShotRecord.HasRange` (every current record carries its window).
  - Test(s): Roslyn matrix -- core plus each component alone, the embedded-deps selection and everything; editor and player; Built-in and URP -- 40/40 builds, 0 errors, 0 warnings. `test_deploy_templates.py` 59/59: new `test_every_carrier_key_is_declared_once` (red on the old core: 7 channel lines, none declaring its key) and `test_one_material_colour_encoding_in_the_set` (red first), the four new LightmapMetadata files, a coupling counted in code only; with `test_base_test` and `test_asset_delivery` 148/148. `test_lightmap_metadata_integration.py` new `test_04b_current_record_only`: the v2 record's probe up at (-1, 1.5, -2) m with its box centred at (1, 2, 1) m, (8, 4, 10) m in size; the same record as v1 refused at import -- no controller, the warning to re-export; 14 hand-parser cases on the current shape; `test_05`'s GLB half reads the map the build binds (pythontk strips the FBX's records from the GLB). Fresh Maya 2025 / Blender 5.1 / Unity 6000.3.10f1 batch runs of the 13 suites on these channels: 103 tests, 0 failures, 4 environment skips (`ARTICULATED_RIG_LIVE_FBX` unset). `sync_scene_records.py --check`: in sync, 22 records, 7 read by Unity.

A project now sits directly in its run (`<run>\p`, or `<run>\urp` / `builtin` / `shared`) with its logs beside it: 94 for the material suite. The age sweep reclaims runs of either layout.

In a shared folder a model binds its own embedded audio, not a neighbour's same-named clip (`FoundAsOwn`), and a model that comes to share (or stops sharing) its folder re-extracts into its own media folder on the next import (the memo key carries the media folder). Install / Remove Deliverable Look keep URP's renderer feature list and its index map in step. Deliverable Lit and EmissiveGroupsLit have a `DepthNormalsOnly` pass: under depth priming with SSAO they drew nothing. A recipe switched away from ACESFilmic takes out the pass and Volume an earlier Apply installed. Unity's texture search is project-wide by name, so the second Maya-surface model in a folder bound all 11 slots to the first model's `_Media` copies; the importer now binds the model's own copy first (media folder, then its own folder).

Unity 6000.3.10f1 / URP 17.3: lightmap 9/9, maya_surface 6/6, emissive_groups 4/4, deploy_templates 57.
- **2026-10-19 - Two deliverables in one folder never bind each other's maps (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` new `CarrierImport.MediaFolder`, `MediaFolders`; the lightmap, shadow-plane, Maya-surface and audio lookups read the model's own folder first).** An FBX's embedded media are extracted beside it, or -- when its folder holds another FBX -- into `<model>_Media/` beside it: the WR2026 standard and MOODY_LIT exports both embed a `Room_Lightmap.exr` (different bakes, different atlas packing), and the second imported into a folder bound the first's atlas. A model that comes to share its folder moves its media on its next import; a folder made for a model that embeds nothing is removed again. Derived files (`_Cube`, `_PMREM`) are written beside their source, so they follow. The FBX deliverable now ships the probe's highlight copies (pythontk `MeshConvert.finish_fbx_deliverable`), which the deliverable shading already reads. README: "Where embedded media land".
  - Test(s): `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` new `test_08_two_deliverables_in_one_folder` (two copies of the crate FBX, each embedding a different `Crate_Lightmap.exr`, in one folder: each binds `Assets/Rooms/<model>_Media/Crate_Lightmap.exr`, its own bytes) -- 8/8.
- **2026-10-19 - On URP, a lightmapped deliverable renders as the WebXR viewer renders it (`templates/LightmapMetadata/`: new `DeliverableLit.shader`, `DeliverableToneMap.shader`, `DeliverableCubeUV.hlsl`, `DeliverablePmrem.cs`, `DeliverableShading.cs`; `LightmapMetadataController.cs` `recipe`, `shadeAsDeliverable`, `probePmrem`, `highlightPmrems`, `shaded`, `BindShading`, `LightmapMetadataImporter.ReadRecipe`, `LightmapProbeRecord.highlights`, `LightmapMetadataApplier.ApplyShading`; `M3trik.Unitytk.asmdef` references URP, `UNITYTK_URP` for URP 14.0.9+).** Measured on the WR2026 office deliverables, Unity's own Lit and ACES made 1.5-3x the viewer's light of the same bake, in a different hue. Four causes: Unity takes a lightmap texel as radiance where three.js takes it as irradiance; URP's ACES is a different curve from three.js's ACESFilmic; Lit has none of the viewer's rules for a baked material (the bake as the split-sum irradiance, reflections held to the bake per texel, a normal map relieving the bake); and Unity handed the model the default sky's reflections, not the bake's probe. Apply Scene Lightmaps now converts a model's materials to `unitytk/Deliverable Lit` (assets beside the FBX, maps read as the GLB reads them). Its probe is prefiltered as three.js prefilters it: `DeliverablePmrem` ports `PMREMGenerator` (half-float writes rounded toward zero, as the GPU does) and matches three.js's atlas within 0.03%. Every renderer gets its lighting context per block. The recipe's tone map (`handoff.rendering`, read at import) runs as a URP full-screen pass with URP's own tone mapping off. A probe's `highlights` copies (layer over probe, as the viewer's `addProbe`) light their objects. Result: the four deliverables match the viewer within 1-2 display codes, linear luma 0.997. `shadeAsDeliverable` off puts Unity's materials back; Built-in keeps them, with a warning.
  - Test(s): `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` `test_06_deliverable_shading_core` (PMREM against three.js r169's own atlas, `test/data/pmrem_three_r169_128x64.npz`; `AddProbe`; recipe and highlight parsing; Built-in fallback) and `test_07_deliverable_shading_urp` (conversion, context, tone-map pass and Volume, both shaders compile, switch-off); `base_test.UnityEnv.make_urp` (shared with `test_maya_surface_materials_integration`, which now uses it; the setup gives the scripted renderer URP's post-process resources, without which URP skips post silently, and fails fast on a project root too deep for the editor, which is not long-path aware: past MAX_PATH URP's imports fail and its Autodesk Interactive graph resolves to nothing) and `TempProjects.BASE` from `UNITYTK_TEMP_PROJECTS` (a URP project is ~2 GB).
- **2026-10-19 - A lightmapped renderer on URP's Autodesk Interactive graph gives up its occlusion map too (`templates/LightmapMetadata/LightmapMetadataController.cs` `SetOcclusion`, `OccludedMaterial(r, byStrength)`).** The graph a Maya Stingray PBS material imports as wires `_OcclusionMap` straight into Occlusion (its `_UseAoMap` toggle gates nothing) and has no `_OcclusionStrength`, so `bakeReplacesOcclusion` skipped it and the bake was darkened by the AO map a second time -- measured on the WR2026 office: walls at 0.19 of the lightmap instead of their 0.33 albedo, in a lens-shaped band per tile. A material with the map but no strength now gets a white map in the renderer's block while bound, its own back once unbound; Standard / URP-Lit keep the strength route.
  - Test(s): `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` `test_03_analyze` `occlusion_map_only` (red: `bound=none|unbound=none`; 5 green, 0 env-skipped).
- **2026-10-19 - A stale comment in the audio importer's Step 1 now matches the one-manifest flow (`templates/AudioEvent/AudioEventController.cs`).**
- **2026-10-17 - The audio importer reads one carrier's manifest, and an FBX's embedded files are extracted only when something is missing, once per model version per project (`templates/AudioEvent/AudioEventController.cs` through `CarrierImport.Collect`, new `PendingEntries` / `ClearPending`; `templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` `CarrierImport.RequestEmbeddedFiles(assetPath, names, resolves = null)`, new `RememberRequest`, kept in `Library/unitytk/embedded_requested.txt`).** An assembly's stray `data_export1` holds the events as last exported alone, and the importer bound every carrier's (measured: a stray `door` beside the scene's `footstep`); it now takes the scene's own carrier, else the first met, as every other importer and the GLB applier do. A clip the audio lookup finds elsewhere (an `Assets/Audio` library) no longer counts as missing -- before, the whole FBX was extracted and the clip rebound to the copy beside the model. The asked-versions memo lived one editor session, so each later session's reimport extracted again over the files beside the model, a touched-up clip included (measured); it is now kept in the project's Library. The lightmap, shadow-plane and surface-material callers pass no resolver: a same-named map elsewhere is not their model's.

- **2026-10-13 - The child-sandbox tests hand a child the run's pythontk temp root too (`test/test_run_tests.py` `ChildSettingsSandboxTest._child_env`).** pythontk 0.15.0's `TempArtifacts.root()` reads `PYTHONTK_TEMP_ROOT` before `TEMP`, and the test copied only `TEMP`, so a child's QSettings store resolved in the OUTER run's root and three checks failed in CI (the release publish of 0.2.1 among them); a real run sets both to one root. Test(s): that class (red against the old env, 4 green).
- **2026-10-13 - An asset copied into the Unity project lands whole or not at all (`asset_delivery.py` new `CopyToAssetsDeliverer._place`, through `ptk.FileUtils.atomic_write`).** A copy cut short (a full disk, a sync client holding the file) left a truncated FBX under the asset's own name, over the one Unity had imported.
  - Test(s): `test_asset_delivery.py` `test_a_copy_cut_short_never_replaces_the_asset_unity_reads` (red: `HALF`).
- **2026-10-10 - Horizon shadow planes march into a cache, not every pixel (`templates/ShadowPlane/ShadowPlaneController.cs` `ShadowPlaneCache`, `cacheShadow`; `ShadowPlane.shader` the `SHADOWPLANECACHE` pass, `_CacheRect`, `_Cached`; `ShadowPlaneHorizon.hlsl` regenerated: the centred, true-angle penumbra).** Measured in the WebXR twin, the per-pixel march cost 1.5-6x a PCF shadow map. A plane is marched over its own UVs into its rect of one global R8 atlas (`_ShadowCache`, so the shared material stays instanced) only when the source, the placement or the ground moved in the contact's frame -- every staged plane in ONE command buffer as the frame's first camera renders (Camera.onPreCull, or beginContextRendering in a scriptable pipeline; no component of its own), within `TexelsPerFrame`, a deferred plane holding the placement it was marched at -- and sampled at one fetch a pixel. `ApplyNow` marches at once. `cacheShadow` off marches per pixel. A plane whose controller is switched off stops sampling the cache (its slot can go to another plane), and swapping a plane's `horizonMap` at runtime re-marches its cache (the key counts map changes; a float instance id lost the difference above 2^24).
  - Test(s): `test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration` `test_05b_the_cached_horizon_matches_the_oracle`; the per-pixel pins (`test_05`, `test_08`) turn the cache off.
- **2026-10-10 - An articulated rig whose root joints hang under different nodes is refused (`templates/ArticulatedRig/ArticulatedRigController.cs` `RootsHangApart`).** The controller solved it in the first root's parent's space, posing the other roots wrong; the WebXR page refuses it, and so now do the GLB applier and this controller, with an error saying why.
  - Test(s): `test_articulated_rig_runtime` `test_09b` (the `split_roots` case against the GLB applier) and `test_09g_a_rig_whose_roots_hang_apart_is_refused` -- red before.
- **2026-10-10 - A file with several `data_export` carriers is read from the scene's own, as the GLB reads it (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs` `CarrierImport.Collect`; the lightmap, shadow, shot, emissive-group and articulation importers).** An assembly export carries one `NS:data_export` per referenced module beside the scene's own; each importer kept the LAST carrier it met while the GLB applier took the FIRST, so one export's two deliverables could read different records (measured: a module carrier last in the file gave Unity the module's lightmap manifest). The scene's own carrier wins; a module's is read only where the scene's carries no such channel, the first met -- pythontk `MeshConvert._data_export_carrier`'s rule. Audio events still merge every carrier's.
  - Test(s): `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` `test_05_the_scenes_carrier_wins` (module first and last; Unity and the FBX2glTF GLB both; red before).
- **2026-10-10 - Maya surface materials import as authored: new `MayaSurface/MayaSurfaceMaterialImporter.cs` (channel `maya_surface`, `UnitytkSettings.cs`).** Measured on 6000.3 and 2022.3: Unity built Maya's standardSurface as a white Transparent Standard material with no maps, and dropped aiStandardSurface's metalness and opacity -- while the GLB pipeline's scene sidecar repaired the same content. An `OnPreprocessMaterialDescription` importer (order 100, after Unity's own at -990 to -960) maps the description's values and maps: base colour x weight, metalness, roughness, emission (above 1 kept), normal / metal / roughness / MSAO maps, and the alpha -- a constant opacity below 1 or a textured one blends, the GLB's rule for these shaders. Targets: Standard or Autodesk Interactive (a separate roughness map) on Built-in, Lit or the Autodesk Interactive graphs on URP; HDRP warns once. aiStandardSurface keeps Unity's Arnold mapping, plus metalness and the blend on Built-in. The maps these materials reference are extracted beside the model (normal maps as normal maps, scalar maps linear); a map that cannot bind falls back to a neutral value. A model with no Maya surface material is untouched.
  - Test(s): `test_maya_surface_materials_integration` (5; Built-in and URP, 6000.3 and 2022.3; red against HEAD), `test_deploy_templates` (component lists).
- **2026-10-10 - Shadow planes are placed in the model's frame, fall back per plane, and resolve names as the GLB does (`templates/ShadowPlane/ShadowPlaneController.cs`).**
  - The plane is evaluated in the model root's frame (bound at import), so moving, turning or scaling an instance carries its shadow; it was placed in world space with the ground at world Y (measured: a model lifted 1 m and scaled 1.5 put its plane 0.667 below it).
  - A horizon plane falls back to its silhouette per plane when its block is one the WebXR viewer refuses (`HorizonRefusal`, the page's `horizonSpec` term for term) or its map is missing; the shared material could march another plane's map.
  - Source and contact resolve by pythontk's rules (`ShadowPlaneNames`: exact, else the namespace-stripped leaf, case-sensitive) and an ambiguous one is refused with a warning naming every candidate; a plane record joins every matching renderer.
  - Test(s): `test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration` (`test_06`-`test_11`), `test_shadow_plane_integration` `test_04` (a moved instance) -- red before, green on 6000.3 and 2022.3.
- **2026-10-10 - FBX render effects match the GLB route (`templates/RenderEffects/RenderEffectsController.cs`).** Measured in batch Unity: a lambert fade imported opaque (Built-in Standard `_Mode` 0) rendered the same pixel at opacity 1 and 0.5 and then popped off; a highlight over a black emission map rendered the same at 0 and 1; an unkeyed `opacity` attribute beside keyed visibility got a 29-frame invented fade.
  - A fading object's material is switched to its pipeline's alpha blend (Built-in / Autodesk Interactive Fade, URP Transparent/Alpha, HDRP Transparent, validated through `HDMaterial.ValidateMaterial` when present) for the whole clip, as the GLB's `alphaMode` BLEND; a shader with no known blend mode is warned about.
  - A pulsing highlight's material drops its emission map and the base emission colour that stood for it (the GLB's `_uncover_emissive`), so emission is dim + (bright - dim) x h.
  - A material is changed in place only when this model owns it and every renderer sampling it needs the same; otherwise the renderers needing the change get a sub-asset copy, so an unfaded sharer stays opaque and a remapped project material is never edited. The `_EMISSION` keyword is no longer switched on in place on a material shared with renderers that do not need it.
  - A fade is rebuilt from visibility only in a file written before the proxy transport that keyed an opacity; anywhere else, and where a proxy curve holds one value, the authored visibility keeps stepping.
  - An opacity HELD at a partial value (a shadow plane over a still prop holds intensity x falloff) blends, and a highlight held away from rest drops its emission map, as the GLB now writes both (pythontk `PointerChannel.rest`); visibility is owned by an opacity that fades by the GLB gate's own predicate (`IsFade`, `MeshConvert._is_fade`).
  - Test(s): `test_render_effects_e2e.TestRenderEffectsGlbParity` (03-07: fade materials -- with an opacity held at 0.5, red under the moves-only gate -- external material untouched, emission map dropped, rendered pixels, unkeyed opacity steps) -- red before, green on 2022.3 and 6000.3; URP 17.3 checked by hand.
- **2026-10-10 - Applied lightmaps survive the scene: the controller records what Apply Scene Lightmaps bound and binds it again whenever it is enabled (`templates/LightmapMetadata/LightmapMetadataController.cs`).** Measured on 2022.3 and 6000.3: Unity keeps only the lightmaps it baked itself, so the scene Apply bound came up unlit on reopening (in the same editor session too) and in a player build -- `LightmapSettings.lightmaps` empty, every renderer at index -1. The WebXR deliverable carries its lightmaps in the file; the Unity one now does too.
  - New `LightmapMetadataController.lightmaps` / `bindings` / `boundRecords` (saved with the scene) and `Bind()` / `Unbind()`; the controller is `[ExecuteAlways]`, so it binds in the editor and in a build, after whatever `LightmapSettings` already holds (a Unity bake keeps its slots). A controller whose records changed since Apply (a re-imported model) binds nothing and says so.
  - Apply takes the previous bindings down first, so a renderer the records no longer name keeps no stale map, and it removes a probe it put up under a model that has lost its controller (re-imported from an export with no lightmaps).
  - Parity with the GLB applier: a bound renderer gives up its material's occlusion map (`_OcclusionStrength` 0 per renderer -- the bake already holds the occlusion; `bakeReplacesOcclusion` off keeps it, and the renderer's place in URP's SRP Batcher), and a mesh with no uv2 is left unbound and named rather than bound through its texture UVs.
  - Apply warns when nothing binds, and names every realtime or mixed light that reaches the lightmapped renderers (the WebXR preview switches its key light off on a baked model). The README gains "The WebXR preview's lighting recipe, in Unity".
  - Test(s): `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` `test_03_analyze` (f): a reopened scene binds again, a renamed record unbinds on Apply, changed records bind nothing; (g) the occlusion override and the uv2 check.
- **2026-10-05 - Every mayapy child of a test run is sandboxed (`test/run_tests.py`, `test/conftest.py`; test only).**
  - The run sets the presets root in its environment, unless one is already chosen.
  - A run-scoped user site's `usercustomize` (via `PYTHONUSERBASE`) applies uitk's QSettings redirect, and the presets redirect only where no root is set, so a suite's own choice is kept. It puts the real user site back first, and loads uitk from the run's own checkout when the child's path has none (seven suites hand mayapy mayatk + pythontk only).
  - The children had read the developer's `%LOCALAPPDATA%\uitk` and could reach their registry.
  - Test(s): `test_run_tests.ChildSettingsSandboxTest` (incl. `test_a_child_whose_uitk_does_not_import_is_sandboxed_all_the_same`, `test_a_presets_root_the_suite_chose_is_kept`).
- **2026-10-05 - The FBX alone is the hand-off: the importers extract its embedded files beside it (`templates/UnitytkSettings.cs`, `templates/LightmapMetadata/LightmapMetadataController.cs`, `templates/ShadowPlane/ShadowPlaneController.cs`, `templates/AudioEvent/AudioEventController.cs`, `templates/README.md`).** An FBX now carries every file its records name (pythontk `FbxMedia.embed_dependencies`: the lightmaps, the reflection probe, the shadow silhouette and horizon map, the audio clips), where Unity needed each one copied in beside the model by hand. The lightmap, shadow-plane and audio importers ask for the extraction (`CarrierImport.RequestEmbeddedFiles(assetPath, names)`) when a named file is missing beside the model or older than it, at most once per model version (path, mtime, size) per session, with one reimport; `ModelImporter.ExtractTextures` answers `true` for an FBX with nothing embedded, so a folder-state guard (`FolderState`) reimports only when the extraction changed the folder and cannot loop. Lookups search beside the model first, then the project by name, so the hand-copied layout still works. `LightmapMetadataController` gains `modelGuid` and `MapNames()`, and its texture caches are keyed per model. The README now says only the FBX needs copying into the project. Known limit: staleness is judged by mtime, so an FBX copied in with a timestamp older than its extracted maps (a backup restore that keeps mtimes) keeps the old maps. Test(s): `test_embedded_dependencies_integration` (`test_01_maya_export_embeds_every_named_file`, `test_02_unity_resolves_every_map_from_the_fbx_alone`), run under `--strict-env` in batch Unity 2022.3 and 6000.3.
- **2026-10-05 - `CopyToAssetsDeliverer` embeds the records' files before it copies the FBX in (`asset_delivery.py`).** The hand-off copied the FBX alone, so an export sent through a bridge reached `Assets/` without the maps its records name. It now embeds them into a scratch copy outside `Assets/` (`ptk.FbxMedia.embed_dependencies`, searching the bridge's `lightmap_search_dirs()`), warns for a map found nowhere and still ships the FBX, and copies that file in. An embed pass that fails for any reason (a truncated FBX included) never fails the send: the FBX ships as exported, with a warning. Test(s): `test_asset_delivery.SelfContainedDeliveryTest` (`test_an_embed_pass_that_fails_ships_the_fbx_as_exported`, `test_a_truncated_fbx_ships_as_it_is`, `test_the_fbx_carries_every_file_its_records_name`, `test_a_bridge_without_a_host_answer_looks_beside_the_source`, `test_a_map_found_nowhere_is_warned_and_the_fbx_still_ships`).
- **2026-10-05 - A Blender-made shadow plane follows its source in Unity (`unitytk/templates/ShadowPlane/ShadowPlaneController.cs`).** Blender's FBX exporter turns only the root nodes upright, so a blendertk ShadowRig plane and its contact arrive in their own Z-up local frames; `ApplyNow()` wrote a yaw-only world rotation onto a quad lying in its local XY, standing it on edge (measured 4.19 m from its baked keys), and read the contact's +Y as the horizon frame's up, so `_AxisB` pointed straight down and the horizon plane drew nothing. The controller now reads the quad's flat axis off its mesh: a Z-up quad is turned (`ZUpPlaneAxes`) and stretched along its local Y, and a Z-up contact's frame is read in the FBX axes its record is written in (`ZUpFrameAxes`); a Maya (Y-up) quad keeps its rotation, and its canvas is fixed in the next entry. Test(s): `test_blender_shadow_plane_integration` (`test_05_point_plane_follows_its_source`, `test_07_horizon_frame_is_the_contact_frame`, `test_08_horizon_pixels_match_the_map`).
- **2026-10-05 - A Maya shadow plane whose silhouette is lopsided across the light lands on its baked keys in Unity (`unitytk/templates/ShadowPlane/ShadowPlaneController.cs`).** The canvas's across fractions are measured along the DCC quad's own +X, which the FBX import's handedness mirror turns into the quad's local -X, while the runtime model offsets along the heading's +X: a Maya (Y-up) plane's canvas landed on the wrong side of the bearing, off by |w0+w1| times the shadow's width (measured 0.14-0.25 m on an L-shaped crate). Every existing fixture was a box or a cylinder, whose canvas is symmetric, so nothing saw it. `Place()` now reads a Y-up quad's canvas mirrored across the bearing; a Z-up (Blender) quad's axes already agree. Test(s): `test_shadow_plane_integration.test_01_maya_export` (asserts the fixture's canvas is lopsided) and `test_04_runtime_placement_matches_baked_keys` (now with a turned L-shaped crate).
- **2026-10-05 - Suites that carry a Blender-made FBX through Unity (`test/test_blender_shadow_plane_integration.py`, `test/test_blender_shot_metadata_integration.py`, `test/test_blender_render_effects_e2e.py`, `test/base_test.py`).** Every integration suite drove mayapy, so nothing proved what blendertk writes. `base_test.BlenderEnv` launches a fresh background Blender through blendertk's own `BlenderConnection` (env-skips without one); the three suites write their FBXs with blendertk's Scene Exporter and Unity Bridge and check them in batch Unity: shadow placement and horizon pixels, shot clips in every Animation Clips mode, render-effect channels plus keyed visibility. The Unity Bridge rows pass since blendertk's bridge bakes animation (2026-10-05). Test(s): `test_base_test.BlenderEnvTest`, the three suites.
- **2026-10-05 - The articulated rig binds its joints and grab parts by the GLB applier's rules (`templates/ArticulatedRig/ArticulatedRigController.cs`).** `Resolve` took the first transform carrying a name, case-insensitively. So an assembly repeating a part name put a second part's grab on the first, a namesake differing only by case (`Elbow`) could take a joint, two namespaced instances bound to whichever came first, and a namespaced export of a rig the record names bare bound nothing. It now ports pythontk's `MeshConvert._bind_articulated_rigs`: a joint binds to the one transform of its exact (case-sensitive) name, else by namespace-stripped leaf; a grab part to the one of its name under its own joint; a name matching several is refused with a logged error (an ambiguous joint leaves its rig unbound, an ambiguous part ungrabbable). Test(s): `test_articulated_rig_runtime` `test_09b_binding_matches_the_glb_applier` (Unity's binding equals the GLB applier's on the same hierarchies), `test_09c`-`test_09f`.
- **2026-10-05 - A Shots + Full Sequence FBX's whole-timeline clip plays every shot's audio events (`templates/AudioEvent/AudioEventController.cs`).** The manifest scopes each event to its shot's clip and the importer injected an event only into the clip it names, so `Take 001` played silent. The importer now runs after the shot-metadata importer (`GetPostprocessOrder` 1) and places each shot event on `ShotMetadataController.sequenceClip` at `(shot.start - sequenceFirstFrame + frame) / fps`, read through `TryGetSequenceWindow` by name so the audio component still compiles deployed alone; an event the manifest scoped into two contiguous shots lands once. Test(s): `test_shots_audio_sidebyside_integration` `test_04_sequence_clip_plays_every_shot_event` (the fixture's shots now share frame 50, with an event on it).
- **2026-10-04 - 0.1.0, and the `pythontk` floor rises to 0.13.0 (`unitytk/__init__.py`, `pyproject.toml`).** The work below sat at 0.0.15, the version already on PyPI, so `publish.yml`'s version gate would fail its push. A minor, not 0.0.16: the release deprecates (the flat deploy names; C#'s `ArticulationRecord.Rigs` / `Grabs`), which CODE_STANDARD section 5 makes a minor bump. 0.13.0 is the pythontk this release ships and was tested with: the suite holds the templates to its `Conformance` cases and to its articulation solve's halving step, both new in it. The hard minimum rises from 0.8.84 to 0.11.3, because `TemplateDeployer` now builds its deprecation notices at import through `Deprecation.values(..., since=)`.
- **2026-10-04 - A bundle missing a `.meta` is refused before a deploy writes anything (`template_deployer.py`, `test/test_deploy_templates.py`).** `deploy` wrote the template and only then refused for its missing `.meta`, which left a script Unity would mint a random GUID for. `deploy_package` refused on that file after it had moved the old layout and written every file before it, and before `package.json`. A bad bundle therefore left about 35 files and no `package.json`: `status()` read that as not installed, and `uninstall_package` refused to delete the folder.
  - New `TemplateDeployer._require_metas` checks, before the first write, every `.meta` a deploy may write: the selection's files, the files a relocation moves, and the folders that hold either.
  - `deploy` checks its file's `.meta` (the bundle's, or one already at the output) before it opens the output.
  - A flat-layout file that the relocation moves with no `.meta` (a 0.0.15 package Unity never opened) now takes its committed one, as a fresh deploy would. A partial update used to leave the files it did not select for Unity to mint random GUIDs for, and those stuck.
  - Tests: `test_a_bundle_entry_without_a_meta_refuses_to_deploy` now patches the bundle. A missing file or folder `.meta` creates no package, `deploy` leaves no content file, and a refused update leaves a flat-layout package byte-identical. `test_an_old_file_moved_without_a_meta_takes_the_committed_one` is new. Both were red before the fix; `test_deploy_templates` 54/54.
- **2026-10-04 - `TemplateDeployer.deploy` takes a pre-move flat name again, with a deprecation (`template_deployer.py`, `test/test_deploy_templates.py`).** The move into component folders (2026-10-03) made a name such as `"RenderEffectsController.cs"` raise `FileNotFoundError`. Each name in `_LEGACY_LAYOUT` now resolves to its folder path through `ptk.Deprecation.values` (`remove_in="0.2.0"`, `since="2026-10-04"`): the call deploys that template with its GUID, and the warning names the new path. Deploy into the component folder too: a copy written at the old flat path compiles twice beside the moved one, until the next Install / Update removes it.
  - No alias reaches a shader include. A hand-written shader that `#include`s `Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/EmissiveGroups.hlsl` (or `ShadowPlaneHorizon.hlsl`) by its pre-move path must change it to `.../EmissiveGroup/EmissiveGroups.hlsl` (`.../ShadowPlane/ShadowPlaneHorizon.hlsl`). A Shader Graph Custom Function node in File mode references the file by GUID, so it survives the move.
  - Test: `test_deploy_still_takes_a_pre_move_name_with_a_deprecation`, red before the fix.
- **2026-10-04 - A joint whose `t` or `q` is cut short is refused with a warning again (`templates/ArticulatedRig/ArticulatedRigController.cs`, `test/test_articulated_rig_runtime.py`).** The generated record reader (2026-10-03) hands `t` / `q` over as `double[]`, so a short one threw `IndexOutOfRangeException`. That escaped `Resolve`'s `catch (ArgumentException)`, and importing such a model threw from `OnPostprocessModel`. The old `List<object>` reader threw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, which is an `ArgumentException`.
  - `ArticulationModel` now refuses a `t` with fewer than 3 values, or a `q` with fewer than 4, as an `ArgumentException`, its documented contract. `Resolve` warns that the rig is invalid and binds nothing. An empty `t` / `q` still reads as the rest default, as in pythontk.
  - pythontk has no such check (a short `q` is an `IndexError` there), so the case is the port's own contract, not one of `_invalid_rigs`.
  - Test: `test_16c_controller_refuses_a_joint_cut_short`, red before the fix (both records threw). `TestArticulatedRigRuntime`: 21 passed, 4 env-skipped, on Unity 6000.3.10f1 and 2022.3.40f1.
- **2026-10-04 - The shadow-plane runtime suite loads on a pythontk without `Conformance` (`test/test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration.py`; test only).** It built `ptk.Conformance.cases(...)` at import, and `Conformance` is new in pythontk 0.13.0. On an older pythontk the module failed to load: a `[FAIL]`, where a machine without Unity reports environment skips. The two Unity tests that read the cases now build them on first use (`_conformance()`, cached). Test: `TestModuleImport.test_imports_on_a_pythontk_without_conformance` runs the module with `ptk.Conformance` stubbed out, red before the fix. The module: 7 passed on Unity 6000.3.10f1.
- **2026-10-04 - The integration suites' mayapy scripts import mayatk through its root, and three stale notes are corrected (`test/test_*_integration.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`; test only).** Ten imports named a private mayatk module, for `AudioUtils`, `AudioClips`, `ShotStore`, `UvUtils`, `CoreUtils` and `SceneExporter`. They now import from `mayatk`, and a fresh mayapy resolved each root name to the same class. `ShotManifest` keeps its deep import, because mayatk's root does not export it. The notes:
  - `test_shot_metadata_integration` said the importer cuts each shot from the whole-timeline take. The fix is mayatk's take split, which now resamples every curve.
  - `test_shots_audio_sidebyside_integration` said ASCII FBX where it writes binary.
  - The bullets below counted eleven mayapy scripts where twelve changed.
- **2026-10-04 - A hand grab out of reach no longer makes the articulated rig thrash (`templates/ArticulatedRig/ArticulatedRigController.cs`).** `Solve` now takes pythontk's new step (`Halvings = 6`): a step that would leave the held point further from its target is halved until it helps, so past the reach the arm settles where it comes nearest. It used to flail from frame to frame, joints jumping 12-25 units on a 0.46 step of the target, warm-started as a grab is. `test_articulated_rig_runtime`: 25 passed (4 env-skipped), the conformance solves and the hand grab included.
- **2026-10-04 - Shot clips in Unity are exact, and each shot keeps its window (`templates/ShotMetadata/ShotMetadataController.cs`, `test/test_shot_metadata_integration.py`).** Unity builds a Maya FBX's shot clips from its takes, and Maya's take split dropped every curve with no key inside a shot. Measured in Unity 6000.3, before mayatk's fix: shot A01 (frames 1-50) imported 40 frames long with two of its three nodes frozen, and A02 imported as a clip with no curves at all. The GLB never had this, because its clips are cut from the whole timeline.
  - The fix is on the DCC side: mayatk's take split now resamples every curve, so each take is an exact slice. With stock import settings, both clips now match Maya on every frame (300 samples across 2 shots x 3 nodes).
  - `ShotRecord` now carries `start` / `end`, the shot's frame window. `HasRange` says whether a record has one: only a record from a file written before the window existed reads 0 / 0, and a one-frame shot keeps its window.
  - `ShotMetadataController` gains `fps`, `clipMode`, `sequenceClip` and `sequenceFirstFrame`. `sequenceClip` is the clip cut over the whole-timeline take, the one take no shot names.
  - New: `TryGetSequenceWindow(clip, out start, out end)` gives a shot's window in seconds inside that clip. A Full Sequence file has no per-shot clips, so this is how it is played shot by shot.
  - The importer warns when a record names a clip the file does not carry (outside Full Sequence). That happens when an FBX is written without its shot takes. The warning names the sequence clip to play the shot from.
  - Test: `test_shot_metadata_integration` imports three FBX files:
    - one written through `FbxUtils.apply_takes_from_node` + `cmds.file`, with a node keyed only inside A01;
    - the Scene Exporter's Shots Only export;
    - the Scene Exporter's Full Sequence Only export.

    It checks every frame of every shot clip against Maya, each mode's clip list (Shots Only ships no `Take 001`), and the new fields. All 5 tests are green on 6000.3.10f1; they were red against the old templates.
- **2026-10-04 - Every Unity integration suite exports binary FBX, and no generated mayapy script exits through Maya's teardown (`test/test_*`; test only).**
  - Unity 6000.3's ASCII FBX reader drops the whole scene, or truncates the string, once a `data_export` channel passes about 6 KB. A 7 KB value arrived as 7 characters, and a 20 KB one as a prefab holding only its root. Binary carried 300 KB intact. The handoff record alone is about 7 KB.
  - So `test_shot_metadata_integration`, `test_audio_events_integration`, `test_clip_name_contract_integration` and `test_shots_audio_sidebyside_integration` imported an empty scene and failed. They now write binary, as every production writer does. `test_shared_material_standalone` stays ASCII: it reads the file's text and ships no carrier.
  - Twelve generated mayapy scripts ended in `maya.standalone.uninitialize()`, and Maya's static destructors fault there at DLL detach. Each run filed a crash dump and an `untitled[Recovered].ma` in `%TEMP%`. The scripts now end with `ptk.ProcessExit.hard_exit(0)`, as `test_lightmap_metadata_integration`'s does too.
- **2026-10-04 - The deploy tests expect the articulated rig's generated record (`test/test_deploy_templates.py`; test only).** `ArticulatedRig/ArticulationRecord.g.cs` (2026-10-03) ships with its component, and four tests still pinned the file list from before it. Three now list it. The fourth, an unmoved 0.0.15 deployment, now expects what such a deployment really reports: every file it shipped at its old path, and the files added since (derived from the components, not spelled out) missing, so that component reads partly deployed. Both warnings point at Install / Update.
- **2026-10-04 - Apply Scene Lightmaps puts up the bake's reflection probe (`templates/LightmapMetadata/LightmapMetadataController.cs`, `templates/README.md`, `test/test_lightmap_metadata_integration.py`).** mayatk's Lightmap Baker now captures the room it lit as an equirectangular HDR (`<scene>_Probe.exr`), and the `lightmap_metadata` manifest carries it as `probe: {map, position, box, unit_scale}`. A lightmap holds no reflections, and a baked metal shows nothing else.
  - The importer reads the probe into the new `LightmapMetadataController.probe` (`LightmapProbeRecord`). JsonUtility cannot hold the nested `box`, so `LightmapMetadataImporter.ReadProbe` reads the probe by hand. A re-export without a probe clears it.
  - The applier puts up one `Baked Reflection Probe` child of each model root that has a probe: a Custom `ReflectionProbe` at the capture point (X mirrored, in metres) with intensity 1, importance 1 and blend distance 0. It is box-projected onto the mirrored, scaled box; an open scene gets no projection and a box that covers the model. A re-run updates the probe in place, and it is removed when the record has no probe. Apply warns once per run about a rotated root, because probes are world-aligned.
  - Measured on Unity 6000.3 and 2022.3:
    - `ReflectionProbe.center` / `size` are world-space; the transform's scale and rotation never reach them. The box is therefore taken through the root's transform.
    - Unity's lat-long cube import reads +Z at the image centre, so with the FBX X mirror the bake's panorama is a quarter turn off. The applier writes the turned copy `<stem>_Cube.exr` beside it and lets the TextureImporter make the cube (Specular convolution, trilinear, sRGB off, BC6H / ASTC-HDR). The copy is regenerated only when the panorama's content changes.
    - A Gamma project's importer gamma-encodes the EXR (exactly `LinearToGammaSpace`). The copy is therefore written back linear.
  - Lightmap texture lookup and import pinning are shared with the probe (`FindTextureAsset`, `Pin`); lightmap binding is unchanged.
  - Test: `test_lightmap_metadata_integration` gains `test_04_reflection_probe`. Maya records a synthetic probe with a coloured cap per axis, and the test checks:
    - the parsed record, placement, box, cube import settings, and each Maya axis's colour on the face the mirror puts it on (+X on NegativeX, +Z on PositiveZ, ...);
    - that a re-run neither duplicates the probe nor regenerates the cube, and that a moved and non-uniformly scaled root carries the probe and its box;
    - the single rotation warning, regeneration on a changed panorama, and removal after re-importing an FBX exported without a probe;
    - the parser on whitespace, exponents, a null or missing box, NaN and malformed input.

    It is green on Unity 6000.3.10f1 and 2022.3.40f1, and the template compiles clean with Roslyn against both. The Maya half now exports binary FBX: Unity 6000.3 empties or truncates an ASCII FBX's data_export string past about 6 KB. It also exits through `ptk.ProcessExit.hard_exit` like its twelve siblings: `maya.standalone.uninitialize()` faulted at DLL detach and left a crash dump and an `untitled[Recovered].ma` in %TEMP% each run.
- **2026-10-03 - The highlight now rides between its two colours, as the GLB's does; `RenderEffectsController.highlightColorDim` is new (`templates/RenderEffects/RenderEffectsController.cs`, `test/test_render_effects_e2e.py`).** The controller added `highlightColor * highlight` to the emission and never read the dim colour. A pulse with a non-black dim colour (the Render Effects colour ramp) therefore read dim-to-bright in WebXR but black-to-bright in Unity.
  - The controller now adds `lerp(highlightColorDim, highlightColor, highlight)`, which is the GLB's `base + dim + (bright - dim) * s`.
  - The importer seeds `highlightColorDim` from the FBX property beside `highlightColor`.
  - Black, the default dim colour, keeps the old look.
  - Test: `test_render_effects_e2e`. It checks that the FBX carries the property, that the controller is seeded with it, and that the controller adds the dim colour at highlight 0 and the bright one at 1. Both templates compile clean with Roslyn against Unity 6000.3 and 2022.3.
- **2026-10-03 - Every Unity verifier test skips at the batch-licence wall instead of failing: `UnityEnv.require_results` (`test/base_test.py`; test only).** Six suites asserted that their results file existed (`test_render_effects_e2e`, `test_render_opacity_e2e`, `test_render_opacity_integration`, `test_render_opacity_controller`, `test_fade_window_preservation`, `test_multi_section_audio_integration`), so on a machine signed out of the Hub they failed where the others skip. The helper is now the one copy of the check:
  - results present: nothing to do;
  - an unlicensed editor (`UnityLauncher.is_license_failure`): an environment skip;
  - a clean run that wrote nothing: a failure.

  The five suites that inlined it correctly now call it too. Batch project creation that fails skips the same way in those six (`UnityEnv.project_creation_reason`). Test: `test_base_test` `test_require_results_skips_unlicensed_and_fails_a_clean_run`.

- **2026-10-03 - Articulated rigs read their record through generated types (`templates/ArticulatedRig/ArticulationRecord.g.cs` new, `ArticulatedRigController.cs`, `test/test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration.py`).**
  - `m3trik/scripts/sync_scene_records.py` generates `ArticulationRecord.Shape` (`Payload`, `Rig`, `Joint`, `Channel`, `Grab`) from pythontk's declaration, with a fixed GUID.
  - `ArticulationModel` builds from `ArticulationRecord.Shape.Rig`, and the dictionary constructor reads through it.
  - `ArticulationRecord.Read(payload, where, context)` turns a malformed number into a warning instead of an import exception.
  - `ArticulationRecord.Rigs` and `Grabs` are `[Obsolete]`: read `.rigs` from `ArticulationRecord.Read` instead.
  - The shadow-plane runtime test also holds the C# model to pythontk's `ShadowConformance` cases (test_02a).

  Docs: `templates/README.md`, *Extending the package: its anatomy*.

- **2026-10-03 - The C# templates ship fixed GUIDs, then move into one folder per component (`templates/**/*.meta` new; `templates/ArticulatedRig/` (with `XR/`, was `ArticulatedRigXR/`), `AudioEvent/`, `EmissiveGroup/`, `LightmapMetadata/`, `RenderEffects/`, `ShadowPlane/`, `ShotMetadata/`; `template_deployer.py`, `templates/README.md`, `pyproject.toml`, `MANIFEST.in`; `test/test_deploy_templates.py`, `test/base_test.py` `TemplateSources` new, `test/test_base_test.py`, `test/test_articulated_rig_runtime.py`, five hand-staging suites).** A scene or prefab names a script by the GUID in its `.meta`, and the deployer shipped none, so every project minted its own GUIDs for the same scripts: a prefab carried between two projects showed "missing script", and moving a template would have minted new ones. Every bundled file and folder now carries a committed `.meta` -- a fixed GUID, in exactly the importer block Unity writes for its type (measured on 2022.3.40f1 and 6000.3.10f1), LF -- shipped in the wheel and the sdist. `deploy` / `deploy_package` write a bundled `.meta` only where the project has none: one already there is the project's own (its scenes reference that GUID) and is never overwritten. A bundled file without a `.meta` refuses to deploy (`FileNotFoundError`) rather than let Unity mint one, and a partial uninstall takes each file's `.meta` and every emptied folder with it. Then the move, which the GUIDs make safe: the root holds the kernel (`UnitytkSettings.cs`, the asmdef, the catalog README: the `core` component) and each top-level folder is one optional component keyed off its name, replacing the file-name-stem rule; the bundle scan recurses. The component keys and labels are unchanged (`articulated_rig`, `audio_event`, `emissive_group`, `lightmap_metadata`, `render_effects`, `shadow_plane`, `shot_metadata`), because the panels store them. A package deployed by 0.0.15 or earlier is moved into the folders by the next `deploy_package`, whole, each old file with its `.meta` (Unity refuses to compile an old copy of a class left beside the new one). The old-to-new table, `TemplateDeployer._LEGACY_LAYOUT`, is explicit and goes once no pre-move deployment remains; until then `status()` reports a package not yet moved (`legacy`, never `up_to_date`) and `uninstall_package` finds its components at their old paths. Checked: `test_deploy_templates` 52/52, red first (8 of the 9 GUID tests, then 22 layout and relocation tests); `test_articulated_rig_runtime` on 6000.3.10f1, 21 passed and 4 environment skips (no `ARTICULATED_RIG_LIVE_FBX`), where the new `test_01b` reads each of the 26 deployed files' and folders' GUIDs back through `AssetDatabase.AssetPathToGUID` (all the committed ones) and finds every imported `.meta` byte-identical, and the XRI class compiled `ArticulatedRig/XR/`; `test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration` 5/5, the shader's relative `#include` resolving from `ShadowPlane/`; a wheel built from the tree carries all 26 `.meta` files and deploys them. A one-off batch probe on 6000.3.10f1 staged the exact pre-move bundle flat with no `.meta` files, the way 0.0.15 deployed it, in a project with URP. Unity imported it and minted GUIDs, the new `deploy_package` moved all 15 old files, and on the second import the package compiled with no duplicate class. All 18 scripts and assets kept the GUIDs Unity had minted for them, the 8 new folders took the committed ones, and Unity left those `.meta` files untouched. Both shaders, `EmissiveGroupsLit` included, compiled with their relative includes, in the flat layout and in the folders.

This one did not, so a full `--all` run wrote into the user's real temp dir, and so did every mayapy and Unity child it launched, since they inherit `TEMP`. The Unity projects the integration suites keep stay under `test/temp_tests/`. Checked with a full `--all` run inside the sandbox, beside a process poll. All 16 Unity batch runs were licensed, and each run's licensing client exited with its editor, since Unity keeps license state outside `TEMP`. The run's children wrote only into the throwaway root, which was gone at exit. Results: 259 tests, 0 failures, the same 13 environment skips as the unsandboxed baseline of 257 (+2 tests below). `test_run_tests.RunnerSandboxTest` drives a fresh runner and a fresh pytest over a probe that checks, at import time, that the process is sandboxed and its temp dir is the run's own root. Tests: `test_articulated_rig_runtime` holds the port to `ptk.ArticulationConformance.cases()` generated at test time (32 cases, 128 solves; bit-exact but for a 3.6e-14 solve difference), the controller to pythontk's solve through real transforms at a 0.37 unit scale, and the importer to a hand-written Maya-convention ASCII FBX -- which also pins the FBX rotation rule on imported joints (orients, all six rotate orders); the Animator override runs without play mode (each frame an edit-mode `Animator.Update(dt)` playing a clip, then the controller's own `LateUpdate`): the held and released pose win over the clip, `ReturnToAnimation` and `resumeOnAnimatorChange` hand it back -- and `ReadState` during a hold now returns the held pose (read between the Animator and `LateUpdate` it returned the clip's, 21.6 degrees off, and the next `LateUpdate` wrote the clip back). The floor still read `pythontk>=0.7.71`, older than what `UnityFinder.find_editors` now calls: `AppLauncher.scan_install_dirs` first ships in 0.8.72 (on an older pythontk the scan's AttributeError is logged and no editor is found), and it treats pattern order as a priority, so the Hub's chosen location wins over the default, from 0.8.84; the floor is 0.12.0, the pythontk this release ships with, whose `UserConfig.xdg_home` the Hub's Linux config dir now reads (a relative `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is invalid per the spec and was used as given). Test: `test_launcher.py` 15/15.
- **2026-09-26 - `UnityFinder.find_editors` scans through `pythontk.AppLauncher.scan_install_dirs` (`launcher.py`, `test/test_launcher.py`).** The hand-rolled `listdir` + per-OS existence checks become one pattern per Hub root over `UnityFinder.EDITOR_BINARY` (the binary's place inside a version folder, per OS); root priority and first-root-wins are unchanged, and a version folder without the binary is still not an editor. The Windows test runs on a real tree instead of mocked `listdir` / `exists`.
- **2026-09-26 - Linux: the Unity launcher reads the Hub's custom install location, and CI runs the suite on Linux too (`launcher.py`, `test/test_launcher.py`, `test/test_keyed_visibility.py`, `test/test_shared_material_standalone.py`, `test/test_standalone_enduser.py`, `.github/workflows/tests.yml`).** New `UnityFinder.get_hub_editor_paths()` returns every folder the Hub installs editors into: the one set in the Hub's preferences (`secondaryInstallPath.json` in the Hub's config dir on each OS) first, then the default. `find_editors` scans all of them, and the first root holding a version wins. Editors installed to a custom location, which is the usual setup on Linux, were not found before. The Windows default comes from `%ProgramFiles%` instead of a hard-coded `C:\Program Files`. The three standalone suites located Unity through a hard-coded Windows glob and now ask `UnityLauncher()`. `tests.yml` runs on a `windows-latest` + `ubuntu-latest` matrix. Tests: `test_launcher` (+1: the Linux default plus a custom Hub location; its home-dir stub expands only a leading `~`, as `os.path.expanduser` does -- the first publish run failed on Windows, where the runner's temp dir is the 8.3 name `C:\Users\RUNNER~1` and the stub rewrote that `~` in the scanned root). PyPI lists `Operating System :: Microsoft :: Windows` and `:: POSIX :: Linux` instead of `OS Independent` (`pyproject.toml`), and `docs/README.md` carries a Platform badge.

- **2026-09-23 - the one-release fixture alias map is gone (`test/base_test.py`, `test/test_base_test.py`).** `TestAssets.LEGACY_ALIASES` let a `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS` tree staged before the 2026-08-17 rename keep resolving under its old, program-identified filenames, and its own note said to delete it after the next release; unitytk has shipped four since (0.0.9 -> 0.0.13), and no tree here is staged under the old names (the variable is unset on this machine). `TestAssets.path` is a plain join under the root now, and `PublicHygieneTest` scans `base_test.py` like every other source file -- no Python or C# file may name the legacy assets. - **2026-09-22 - `LightmapMetadataController.cs` binds same-named objects by hierarchy.** `FindRenderer` matched a record to the first Renderer of its name, so a scene with two objects sharing a leaf name -- a production room's two machine bodies are both `BODY`, its tables both `TABLE` -- bound one machine's lightmap onto both, and mayatk warned on every export to rename them. `LightmapRecord` now carries the `hierarchy` mayatk publishes from 0.19.0 (the object's Maya DAG path, root first), and `ResolveRenderers` -- in place of the per-record `FindRenderer` -- settles each name's records and Renderers as ONE assignment, the rule pythontk's GLB applier follows: a record binds only where it and a Renderer are each other's unique best match by how many trailing entries of that path match the Renderer's ancestor chain (namespace-blind). A record whose best candidates all went to better matches has no object of its own here -- a partial export: the manifest is the whole scene's -- and is skipped, where a greedy per-record match would bind it to the other object of its name and overwrite that object's map; a tie, or same-named records with no hierarchy, are ambiguous and never guessed. `Rebuild` warns on a duplicate name only when the record has no hierarchy to be told apart by. mayatk's manifest message is an info line now. The templates compile clean under Unity 2022.3's Roslyn, and `test_lightmap_metadata_integration`'s in-memory verifier drives `ResolveRenderers` through both bodies, an absent same-named record, a partial export and hierarchy-less duplicates.

They now use `RenderEffects.create` / `key_fade` / `prepare_for_export()`, and the lightmap fixture drops its `commit_lightmap(intensity=1.0)` call (a deprecated parameter). `RenderEffects` has been the class since mayatk 0.14.14. The Unity-side names (`RenderOpacityController`, the log filter) are unchanged. Test-only, so there is no release and no pythontk floor change.
- **2026-09-21 - a push to main that changes what ships at the published version fails instead of going green (`.github/workflows/publish.yml`).** The publish job printed "already on PyPI" and passed whenever `__version__` equalled PyPI's, so 2026-09-20's deliberate hold at 0.0.12 and a forgotten bump looked identical. A new `version` job runs m3trik's `scripts/check_version_bump.py` over the push range before anything builds: a moved version publishes, a push that touches nothing under `unitytk/` or `pyproject.toml` passes, a shipped change at the published version is red and names the files, and a hold passes when the push adds a CHANGELOG line declaring it in the words the 2026-09-19 entry used (the 2026-09-20 push replays as a pass unchanged). `publish` -- and with it the registry-refresh dispatch -- now needs `version` and runs only when it says publish.

- **2026-09-14 - the opacity end-to-end tests expect the curve proxy, not a visibility mirror (`test/test_render_opacity_e2e.py`, `test/test_shared_material_standalone.py`).** mayatk's `RenderEffects.prepare_for_export` stopped keying `visibility` from `opacity` the same day -- presence is derived from the authored channels where a GLB is built, and Unity has read each object's curve off its `<leaf>__opacity` proxy since 2026-09-04 -- so both Maya export scripts now assert the proxy was staged and that nothing keyed visibility, and the shared-material FBX check counts one `Model::<leaf>__opacity` per object where it counted `Visibility` curves. The Maya leg (`test_01_maya_export`) passes; the Unity legs were not run in this change.

Only what a hand-added component starts at: the importer seeds the field per object from the file, so no imported prefab changes. Redeploy the templates for it to reach a project.

- **2026-09-07 -- the render-effects e2e pins the BRACKETED highlight pulse (`test/test_render_effects_e2e.py`).** mayatk's `key_pulse` now brackets its train with dim keys at `start` and `end` (2026-09-05: Maya holds a curve's first value back to the start of time, so a train that merely began bright glowed for the whole timeline before it), and the default lead-in is the cycle's own ramp -- which shifts the whole train by that ramp. This suite replays the same authored curve through a real Unity import and still asserted the old shape, so it was the one place the change was caught failing: `1 -> 1.0` was `0.0`. Expectations re-measured against the authored curve in mayapy for the fixture's exact params (`start=1, end=120, period=60, bright 50%, ramp 25%`) and confirmed by the round trip: `1 -> 0, 16 -> 1, 31 -> 1, 46 -> 0, 61 -> 0`. `test_render_effects_e2e` 3/3.
- **2026-09-07 -- `ShadowPlaneController.Placement` keeps the feet under the silhouette (`templates/ShadowPlaneController.cs`).** The canvas's near edge is now `u0 x` the far edge, not `u0 x` the base radius -- pythontk `ShadowModel.rect`'s feet-pinned stamp of the same day (the drawn feet slid forward with the far edge as a runtime light lowered). Redeploy the templates for the change to reach a project; `test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration` 5/5 against the model.
- **2026-09-05 — `ShadowPlane.shader` marches the height-field map; the coverage map's block is refused to the silhouette (`templates/ShadowPlane.shader`, `templates/ShadowPlaneHorizon.hlsl`, `templates/ShadowPlaneController.cs`, `test/test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration.py`).** pythontk retired the coverage-aware horizon map for a height-field ray march (its entry has the measurements: 7-22 % against the exact projection on furniture, now 1-5 %). The generated `ShadowPlaneHorizon.hlsl` mirror carries the new body; the host half's `SH_Fetch(tile, xi, yi)` addresses S-square tiles side by side with ONE row flip (a tile's row 0 is the PNG's top row; `Load` counts from the bottom) through the block's exact rect (never gutter-inset: a data map is addressed by texel). The instanced properties are `_HorizonParams` (size, spans, levels), `_HorizonBounds` (the footprint in the frame, scene units) and `_HorizonMeta` (height scale, ground in frame); `_HorizonRange` and `_MaxStretch` are gone with the cotangent encoding. `ShadowPlaneHorizon` reads the block's `mapping / encoding / size / spans / levels / bounds / height_scale`; the controller's fields follow, and **`IsHorizon` requires `encoding == 2`** -- an old map renders its silhouette (the R6 fallback) rather than a guess.
