The integration suite's fixture is now the real bake (`ptk.ShadowHorizon.bake` over two posts and a floating slab, `HeightFieldMap.alpha` the oracle, the PNG the very bytes Unity loads): 5 of 5 against a live 6000.3 batch import, the horizon pixels within the oracle's tolerance for a sizeless and a finite source.

- **2026-09-04 — `RenderOpacityController` becomes `RenderEffectsController`: opacity AND highlight, driven by the DCC's curve-proxy transport (`templates/RenderEffectsController.cs`, `templates/UnitytkSettings.cs`, `template_deployer.py`, `test/test_render_effects_e2e.py`).** The component gains `highlight` (0-1) and `highlightColor`, applied as `material emission + highlightColor x highlight` through the same MaterialPropertyBlock (composed over the material's own emission, exactly as the GLB route's `emissiveFactor` channel is written; `_EmissionColor`, falling back to HDRP's `_EmissiveColor`), beside the existing `_BaseColor.a` fade and auto-hide at zero. **Import.** Unity flattens animated custom properties onto the root Animator with empty paths, so several objects' curves of one attribute collapse; the importer used to reconstruct the fade from the stepped visibility mirror -- lossy, and with no analogue for a pulse. Both DCCs now stage one transient child transform per keyed channel, `<leaf>__<attr>`, stamped with the `curveProxy` user property, whose `scale.x` carries the curve; `RenderEffectsImporter` rebinds that `m_LocalScale.x` to `RenderEffectsController.<attr>` on every Renderer under the proxy's parent, strips the proxy's curves and deletes the node from the prefab, drops the root-flattened duplicates, and switches `_EMISSION` on for highlighted Renderers' materials (a property block cannot enable a keyword). A file exported before the transport existed still gets its opacity fade from the visibility mirror -- the fallback stays, per Renderer, wherever no proxy served it. `UnitytkChannel.Highlight` joins the gate (settings key `highlight`, panel row "Highlights"). The template is RENAMED, not aliased: a renamed template file gets a new script GUID, so an obsolete subclass would have bought nothing; the deployer's component key is `render_effects`. Verified against a live 6000.3 batch import through `test_render_effects_e2e` (Maya export inside `FbxUtils.export_prepared()` -> import): one controller on the Renderer, `highlight` and `opacity` both bound to it on the Renderer path, no orphan or proxy binding, no proxy GameObject, the colour seeded from the DCC (to 8-bit precision -- the FBX colour property round-trips quantised, 0.5 arrives as 128/255), the pulse's holds sampled at 1 and 0, `_EMISSION` on. `test_deploy_templates` follows the key.

- **2026-09-04 — `_AxisUp` is the contact's own up, not `Cross(B, A)` (`templates/ShadowPlaneController.cs`).** The two DCCs bake with opposite bearing senses (Maya's frame is `(X, Z)`, Blender's arrives as `(X, -Z)`), so a cross product of the bearing axes is `+Y` for one and `-Y` for the other -- which put every Blender-exported source below the horizon and drew nothing. The frame's up is the exporter's up; the runtime suite still measures 7143 pixels at mean |d| 0.0008 against `HorizonMap.alpha`.

- **2026-09-03 — `ShadowPlane.shader`'s horizon evaluation is now generated, and its test compares against the real oracle (`templates/ShadowPlaneHorizon.hlsl`, `ShadowPlaneController.cs`).** The 300-line HLSL horizon block is replaced by an include of `ShadowPlaneHorizon.hlsl`, a mirror of pythontk's `geo_utils/shadow_horizon.glsl` written by `m3trik/scripts/sync_shadow_shaders.py` and guarded by its `--check`; what stays hand-written is Unity's half — `SH_Fetch`, which owns the two row flips (the PNG's tile grid counts from the top, `Load` counts texel rows from the bottom), and the instanced property block. The file rides `ShadowPlaneController` through the deployer's longest-stem rule, so it lands in the optional `shadow_plane` component and not in the mandatory `core` one. **`_WorldToContact` (a `float4x4`) is gone**: the frame travels as `_Origin` / `_AxisA` / `_AxisB` / `_AxisUp` in world space, which takes fifteen floats per plane out of the instancing buffer and removes the one property that needed the Properties-block exemption; `_SourceLocal` becomes world-space `_Source`, and `_HorizonRange` drops the unit-scale slot (radii arrive scaled) for the ground plane's height in the frame — the shared body projects each fragment onto that plane before forming `L`, which the shipped shader did not. **`HorizonFixture`'s numpy oracle is deleted.** It was a transcription of this shader, so it pinned the shader to a copy of itself and could not see a divergence by construction; the test now goes through `HorizonMap.from_rgba` to `HorizonMap.alpha`. Its *bake* half stays — a map with two runs per bin and a floating disc is a fixture worth having. Measured on the rendered frame: 7143 pixels in the plane, 1306 in shadow, mean |shader − reference| **0.0008** for a point source and **0.0002** for a disc, p98 0.002.

- **2026-09-03 — Shadow planes gain the runtime rig: `shadow_metadata` v2, one instanced shadow shader for both rig types, and a controller that follows the light (`templates/ShadowPlane.shader` new, `templates/ShadowPlaneController.cs`, `templates/README.md`).** The Unity half of the shadow-rig plan of record (`mayatk/docs/shadow_rig_morphing.md`, "Contracts (implementation)").
  - **Records v2.** `ShadowPlaneRecord` gains `type`, `source`, `source_type`, `source_size`, `source_angle`, `follow_source`, `contact`, `ground`, `radius`, `height`, `max_stretch`, `canvas`, `atlas {texture, rect}` and `horizon {texture, bins, layers, tile, layout, mapping, r_min, r_max, frame_a, frame_b, encoding, rect}`; the payload gains `unit_scale` (metres per DCC unit). `SUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSION` is 2; every added field defaults, so a v1 payload imports exactly as before (verified: the two v1 scenarios of `test_shadow_plane_integration` stay green).
  - **`ShadowPlane.shader` (`m3trik/ShadowPlane`).** Legacy CG unlit-transparent with `multi_compile_instancing` — every per-plane value in the instancing buffer — so it renders in Built-in and URP's SRPDefaultUnlit pass (HDRP keeps the per-plane `HDRP/Unlit` rewire). `_Mode` 0 samples the silhouette or its atlas tile through `_Rect` (the R6 fallback: a plane with no controller shows its silhouette); `_Mode` 1 evaluates the horizon map per fragment in the contact frame per the contract's final channel spec: log-polar texel, `layers × bins` tile grid (row 0 at the PNG top; Unity's bottom-left rows flipped at address time), two layers per bin (grounded / floating), `B/A` a 16-bit sub-bin occupancy mask, `R/G` **cotangents** (`cot(angle) / max_stretch` clamped to 0..1: 0 = the zenith, 1 = the reach cap). The decode scale is the horizon block's own `max_stretch` — the cap the MAP was baked with — never the record's top-level one, which is the plane's live keyable placement cap: an artist retuning it after a bake would otherwise rescale every decoded shadow length by the ratio (measured on the fixture: 586 of 7143 pixels more than 0.5 apart). `ShadowPlaneController.HorizonMaxStretch` owns that choice — it falls back to the live cap only when the record carries no baked scale, so a map baked before the field existed still decodes — and `_MaxStretch` rides the block from it, while the placement model keeps using the top-level value — floating `R = cot(lo), G = cot(hi)`; grounded `lo` = the cap, `R = cot(hi)` of the first run of set sub-bins, `G` of the later runs, the light's sub-bin picking the run (run ≥ 2 reads `G` and holds, so two legs in one bin never share the nearer leg's shadow length); texels `Load`ed (never filtered) — 4 taps × 2 tiles × 2 layers, coverage bilinear over the bit at the light's sub-bin (a source disc's span exactly over the sub-bins it touches, the spill read from the neighbour's mask), the interval blended only over covered taps (an empty texel encodes the zenith), the neighbour chosen on the light's side of bin `k`'s coverage centre and lerped by the bearing's place between the two centres with hold-not-fade, the elevation test in cot space (`cot(hi) ≤ cot(e) ≤ cot(lo)`; a disc's `[cot(e+ρ), cot(e−ρ)]` overlap in cot units; a light below the horizon casts nothing), layers combined `1 − (1−αG)(1−αF)`. Alpha × `_BaseColor.a` (RenderOpacityController's channel) × `_Intensity`.
  - **Import.** Every plane of a rig type is rewired to ONE shared material (the first plane's imported sub-asset, instancing on) bound to the silhouette / atlas / `_horizon` PNGs found by name — `Shader.Find` missing or HDRP falls back to the old per-plane rewire. Atlased planes share the first plane's mesh with its UVs normalised back to 0..1 (the DCC remaps a packed plane's UVs *into* its tile; here the tile rides `_Rect`), and a plane whose atlas PNG is missing keeps its own mesh with the inverse of its UV rect so the silhouette still reads whole. `*_horizon` PNGs are pinned on first import as data: sRGB off, alpha-is-transparency off, no mips, clamp, point, NPOT kept, uncompressed, RGBA32 on Standalone and Android (`PinHorizonImportSettings`, the lightmap precedent). The controller is filled from the record (plus `unit_scale`); `frame_a`/`frame_b` are converted from FBX axes by the importer's X mirror. The record's `intensity` lands in `recordIntensity` and is NOT applied again — it already rides the DCC's baked `opacity` keys (`_expr_opacity` multiplies `shadowIntensity` in), so writing it into `_Intensity` too would square it and regress every v1 rig; `intensity` (default 1) is the runtime knob.
  - **Runtime.** `ShadowPlaneController` resolves `source` and `contact` by leaf name (nearest enclosing scope first, then the scene roots; cached, retried every 30 frames, `ResetResolve()` after a rename), evaluates `ShadowPlaneModel` — pythontk `ShadowProjection.model` + `ShadowModel.placement` ported term for term in **double** precision (a directional source sits `1e6` object sizes away; in `float` the reach lost ~10 %) — in `LateUpdate` while `followSource` and both resolve (otherwise the imported keys play), places the plane (centre, `ground + 0.01` lift, yaw `atan2(bearing.x, bearing.z)`, extents over the mesh's own bounds — the DCC's unit quad imports at `unit_scale` metres, exactly as the baked keys divide by `basePlaneSize`), and read-modify-writes the block: `_Rect`, `_Mode`, `_Intensity`, the horizon uniforms, `_WorldToContact` (the contact's rotation + position, unit scale), `_SourceLocal`, `_SourceRadius`; a per-plane texture goes into the block only when the shared material binds another. `ApplyNow()` for scripts and batch tests. Directional shine axis: a `Light` shines along `+Z`, a bare DCC transform along its local `−Z`.
  - **Pinned empirically through a real FBX** (`test_shadow_plane_integration.test_04`, new v2 fixture hand-built from the rigs' stamped attrs — the producer still publishes v1): Unity's importer is the **X mirror** (Maya `(4, 0, −3)` cm → `(−0.04, 0, −0.03)` m; baked `rotateY` negates), the plane mesh arrives at `0.01 × 0.01` m, a Maya `directionalLight` imports as a Unity `Light` whose `forward` is exactly the mirrored Maya `−Z` shine, and `ApplyNow()` reproduces the DCC's baked keys to ~1e-7 for a positional locator and for the directional light. The locator-as-directional convention (`−Z`) follows from the mirror and is exercised only synthetically.
  - **New `test_shadow_plane_runtime_integration.py`** (Maya-free batch Unity, `-batchmode` without `-nographics` so a camera can render; a machine with no graphics device env-skips the two pixel tests): six placements against pythontk within 1e-3 (generic, overhead within EPS, below the target's top, below the contact, directional from a locator and from a Light); the `_horizon` import flags and the block wiring (mode, params, range, source, no duplicated textures, RenderOpacityController co-residence); projected pixels through a top-down orthographic render (stripe top row on the near edge: near 0.996 / far 0.251, `_Rect` × `_Intensity` × opacity 0.0638 vs 0.0625); horizon pixels against a numpy oracle of the contract sampling the very same two-layer PNG (two grounded poles — the second on a later run of the same bin, so the `G` path renders — and a floating disc, baked analytically in the cot encoding): sizeless source mean |Δ| 0.0014, 0.33 % of pixels > 0.05 (bin-boundary texels), Unity umbra 1305 px vs oracle 1306; finite source mean |Δ| 0.0011, max 0.035; the fixture's map is baked at 4.0 while the record's placement cap is 6.0, so the pixels themselves fail if the two are confused, and the block reads 4.0 (6.0 when the baked scale is absent); the 25 pixels on a second run (the `G` path) average 0.749 in Unity vs 0.760 in the oracle, one boundary pixel 0.20 apart; and, independent of the oracle, the pole's shadow centroid lies along the bearing away from the light. `test_deploy_templates` pins the shader riding the `shadow_plane` component. Real results: deploy 29/29, integration 4/4, runtime 5/5, `SKIPPED (env): 0`.
- **2026-09-02 — Shadow planes import plug-and-play from an FBX exported with Embed Textures: the importer extracts the embedded silhouette beside the model and reimports once (`templates/ShadowPlaneController.cs`, `templates/UnitytkSettings.cs`, `templates/README.md`).** The DCC side (mayatk 2026-09-02) now binds the silhouette as the plane material's base colour, so the PNG rides an embedding export — but Unity's stock import never binds an FBX's embedded textures (measured 2026-08-25, re-measured here: `mainTexture` null after import), so the hand-copied PNG had stayed mandatory. `CarrierImport.RequestTextureExtraction(assetPath)` joins the per-asset request set: the shadow importer asks for it when a record's texture is not in the project, `ApplyPending` runs `ModelImporter.ExtractTextures` into the model's own folder after the batch and reimports the asset once, and the by-name lookup then succeeds; an FBX that embeds nothing extracts nothing and is not reimported, so a missing loose PNG cannot loop. The importer also keeps the material's own bound texture when the lookup fails (harmless today; the path that would matter once stock import binds embedded maps). `test_shadow_plane_integration` gains an embedded scenario — a second rig on another source, exported with Embed Textures on, its PNG never shipped loose — and verifies both against a licensed batch Unity: loose and embedded planes arrive unlit-transparent with the silhouette bound, casting/receiving nothing, controller attached, baked keys present. README's Shadows bullet: the Scene Exporter's smart bake makes the manual bake optional, Embed Textures replaces the hand-copied PNG, and the plane's fade rides its `opacity` custom attribute through the Render Opacity importer (visibility-pair fades; a ramp that never reaches zero is not reconstructed — the exact per-plane rebind is in FUTURE.md).
- **2026-08-23 — `publish.yml` now gates on the test job; it was publishing with nothing checked at all.** `publish` carried no `needs:`, so any push to `main` touching `unitytk/**` uploaded a wheel with zero verification — alone among the ecosystem packages (the other six gate on `tests.yml` or `static-analysis.yml`). `tests.yml` already declared `workflow_call`, so this is the standard two-job shape the cascade repos use. What a green gate certifies is bounded and stated in the workflow: `run_tests.py` exits 0 on a `[PARTIAL]` run, so it means everything reachable **without a DCC** passed — the env-gated half needs `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS` plus a real Maya+Unity pass, which no runner can provide. `workflow_dispatch` stays as the escape hatch if a Qt teardown ever wedges a release.

- **2026-08-23 — CI: a test job now runs on every PR (`.github/workflows/tests.yml`, new).** This repo publishes a wheel and had no test workflow at all. It runs `test/run_tests.py --all`, which reports env-gated modules as `[PARTIAL]` rather than green, so the suites needing `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS` cannot be mistaken for covered. `mayatk` is installed by name — it is a test fixture, not a dependency (the arrow points the other way: unitytk is mayatk's optional `[unity]` extra) — but with `--no-cache-dir`, and the editable install of this package resolves its own `pythontk` floor through a six-attempt retry loop. A stale PyPI CDN edge then fails loudly with "No matching distribution" and is retried instead of silently installing the previous release; measured 2026-08-23, a bare-name install resolved pythontk 0.9.28 82 s after 0.9.30 was uploaded. `CLAUDE.md` corrected to match: it documented the CI invocation as `--all --strict-env`, which this job cannot use — a GitHub runner has no Maya, no Unity and no `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS`, so ~45 tests env-skip there and `--strict-env` would make the check permanently red rather than informative. The consequence is now stated where it will be read: **a green `Tests` check does not mean the env-gated contract ran**, only that everything reachable without a DCC passed, with the `[PARTIAL]` banner and `SKIPPED (env): N` count in the job log saying so.

- **2026-08-18 — The Unity half of the environment-skip contract closes: an unlicensed machine can no longer stamp the badge.** Review follow-up to the 2026-08-17 entry below, which marked every *decorator* gate and left the in-body ones behind.
  - **`base_test.UnityEnv` owns the two reasons a Unity step is environmental.** Maya's absence is settled up front by a decorator, but `UnityLauncher.create_project` returning False and an editor that runs and exits unlicensed only surface *mid-test*, so eight suites took them as `self.skipTest("…")` with a hand-copied string — and fifteen of those copies forgot `EnvSkip.reason`. An unmarked reason contributes nothing to `EnvSkip.tally`, so `run_tests.py` saw `env_skipped == 0`, stamped the README badge and returned success on a machine that never imported one FBX into Unity; `--strict-env` never tripped. That is the exact hole `EnvSkip` was added to close, reopened for the half of the pipeline the marker had not reached. The reasons are deliberately **constant** — no return code, no log path: `tally` groups by reason text, so interpolating a per-run temp path would split one environmental cause across eight summary lines. The call sites `logger.info` the rc and the log path instead, so nothing diagnostic is lost.
  - **`test_base_test.UnityEnvTest` parses rather than greps for the regression.** A skip reason spans up to four source lines, so the guard walks the AST for `skipTest` calls given a *literal* (a reason built by a call is marked by construction) and fails on any whose text names a batch license or batch project creation. Run against the last commit it reports 16 offenders -- the fifteen above plus the clip-name project-creation skip already marked earlier in this pass; a grep-based guard would have missed the multi-line ones, which is most of them.
  - **`test_keyed_visibility` env-skips instead of failing red.** It was the only replay suite with no Unity gate at all: `assertTrue(unity_exe, …)` and `assertTrue(os.path.isdir(assets), "Unity project creation failed")` turned a missing editor and a missing batch licence into FAILURES. It now carries the second `@EnvSkip.unless(_find_unity_exe(), …)` decorator its sibling `test_standalone_enduser` already had, plus the two in-body skips. Pre-existing rather than introduced, but the 2026-08-17 pass that made env-gating a contract is where it should have been caught.
  - **The C# carrier match is generated, not pasted.** `UserPropsProbe.carrier_match_cs()` had zero production callers — three verifiers each carried a byte-identical copy with a literal `"data_export"` — so "one carrier rule, both halves" was documentation, not mechanism, and a change to `UserPropsProbe.CARRIER` would still have moved only the Python half. The verifiers now hold a `__CARRIER_MATCH__` token substituted at the write site, exactly as they already do for `__RESULTS_PATH__`. The 2026-08-17 guard banned only the `== "data_export"` form it had just removed; it now also bans a pasted `StartsWith("data_export"` and checks the token never ships unsubstituted.
  - **The user-properties sidecar keys by scene path, not by GameObject name.** A name is not unique in an imported hierarchy, so `_all[go.name] = props` collapsed two same-named objects into one entry and silently under-reported every count derived from the sidecar — a regression against the per-callback JSONL this replaced, which had the opposite flaw (it triple-counted re-imports). The path is unique *and* stable across the re-imports each suite performs, so it keeps the overwrite semantics that fixed the JSONL problem. `UserPropsProbe.is_carrier` matches the **leaf** of the key, so what counts as the carrier is unchanged.
  - **`test_multi_section_audio`'s opacity floor was re-derived for the new counting.** The probe swap changed `opacity_hits` from an append-per-callback substring count (~3x the truth: the FBX is imported three times) to a distinct-object count, but `assertGreater(opacity_hits, oc_count)` was left as it was — and `oc_count` includes the 12 propagated children, which are Renderers the importer synthesises curves for and which carry no user properties, so distinct carriers can never exceed it. Trivially-true became unsatisfiable. It now floors against the FBX's own 33 opacity nodes. The other candidate — having the C# emit an explicit direct-controller count — was rejected as more untested C# for the same statement. **Not yet executed**: this suite needs `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS` staged plus a real Maya+Unity pass (backlogged).
  - **`MayaEnv._resolve` also catches `AttributeError`.** `AppLauncher.resolve_app_path` landed in pythontk 0.8.64, well above this package's declared `pythontk>=0.7.71`; on an older-but-permitted pythontk the import succeeds and only the attribute is missing, so guarding on `ImportError` alone let an `AttributeError` escape — and all twelve integration suites resolve `MAYAPY` at module scope, so they would fail to *load* rather than skip. The floor is deliberately not raised: nothing in the shipped package uses `AppLauncher`, so this is a test-harness need and bumping `dependencies` would over-constrain wheel consumers.
  - `UserPropsProbe.carrier_props()` had no callers; rather than delete it, the three suites that inlined the same generator expression now call it. `test_base_test.py` 36 → 47 tests.

- **2026-08-17 — The integration suites stop reporting green without ever running, and the client program identifiers leave tracked source.** Two backlog entries, one pass, because they touch the same 16 files.
  - **mayapy is resolved, not pinned.** Twelve suites hardcoded `C:\Program Files\Autodesk\Maya2025\bin\mayapy.exe` and gated on `os.path.exists` of that literal, so on any machine whose Maya lived anywhere else — a different year, a different drive, Linux/macOS — the entire DCC→Unity consumer contract skipped and the run still exited 0. **`base_test.MayaEnv`** ports mayatk's shape (`test/run_tests.py:find_mayapy`): `UNITYTK_MAYAPY` → `MAYATK_MAYAPY` → newest standard install (`AppLauncher.resolve_app_path`) → PATH, resolved once per process. The 11 now-dead `_maya_available()` copies are gone; `MAYAPY` stays as the module constant the subprocess call sites already used.
  - **Environment-scoped skips are LOUD.** `unittest` files "Maya is not installed" in the same `skipped` bucket as "not applicable here", so the trailing `OK` was identical whether the contract ran or was skipped wholesale — which is *why* the hardcoded path went unnoticed. **`base_test.EnvSkip`** marks those reasons (`[env] …`); `run_tests.py` now prints a separate **`SKIPPED (env): N`** line with a per-reason breakdown, and when N is nonzero prints `[PARTIAL]` instead of a plain pass and **refuses the README badge stamp** (same rule as a load error: a count that never covered the gated slice is not coverage). New `--strict-env` turns it into exit 1 for CI; the default stays 0 so a dev box without Maya can still run the venv slice.
  - **One user-properties probe, one carrier rule.** Five suites each embedded a near-identical 50-line C# `AssetPostprocessor`, and the two halves of the carrier lookup had drifted: C# compared `t.name == "data_export"` while the Python assertions used `name.lower().startswith("data_export")`, so one run could answer "is the carrier there?" two ways. **`base_test.UserPropsProbe`** is now the single template (`deploy()` writes `UnitytkUserPropsPostprocessor.cs`), and **prefix, case-insensitive wins** on both sides — deliberately, not as a coin toss: Unity suffixes a colliding GameObject name on import (`data_export 1`), so the strict form reports a missing carrier on a hierarchy that plainly has one, and the carrier is produced by mayatk's own exporter, so an unrelated `data_export`-prefixed node is not a case that occurs. The former JSONL probe in the multi-section suite folds into the shared sidecar; its property counts are now **distinct GameObjects** carrying the property rather than raw substring hits (the assertion floors are unchanged and still hold).
  - **Client program identifiers removed before the repo goes public** (the 2026-08-15 entry below deliberately scoped itself to drive layout and left these). `test_<client>_<client>_integration` → **`test_multi_section_audio_integration`**, `test_<client>_visibility` → **`test_keyed_visibility`**; `Test<client>FcrIntegration` → `TestMultiSectionAudioIntegration`, `Test<client>Visibility` → `TestKeyedVisibility`; generated C# `<client>FcrVerifier` → `MultiSectionAudioVerifier` and `<client>FcrPostprocessor` → the shared `UnitytkUserPropsPostprocessor`; env vars `<client>_PRESERVE`/`<client>_PRESERVE` → `UNITYTK_PRESERVE` and `<client>_MANUAL` → `UNITYTK_MANUAL` (centralized on `base_test.SuiteOptions`, and **not** aliased — an alias would put the old names straight back into tracked source); the in-project asset name and the `<client>_Package` subfolder become `FBX_ASSET_NAME` / `MyModel_Package` constants, which also decouples the C# `"Assets/<name>"` literals from whatever the fixture is called on disk.
  - **The asset filenames are the one deliberate exception, because they live on the user's disk.** `fixture_multi_section_audio.fbx`, `fixture_audio_clips/` and `fixture_keyed_visibility.fbx` are the names now, but **`base_test.TestAssets.LEGACY_ALIASES`** keeps the old ones resolving for **one release**, so an already-staged `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS` tree does not break; `asset_path` prefers the neutral name and falls back only when the legacy entry is the one actually present, so fresh, legacy and half-migrated layouts all work. That map is the single place the old names may appear in tracked Python/C# source — enforced, along with the whole rename, by a new `PublicHygieneTest`. Mapping and deprecation documented in `CLAUDE.md`; delete both at the next release.
  - New `test_base_test.py` (36 tests: `EnvSkip` marking/counting/tally, `MayaEnv` resolution+caching+reset, `SuiteOptions`, the alias map's three resolution orders, `UserPropsProbe`'s two carrier halves and its deploy, plus four repo-wide source scans that keep the hardcoded path, the duplicated probe, the `==` carrier match and the program identifiers from coming back). `test_run_tests.py` +9 → 16 for the env-skip summary, the `[PARTIAL]` banner, the withheld badge and `--strict-env`. Scoped verification: 155 tests green across the eight venv-runnable modules, all 24 test modules import, and the two renamed suites env-skip through the runner with `SKIPPED (env): 5` and the `[PARTIAL]` banner (exit 0; exit 1 under `--strict-env`). The DCC→Unity half is unrun here — `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS` is unset on this machine, which is exactly the condition the new reporting exists to make visible.

- **2026-08-15 — Public-repo hygiene: the integration suites no longer carry a studio drive layout.** This repo publishes a wheel (`publish.yml` → `twine upload` on push) and is headed public, but four tracked test modules hardcoded absolute client paths — the cloud-sync vendor, the team-folder tree, a person's name, and the client program directories (`test_<client>_<client>_integration`, `test_<client>_visibility`, `test_standalone_enduser`, and the docstring header of the first). Client paths and program identifiers in tracked files leak business relationships, so they now resolve through **`base_test.asset_path`** off `UNITYTK_TEST_ASSETS`, mirroring mayatk's `MAYATK_TEST_ASSETS`. Unset yields a **can't-exist sentinel** rather than `""` — `Path("").exists()` is `Path(".").exists()`, i.e. `True`, which would betray any pathlib-style guard — so a suite's availability check skips cleanly. **Behavior change: with the variable unset those suites now skip**, so set it to keep the coverage (expected layout in `CLAUDE.md`). `test_<client>_visibility` needed a guard *added* to survive this: alone among the replay suites it had no class-level `skipUnless` and instead `assertTrue`d the FBX into existence inside the test body — harmless while the path was a hardcoded studio one that happened to be present, but it turned an opt-in root into a hard FAILURE (caught by the suite run, not by inspection). It now carries the same `_sources_available()` / `skipUnless` pair as its siblings. Scope, verified rather than assumed: the wheel never carried any of this — `pyproject`'s `packages.find include = ["unitytk*"]` and `MANIFEST.in`'s `recursive-include unitytk` both exclude the top-level `test/`; the only client reference that ever shipped was an illustrative comment in `templates/AudioEventController.cs` (`"Assets/<client>_Package"` → `"Assets/MyModel_Package"`). CHANGELOG history keeps vendor names by convention — it records what factually happened. **Scoped deliberately to the drive layout:** the client *program* identifiers stay in tracked source — the asset filenames, the module names, the `<client>FcrVerifier` class — and `CLAUDE.md` now documents the expected asset layout verbatim, because renaming them would decouple the suites from the assets they replay. That is a judgment call, not an oversight; revisit it before the repo actually goes public.

- **2026-08-14 — The integration suites' preserved Unity projects now have an owner: `temp_tests/` no longer grows without bound (it had reached 3.9 GB across 81 runs).** All 16 suites generate a whole Unity project per run and keep it *on purpose* — a failed import is only diagnosable from the project Unity actually built, so their `tearDownClass` logs the path instead of deleting it. With a raw `tempfile.mkdtemp` that left the artifact with no owner at all, which is precisely the leak the root CLAUDE.md rule names `ptk.TempArtifacts` to prevent; the temp-artifacts gate missed it because it exempts tests on the premise that "a test harness owns its own teardown", and these deliberately renounce teardown.
  - **`base_test.TempProjects.allocate(prefix)`** replaces the copy-pasted `_temp_base` / `os.makedirs` / `mkdtemp(dir=…)` triple in all 16 files (and the two-line rationale comment each carried — it now lives once, on the class). It wraps `ptk.TempArtifacts` in `detached` policy, so allocating sweeps same-prefix runs older than `MAX_AGE_DAYS` (3) while leaving the current one untouched. The sweep is **prefix-scoped**, which is load-bearing: all 16 suites share one `temp_tests/` base, so an unscoped age sweep would delete a sibling suite's in-flight project — and `vis_probe/`, the hand-made repro harness two backlog entries point at, is owned by no prefix and is therefore never a sweep target.
  - Verified against the real directory: 72 stale runs reclaimed, 8 same-day runs and `vis_probe/` kept — 3.9 GB → 86 MB. New permanent coverage in `test_temp_projects.py` (7 tests: allocation, distinctness, stale-swept, fresh-preserved, prefix-scoping, default location, and creation of a **missing** base — the 16 suites each called `os.makedirs` before allocating, so centralizing had to keep that guarantee for a fresh clone). The now-dead `import tempfile` is dropped from all 16.

- **2026-08-14 — `run_tests.py` no longer reports a green run when a test module fails to import.** A module that raises on import contributes zero test cases, so counting only the failures of what *did* load called a broken suite a pass: the per-module `except` printed `[ERROR] Failed to load module` and the run still exited 0 — caught live when `test_temp_projects.py` was added against a not-yet-existing symbol. A syntax error or bad import in any suite therefore read as green, and the README badge stamped the reduced count as if it were coverage. Load failures are now collected, summarized as a `[FAIL]` line, fail the run, and **refuse the badge stamp**; a genuine test failure stays red exactly as before. The same defect had a second form in the same function: a **requested module that does not exist** (`run_tests.py launchr`) printed a `[WARNING]`, ran nothing, and exited 0 — so a typo in a CI invocation passed silently. Unknown names now fail the run too, and both forms are kept out of the badge. Covered by `test_run_tests.py` (7 tests, driving the real runner against broken / missing / passing / failing modules on disk). Dropped the write-only `self.results_file` while in there — grep-confirmed unread repo-wide. Scoped verification for both entries: 110 tests across the seven venv-runnable modules, all green.

- **2026-08-14 — The "runs from any checkout" bootstrap now covers the integration half of the suite, and survives a renamed clone.** Two gaps in the same-day sweep below: it inserted only `unitytk`/`pythontk`, while ~10 modules (`test_fade_window_preservation`, `test_render_opacity_controller`, `test_audio_events_integration`, `test_shadow_plane_integration`, …) import `mayatk` at parent-process top level — two of them had inserted the mayatk repo root explicitly before the sweep and lost it, so a fresh checkout without an editable mayatk failed at collection, before any skip decorator could run. `uitk` joins it for the same reason. The host repo is also inserted from `parents[1]` unconditionally rather than resolved as `SCRIPTS_ROOT/"unitytk"`, so a clone named anything else (`unitytk-fork`) no longer silently skips insertion via the `isdir` guard and reproduces the exact `ModuleNotFoundError` the sweep set out to remove; siblings still resolve by canonical name.

- **2026-08-14 — Test suite runs from any checkout: hardcoded absolute workspace-root bootstraps removed (16 files); `base_test`/`run_tests.py` derive the workspace root from their own location and insert the sibling repo roots (`unitytk`, `pythontk`) — never the bare workspace root, which resolves siblings as empty namespace packages.** The embedded mayapy child scripts drop their literal path loops too: every launcher already builds the child's `PYTHONPATH` from the (now derived) root with the same package set, so child semantics are identical. `pyproject` gains the standard pytest config (`testpaths`/`pythonpath`/temp_tests ignore). Scoped verification: 96 tests across the five venv-runnable modules, all green.

- **2026-08-02 — the deployed C# set is selectable per script instead of all-or-nothing, and the panels' Manage-Unity-Scripts reporting moves here.** A project that only imports shot metadata had no way to take just that: `deploy_package` wrote every controller, and `uninstall_package` was whole-folder. The bundle has real structure — each `*Controller.cs` is one import channel that compiles against `UnitytkSettings.cs` alone — so that structure is now first-class rather than implicit prose in a docstring.
  - **`TemplateDeployer.components()` → `PackageComponent(key, label, files, required, summary)`**, *derived from the bundle*, not a hard-coded table: every `*Controller.cs` becomes one optional component (key/label split off the filename on real word boundaries, acronyms included: `EmissiveGroupController.cs` → `emissive_group` / "Emissive Group", a `UVLayoutController.cs` → `uv_layout` / "UV Layout") that claims any non-C# bundled asset sharing its stem, so `EmissiveGroupsLit.shader` + `EmissiveGroups.hlsl` can never travel apart from the controller that needs them (the shader `#include`s the .hlsl by relative path); everything left over — `UnitytkSettings.cs`, the `.asmdef`, the catalog README — is the required `core`. Ownership is **longest-stem-wins**, so adding an `EmissiveController.cs` beside `EmissiveGroupController.cs` can't hand the shader pair two owners — a doubly-owned file would deploy twice and survive the first uninstall, desyncing deploy from remove. A controller added to `templates/` therefore surfaces as a new option in every consumer with nothing to register.
  - **`deploy_package(project_root, components=None)` / `uninstall_package(project_root, components=None)`** take that selection; `None` still means the full set, so every existing call site is unchanged. Deploy is additive (Install / Update, not Sync) and always writes the core the selection compiles against; uninstall refuses a required component by name and drops the whole folder once the last controller goes, rather than leaving a settings page gating no channels. `uninstall_package` now returns the **removed paths** (was `bool`) to mirror `deploy_package` — truthiness is unchanged, so `if uninstall_package(...)` still reads the same.
  - **`status()` gains `components` (per-component `installed`/`missing`) and `incomplete`, and `up_to_date` now means "current version, nothing broken".** Measuring against the full set made every deliberate opt-out look like a failed install: `missing` (kept, full-set view) can't distinguish "this project doesn't use shadows" from "half the shadow files are gone". `incomplete` is the genuinely broken set — a component partly present, plus a core that a deployed controller has nothing to compile against.
  - **`run_action(project_root, action, components) -> [(level, message)]`** — the status/install/uninstall reporting the Maya, Blender and standalone Unity panels each had their own near-identical copy of. One implementation now, testable without a DCC or Qt: an empty selection is a reported error rather than a silent no-op (`None` is still the full set), and a bad key or action comes back as an `error` line instead of escaping into a DCC event loop.
  - **Released as 0.0.8, and the consumers enforce that floor at RUNTIME, not just in `pyproject.toml`.** `components()` / `run_action()` are a hard dependency of the new Manage-Unity-Scripts panels: the `components()` probe degrades to the full set, but the action itself called `run_action` unguarded. Raising `unity = ["unitytk>=0.0.8"]` from `>=0.0.2` in `mayatk`/`blendertk`/`extapps` is necessary but *not sufficient* — an extra only constrains a fresh install, so a session already carrying 0.0.7 imports fine and then `AttributeError`s inside the panel handler. The panels now probe the versioned requirement (`uitk`'s `optional_package_available("unitytk>=0.0.8")`, see uitk's CHANGELOG), so a stale release reads as unavailable and routes the user to Install / Update, which resolves the constraint through pip. unitytk is off-chain (floor-pinned, not cascaded by `push.ps1`), so it publishes *before* the ecosystem release that requires it.
  - `test_deploy_templates.py` +16 → 27 (derivation and file ownership, shader-pair grouping, longest-stem ownership against a synthetic prefix-colliding bundle, acronym-aware keys/labels, selective/additive deploy, selective uninstall + the last-component rule, core-not-removable, per-component status, controller-without-core, and the four `run_action` shapes). Catalog README: selective-deploy recipe in Setup, and the CS-compile-errors troubleshooting row rewritten around components + `status()["incomplete"]`.

- **2026-08-01 — 0.0.7: small consistency fixes from the DCC→Unity data_nodes architecture review.** (1) `AudioEventImporter.ParseManifest` null-guards `JsonUtility.FromJson`'s result — a manifest of the literal `null` NRE'd on the version read inside the `try` and surfaced as a misleading generic "Failed to parse" warning. (2) `ShotMetadataController.Rebuild` warns on a duplicate clip name instead of silently keeping the first record — matching the lightmap channel's behavior for the same case (the DCC side resolves take/clip names in one pass, so a duplicate indicates a producer bug upstream that was previously invisible). (3) `EmissiveGroupImporter` passes a CamelCase display label to `WarnIfNewer` like every other channel, instead of the raw attr string. (4) README's import-contract table gains the `emissive_groups` row it was missing (the channel was fully implemented, toggleable, and tested — just absent from the contract table), and its blendertk coverage note now lists `shadow_metadata` + `emissive_groups`. (5) `test_audio_events_integration.py`'s docstring no longer claims the controller attaches to `data_export` — the test itself asserts the opposite (root-attach, carrier explicitly clean) — and its Unity half now matches the sibling tests' harness contract: `UNITYTK_TEST_EDITOR` is honored and a failed batch project creation `skipTest`s (typically a license wall) instead of hard-failing the suite on unlicensed machines. `test_deploy_templates.py` 11/11.

- **2026-07-29 — 0.0.6: the runtime half of the template set gets its first live coverage, and four defects fall out of it.** Everything shipped so far was verified import-side; what the MonoBehaviours actually do to a Renderer had never been exercised in a Unity scene. New `test_controller_runtime_integration.py` (Maya-free batch Unity: cube + Standard material, both controllers driven directly, MaterialPropertyBlock read back) closes that, and immediately caught the first two of these:
  - **Opacity now resolves its color property at runtime instead of trusting the serialized name.** `colorProperty` serializes as `_BaseColor` (URP/HDRP) while a Built-in material exposes `_Color`; when the two disagree `HasProperty` fails and the apply returns early every frame, so the object hard-pops at zero instead of fading. Measured on the real import path first (`test_render_opacity_controller` now reads the value back): `Reset()` *does* run on the postprocessor's `AddComponent` and the material *is* already assigned, so the standard Maya-FBX-into-Built-in flow was auto-detecting correctly — this was **not** a broken-in-the-common-case bug. What `Reset()` cannot cover: a material assigned after the component (streaming, hot-swap, prefab variants), a prefab authored under one pipeline and opened under another (the serialized value is stale and `Reset` never re-runs), and components added at runtime in a player build, where `Reset` is `#if UNITY_EDITOR` and never exists. `ResolveProperty` closes all of those — configured name first, falling back `_BaseColor` → `_Color`, latched per material (steady state is a reference compare) and re-derived from `colorProperty` each resolve so a fallback forced by one material cannot outlive it and beat an explicitly configured property the next material does expose.
  - **`RenderOpacityController` teardown wiped every other override on the Renderer.** `ClearOverrides` called `MaterialPropertyBlock.Clear()` on the whole block, erasing `EmissiveGroupController`'s `_GroupWeights` (both controllers legitimately share one Renderer) and any user override. It now writes the material's own color back and leaves the rest of the block alone.
  - **Emissive brought in line with the other four channels.** It was the only one with no stale-controller branch, no `ShouldProcess()` gate on `OnPostprocessAnimation`/`OnPostprocessModel` (relying on `_pending` as an implicit gate instead), and it re-parsed the manifest once per AnimationClip — a multi-shot FBX paid for the identical `JsonUtility.FromJson` N times, now memoized. On the stale branch specifically, measured rather than assumed: the new integration test greps the importer's own removal log and finds it **never fires**. Unity rebuilds the imported hierarchy from the FBX on every reimport, so a controller added by a previous import is simply not on the fresh root — which means the equivalent branches in ShotMetadata, AudioEvent, Lightmap and ShadowPlane are not load-bearing either. Kept in all five as belt-and-braces (one `GetComponent` per import) rather than deleted, since it is what guarantees the user-visible contract if that Unity behaviour ever changes; the test asserts the contract and records the mechanism separately so neither is mistaken for the other.
  - **The texture preprocessors were still overwriting user settings on every reimport** — the one place 0.0.4's "leave the user's settings as they had them" wasn't actually honoured. `*_EMask` / `*_shadow` had sRGB, compression, alpha and mip settings re-pinned on every single import. Now first-import-only (`assetImporter.importSettingsMissing`), so they are sensible defaults for a newly added texture rather than an enforced policy, and an Inspector change sticks. README's safety section documents the suffix matching and that the FBX allowlist does not apply to textures.
  - **`EmissiveGroupsLit.shader` no longer logs a warning in every non-URP project.** Its `FallBack "Universal Render Pipeline/Simple Lit"` is only ever *reached* in a non-URP project (the SubShader's `RenderPipeline=UniversalPipeline` tag makes it the match under URP) — where that name cannot resolve either, so every Built-in install logged `fallback shader ... not found` as pure noise against a shader that is never auto-assigned. Dropped; verified against the real Unity logs, which also confirmed Unity suppresses the templates' `CS0649` warnings (zero compiler warnings on install).
  - New public `ApplyNow()` on both controllers — pushes the current value without waiting for the next frame (the honest API for script-driven opacity/weights), self-initializing so it also works before `OnEnable`, which a component added from an editor script never receives. Catalog README gains the `EmissiveGroupController` row it had been missing entirely.

- **2026-07-29 — 0.0.5: import-settings applier bounded to one nesting level.** `SaveAndReimport` inside `OnPostprocessAllAssets` re-enters the applier through the nested import's own postprocess-all pass — correct, but one nesting level of the FULL native import pipeline per pending asset, so a folder-drop of N carrier FBX nested N deep. A re-entrancy guard keeps the loop flat: the nested pass no-ops, its re-requests (settings now satisfied) purge on the next batch. Also unstales the 0.0.3 panel wording in the catalog README (the field-menu button became the Manage Unity Scripts template) and the mirrored parameter-module docstrings (visibility DOES gate per combo entry now). Full live batch-Unity suite re-run green.

- **2026-07-29 — 0.0.4: importers scoped to their own assets (safety pass), and the deployer grows the management surface the DCC panels drive.** Follow-up to 0.0.3 after a production-safety review: the panel-facing question "do these scripts affect assets they aren't even associated with?" had a real *yes* behind it, now removed.
  - **Per-asset import-settings scoping — unrelated FBX are never modified.** Previously every importer's `OnPreprocessModel` forced `importAnimatedCustomProperties = true` on every allowlisted FBX (default: all of them), and the opacity importer additionally forced `resampleCurves = false` — altering animation import for production assets with no unitytk data. A live batch-6000.3 probe established the key fact: **FBX user properties reach `OnPostprocessGameObjectWithUserProperties` with Unity's default settings** (string channels *and* the animated `opacity` attr; only the flattened curve needs the flag). So detection is now zero-touch: the string channels (audio/shots/lightmaps/shadows/emissive manifest) need no importer settings at all, and the two animated-data settings are **requested per asset on detection** (`CarrierImport.RequestAnimatedCustomProperties` / `RequestSparseCurves` — opacity requests both; emissive only when the manifest carries keyed-weight `attr` records) and applied by the new `UnitytkImportConfigurator` in `OnPostprocessAllAssets` (the supported reimport point), which sets the flags on that one asset, logs, and reimports it once. Change-detection stops loops; nothing is ever switched *off*; a detection pass skips the opacity curve conversion (`CarrierImport.IsDetectionPass`) rather than fabricating fades from resampled data. README gains a "Safety: unrelated assets are never modified" section.
  - **`TemplateDeployer.status(project_root)` / `uninstall_package(project_root)` / `package_dir(project_root)`** — the engine surface behind the DCC panels' new *Manage Unity Scripts* template (status / install-update / uninstall as a panel-native parameterized flow, replacing 0.0.3's field-menu button). `status` never raises (absent is a reportable state: version vs bundled, missing files, `up_to_date`); `uninstall_package` refuses to delete a folder whose `package.json` does not identify it as `com.m3trik.unitytk`. `test_deploy_templates.py` +3 → 11.

- **2026-07-29 — 0.0.3: the template set becomes a real Unity citizen — one embedded UPM package, native Project Settings, one Tools menu.** unitytk had shipped loose `.cs` files the user copies into `Assets/Scripts`, configured by a JSON hunted for anywhere under `Assets/`, with menu items scattered across three `Tools` roots. Since nothing had adopted the published package yet, the structure was fixed now rather than migrated later:
  - **`TemplateDeployer.deploy_package(project_root)` replaces `deploy_all(scripts_dir)`** (breaking; never adopted). It writes the full compile-coupled set into `Packages/com.m3trik.unitytk/` together with a generated `package.json` (version = the unitytk release) and a new `M3trik.Unitytk.asmdef` — Unity picks the folder up as an *embedded package*: it lists in the Package Manager, stays out of the user's `Assets/` tree, updates in place on re-run, and uninstalls by deleting one folder. The old "do NOT put these in Assets/Editor" footgun is gone with the location choice. Guards against scattering: raises `ValueError` when *project_root* has no `Assets/`. `deploy(filename, output_path)` stays for single-file overwrites. The catalog `README.md` now ships inside the package (it's the Package Manager's docs surface); per-project config is never written by the deployer.
  - **New `UnitytkSettings.cs` — the shared compile-coupled home.** `ImportAllowlist` + `CarrierImport` move here out of `RenderOpacityController.cs` (the opacity controller was an arbitrary host for shared infrastructure). The allowlist generalizes into **`ImportGate.ShouldProcess(assetPath, UnitytkChannel.X)`**: one gate = per-channel enable toggle + FBX allowlist. Settings live in **`ProjectSettings/UnitytkSettings.json`** (JsonUtility round-trip, mtime-cached with a 2s throttle; missing file/keys = everything on) — replacing the hand-rolled JSON scanner that recursively searched `Assets/` for `maya_fbx_import_config.json` (template deleted). External tools write the same file the UI edits.
  - **Native settings UI, not a custom manager window.** A `SettingsProvider` page at **Edit ▸ Project Settings ▸ unitytk**: one toggle per channel (Opacity, Audio, Shots, Lightmaps, Shadows, Emissive — each importer early-outs on its toggle, including the two texture preprocessors, which gate on channel only since the FBX allowlist can't apply to PNGs) plus the allowlist as a one-per-line text area (applied on focus loss/button, not per keystroke). Unity's Package Manager answers "what's installed", Project Settings answers "what is it doing" — no bespoke EditorWindow to discover.
  - **Menus consolidated under `Tools ▸ unitytk`** (`Render Opacity/…`, `Audio Events/…`, `Lightmaps/Apply Scene Lightmaps`, `Settings`) — previously three unrelated top-level `Tools` roots (`Render Opacity`, `Audio Events`, `Maya Lightmaps`).
  - **DCC panels gain "Install/Update Unity Scripts"** on the Unity Project field's option menu (mayatk + blendertk `unity_bridge_slots`, extapps `unity_workflow` — in their repos): `ensure_optional_package("unitytk")` then `deploy_package(project)`, so provisioning the Unity side is one click from the exporter that needs it. Nothing in production had called the deployer before — it was tests-and-docs only.
  - Tests: `test_deploy_templates.py` rewritten around the package (manifest identity/version, non-Unity-folder rejection, never-writes-project-config, coupling pins moved to `UnitytkSettings.cs` as the one definer, +3 → 8); the six batch-Unity integration tests now deploy via `deploy_package`; the standalone/manual-copy scenarios include `UnitytkSettings.cs` in their minimal sets and write the allowlist to `ProjectSettings/UnitytkSettings.json`. `.asmdef` added to `package-data`/`MANIFEST.in` (wheel-content check applies).

- **2026-07-29 — unitytk joins the ecosystem's encapsulation standard, and 0.0.2 is the first release to actually reach PyPI since February.** Two things were wrong and they compounded. **(1) The publish workflow was a no-op.** `publish.yml` greps `__version__` out of `unitytk/__init__.py`, compares it to `pip index versions unitytk`, and uploads only when they differ — but `__version__` had been the literal `"0.0.1"` since the repo was created, so every run since 2026-02-24 concluded *success while publishing nothing*: a green check that meant "skipped". PyPI still served the February wheel, which contains only `launcher.py`, `scene_builder.py`, two `.cs` templates and a `deploy_template` function — no `asset_delivery.py`, no `deploy_templates`, no Emissive Groups anything. Since `mayatk`, `blendertk` and `extapps` all declare `unitytk>=0.0.1`, a pip install of any of them resolved that February copy; the gap went unnoticed because this workspace imports unitytk from its local clone. Version bumped to **0.0.2** so the upload actually fires. **(2) Packaging dropped the shader assets.** Neither `MANIFEST.in` nor `[tool.setuptools.package-data]` listed `*.hlsl` / `*.shader`, so `EmissiveGroupController.cs` shipped while `EmissiveGroups.hlsl` and `EmissiveGroupsLit.shader` — which it needs, and which `EmissiveGroupsLit.shader` includes by relative path — did not. Caught by building the wheel and listing its contents rather than reading the declarations; both now list the extensions, re-verified by rebuild.

  **Encapsulation (public-surface change).** The ecosystem-wide "no root-level functions" sweep covers unitytk too, and it had three left: `deploy_template`, `deploy_templates` (package root) and `sanitize_asset_name` (`asset_delivery`). They now live on classes, per the standard that a leaked module function is an API-surface defect:

  | was | now |
  |:---|:---|
  | `unitytk.deploy_template(name, path)` | `unitytk.TemplateDeployer.deploy(name, path)` |
  | `unitytk.deploy_templates(scripts_dir)` | `unitytk.TemplateDeployer.deploy_all(scripts_dir)` |
  | `unitytk.sanitize_asset_name(name)` | `unitytk.CopyToAssetsDeliverer.sanitize_asset_name(name)` |

  New `template_deployer.py` hosts `TemplateDeployer` (classmethods only — the bundle is fixed at install time; named to avoid colliding with the `templates/` package it reads through `importlib.resources`, so it resolves from a wheel as well as a checkout). `sanitize_asset_name` becomes a staticmethod of the deliverer that was its only caller. The package root is now imports + `__all__` and defines nothing. Only `deploy_template` was ever published, so for anyone installing from PyPI this is effectively a first-release surface rather than a break; the ~30 in-repo call sites across 11 test files, both DCC shadow-rig tooltips (`mayatk`/`blendertk`), `mayatk/docs/shot_export_unity.md` and the template catalog were all migrated. Suite unchanged at **112 tests, 111 passed / 1 skipped**.

  **Docs fix found on the way:** `templates/README.md` still said the compile-coupled set was "five `.cs` files" in two places — it has been six since `EmissiveGroupController.cs` landed, and the shader pair's travel-together requirement wasn't stated at all.

- **2026-07-28 — `run_tests.py` now stamps a README Tests badge (111 passed).** unitytk had a runner but no badge, so its coverage was invisible next to every sibling package's. The badge goes through the ecosystem writer `ptk.StatusBadge` (individual test cases, skips excluded) and only a run that covered every discovered module writes it — a scoped run would otherwise publish a partial count that reads as a coverage regression. New `--no-badge` flag matches the other runners. Standard: [m3trik/docs/TEST_BADGE_STANDARD.md](../m3trik/docs/TEST_BADGE_STANDARD.md).

- **2026-07-28 (curve transport) — `EmissiveGroupController.cs`: the importer now also reads Blender's keyed-weight transport (scale.x curve proxies), so both DCCs' weight animation arrives identically bound.** Blender's FBX exporter cannot ship custom-property animation, so blendertk's Scene Exporter stages one transient child empty per keyed group — NAMED the manifest `attr` — whose `m_LocalScale.x` carries the 0-1 weight curve (scale: the one transform channel unit conversion never touches). `OnPostprocessAnimation` gains the second match: a binding whose path *leaf* equals a manifest `attr` with propertyName `m_LocalScale.x` rebinds to the same `groups.Array.data[i].weight` target the Maya (root-flattened custom-property) pass uses; every remaining baked transform curve on the proxy path is stripped, and `OnPostprocessModel` deletes the proxy node from the prefab (`FindDeepChild` by exact name — a no-op on Maya-produced FBX, where the attr is an attribute, not a node). Compiles clean against Unity 6000.3.10f1 editor assemblies via the bundled Roslyn; the DCC-side transport is FBX round-trip-proven in blendertk (imported `scale.x` evaluates 1.0→0.0), the Unity-side rebind compile-proven pending the licensed session. Contract doc: `mayatk/docs/emissive_groups.md` §Keyable weights.
- **2026-07-28 (keyable weights) — `EmissiveGroupController.cs`: DCC-keyed weight curves now import pre-bound to the controller.** The DCC keyable-weights opt-in keys each group's weight on a uniquely named `data_export` custom attribute (`emissiveGroup_<name>`); Maya's FBX export ships those as animated custom properties, which Unity flattens onto the root Animator with the attribute name as `propertyName`. Because every group's attr is unique (unlike RenderOpacity's shared `opacity`, which needs the visibility dual-key reconstruction), the flattened curves stay unambiguous: new `EmissiveGroupImporter.OnPostprocessAnimation` maps each manifest `attr` (new `EmissiveGroupRecord.attr` field, schema still v1/additive) to its controller list index and rebinds the curve to `groups.Array.data[i].weight` at the prefab root, dropping the Animator orphan. The index contract is pinned by a shared `SortedGroups` helper — the SAME slot-sorted order `OnPostprocessModel` writes the controller list in (previously it trusted the producer's ordering; now both sort defensively), and the payload is *peeked* from the pending map since the animation callback fires before the model one consumes it. Manifest parse extracted to a shared `ParsePayload`. Compiles clean against Unity 6000.3.10f1 editor assemblies via the bundled Roslyn (`-define:UNITY_EDITOR`); in-editor import verification still owed to a licensed session (rc-198 wall). Contract doc: `mayatk/docs/emissive_groups.md` §Keyable weights.
- **2026-07-28 — New Emissive Groups templates: `EmissiveGroupController.cs` + `EmissiveGroups.hlsl` + `EmissiveGroupsLit.shader` — the Unity half of the mayatk/blendertk Emissive Groups tool.** The controller follows the established carrier pattern (`CarrierImport`/`ImportAllowlist`, ShotMetadata-style pending-JSON handoff): `EmissiveGroupImporter` parses the `emissive_groups` FBX user property, attaches a populated controller to the prefab root (name/slot/default per group, schema-version warning via `WarnIfNewer`), and enforces mask-texture import settings on `*_EMask*`/`*_EmissiveMask*`/`*_EmissiveGroups*` (linear, HQ compression, alpha-as-data — block artifacts on a hard-edged multi-channel mask read as faint cross-group glow under bloom). Runtime: per-group weights pack by slot into `_GroupWeights` (Vector4) applied to every child Renderer via MaterialPropertyBlock (change-detected per frame; **an MPB opts a renderer out of SRP batching** — stated plainly, unlike the older templates' comment); unassigned slots stay ON so ungrouped emissive texels keep glowing; a disabled controller reverts to all-on (the material default, matching the all-on bake); `SetWeight`/`GetWeight`/`SetAll` + animatable `groups[i].weight`. Shader side avoids both a URP-Lit HLSL fork and hand-authored `.shadergraph` JSON (GUID-fragile): `EmissiveGroups.hlsl` is the pipeline-agnostic gate (`saturate(dot(mask, weights))`, Custom Function node compatible), and `EmissiveGroupsLit.shader` is a self-contained URP (12+) Blinn-Phong material with vertex-color or texture mask source behind a toggle (ForwardLit + ShadowCaster + DepthOnly; SH ambient by design — dynamic-emissive props aren't lightmap-static, and baked GI ignores runtime toggles anyway). `deploy_templates` now ships `.shader`/`.hlsl` alongside the `.cs` set (the Lit shader includes the .hlsl by relative path, so they must travel together). Verified: all six templates compile clean against Unity 2022.3.40f1's editor assemblies via the bundled Roslyn (batch-editor verification is walled by the known rc-198/machine-binding license failure on the dev box — in-editor import + shader compile owed to a licensed session). Contract doc: `mayatk/docs/emissive_groups.md`.
- **2026-07-12 Review follow-ups: schema-version warning ungated, `CarrierImport` coupling guard, stricter `SceneBuilder` validation.**
  - **`ShotMetadataController.cs` / `LightmapMetadataController.cs`: `CarrierImport.WarnIfNewer` now fires whenever a payload parsed**, not only when it yielded records. A *newer* producer schema that no longer fits the v1 shape parses to zero records and previously took the stale-controller-removal branch silently — the prefab lost its controller with no "update the unitytk templates" hint, the exact case the warning exists for. `AudioEventController.cs` already warned unconditionally; the three JSON importers now match. Stale-removal behavior itself is unchanged.
  - **`test_deploy_templates.py` now also pins the `CarrierImport` compile-coupling** (exactly one definer — `RenderOpacityController.cs` — and every other template a referencer), mirroring the `ImportAllowlist` guard via a shared `_coupling_surface` helper: a partial deploy mixing a pre-`CarrierImport` opacity template with current controllers is a CS0103 the Python suite can now catch (4 → 5 tests).
  - **`SceneBuilder.install_builder_script` rejects C# reserved keywords and non-ASCII class names** — `class_name="class"` generated `public class class` (a CS error only visible in Unity's log), and Python's `isidentifier()` accepted non-ASCII names; validation is now `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*` plus a reserved-keyword frozenset. Also rejects a `"` in `scene_output_path` (it would terminate the embedded C# string literal mid-path, CS1010). TDD: failing tests first in `test_scene_builder.py` (+3 → 8).
  - CHANGELOG accuracy fixes to the 2026-07-11 hardening entry: the launcher TDD count read "+4 → 14" (3 tests were added, 11 → 14), and the "Documented, not changed" bullet claimed the README documents the multi-take audio limitation — that note was superseded the same day by manifest-v2 clip scoping and never shipped.
- **2026-07-11 — Data-flow elegance pass: `audio_manifest` v2 (clip-scoped JSON), schema-version checks, shared `CarrierImport` helpers.**
  - **`AudioEventController.cs` consumes manifest v2** — `{"version": 2, "events": [{"clip", "frame", "name"}]}`, produced by mayatk the same day (see its CHANGELOG): an event with a `clip` is injected **only** into the AnimationClip of that name (case-insensitive — the same join key `shot_metadata` uses), fixing the multi-take duplication where every event fired in every clip; empty `clip` = every clip (single-take exports). The v1 wire-string parser stays for pre-v2 FBX (the checked-in <client>H regression FBX pins that path live). JSON also lifts v1's no-spaces restriction on event names.
  - **Schema-version checks in all JSON importers** — `shot_metadata`/`lightmap_metadata`/`audio_manifest` payloads from a *newer* producer now log a clear "update the unitytk templates" warning instead of silently best-effort parsing (`CarrierImport.WarnIfNewer`).
  - **New `CarrierImport` helper class** (in `RenderOpacityController.cs`, the compile-coupled home of `ImportAllowlist`): `HasProp`/`TryGetProp` (case-insensitive user-prop scan), `EnableUserProps`, `WarnIfNewer` — replaces four hand-rolled copies of the same scan/preprocess logic across the templates; adding a new carrier channel is now a ~20-line importer.
  - **Side-by-side integration test now proves clip scoping end-to-end**: a third track (`land`) inside Outro's range, Maya-half asserts the exact scoped event set (`Intro: footstep@9, jump@29; Outro: land@9` — capture moved after export so it reads the hook-rebaked manifest), and the Unity verifier dumps injected `OnAudioEvent`s per clip (`clip_events`) with per-clip assertions. `test_audio_events_integration` asserts the v2 JSON envelope (unscoped case). `templates/README.md` documents the v2 contract and drops the (now fixed) multi-take limitation note.
- **2026-07-11 Production-hardening pass over the Maya→Unity import scripts (C# templates + Python engines).**
  - **All four `AssetPostprocessor` importers: per-asset static state now resets in `OnPreprocessModel`.** The per-`assetPath` accumulators (`_opacityTransforms`, `_pendingManifests`, the two `_pending` JSON maps) were only cleaned at the END of a successful import — an import aborted between callbacks (any postprocessor throwing) stranded them, so the *next* import of the same FBX would: inject every audio AnimationEvent **twice** (Step 1 `AddRange`s onto the survivor), resurrect stale shot/lightmap JSON on a re-export that removed the carrier, or trip `EnsureControllers` on destroyed `Transform` refs. A fresh-import reset at the top of the pipeline makes every import self-contained.
  - **`AudioEventController.cs`:** injected AnimationEvents are now set in ascending time order (stable `OrderBy` — injected events landed after surviving user-authored ones, so the list wasn't sorted on arrival); `OnAudioEvent` self-heals / warns instead of `NullReferenceException` when the `AudioSource` was removed post-import.
  - **`LightmapMetadataApplier` (Apply Scene Lightmaps):** records sharing one baked atlas now share one `LightmapData` slot (was: one duplicate entry per record); renderer bindings are undo-recorded and the scene is dirtied so the applied lightmaps actually **survive a scene save**; textures are cached per map filename (no repeated `FindAssets`); inactive objects (e.g. keyed to fade in later) are included; the log reports renderers *and* distinct lightmaps. Dropped the stale `COMPILE-UNVERIFIED` header — the template has compiled in the live Unity integration runs since 2026-07-01.
  - **`RenderOpacityController.cs`:** `LateUpdate` no longer latches `_prevOpacity` when the Renderer's material is missing or lacks the color property — a material assigned later now receives the current opacity on its first available frame instead of waiting for the next value change; editor `OnValidate` keeps the cached `Shader.PropertyToID` in sync with Inspector edits of `colorProperty`; `ImportAllowlist`'s config search no longer swallows IO exceptions silently.
  - **`UnityLauncher` auto-select was lexicographic** — with `2022.3.4f1` and `2022.3.10f1` installed it launched **4f1** (`"10" < "4"` as strings). New `UnityFinder.version_sort_key` orders numerically and ranks release channels `a < b < f < p`; unparseable folder names sort lowest instead of raising. TDD: failing tests first in `test_launcher.py` (+3 → 14). Also: `create_project` no longer passes `cwd=""` for a bare folder name, and an explicit version/path that can't be resolved now logs a clear warning up front (it is deliberately NOT swapped for another install — the launch fails on the requested version instead of silently opening a wrong one).
  - **`SceneBuilder`:** generated builder scripts are now written UTF-8 (Windows default cp1252 mojibaked non-ASCII setup code), the scene path is normalized to forward slashes before embedding in the C# string literal (backslashes formed escape sequences → CS1009 only visible in Unity's log), `class_name` is validated as an identifier up front, and the generated script guards `Directory.CreateDirectory` against a root-relative scene path (empty dirname → `ArgumentException`). New `test/test_scene_builder.py` (5 tests) closes the coverage gap flagged in the 2026-07-01 audit.
  - **Documented, not changed (producer-contract gap):** `lightmap_metadata`'s `uvIndex`/`intensity` are carried but informational-only in the native-lightmap path (README says so). This pass also found the scene-global audio manifest injecting every event into every clip of a multi-take FBX — fixed the same day by the manifest-v2 clip scoping (entry above), so no limitation note shipped.

- **2026-07-10 — Review-pass fixes for the ShadowPlane template's supporting surfaces.** `test_deploy_templates`' pinned template set and allowlist-referencer set include `ShadowPlaneController.cs` (the new template landed in the deploy glob but the coupled-set tests still asserted four files — the suite was red as committed); `templates/README.md` gains the missing **Shadows** DCC-side-requirements bullet (create via `ShadowRig`, bake before export, ship the silhouette PNG, keep material import on) and the troubleshooting row says five files; the batch-Unity verifier binds the renderer **by name** the way the importer itself joins records (a second renderable in the exported group can no longer silently become the assertion target). Full suite re-verified green.

- **2026-07-10 — New `ShadowPlaneController.cs` template: a mayatk/blendertk `ShadowRig` export becomes a working Unity shadow on import, zero manual setup — behavior-verified against a licensed batch Unity.** The DCC side publishes a `shadow_metadata` JSON channel onto the shared `data_export` carrier (`{version, planes:[{name, texture, intensity}]}` — mayatk refreshes it at export time via `FbxUtils.run_export_preparers`' known-producer registry; blendertk publishes at authoring time), so the standard Scene Exporter flow ships it with zero extra steps. On import the template joins each record to its renderer **by GameObject name** (the lightmap-channel pattern), rewires the imported material **in place** to a pipeline-correct unlit-transparent shader bound to the silhouette PNG resolved by the recorded filename (Built-in `Unlit/Transparent`; URP Unlit + transparent surface via property/keyword names only — no SRP assembly references, so it compiles in any project; HDRP best-effort with a revalidation warning), turns off shadow casting/receiving + light/reflection probes on the plane's renderer, attaches a `ShadowPlaneController` marker, pins `*_shadow` texture imports (`alphaIsTransparency`), and removes stale markers when a re-exported FBX no longer carries the channel. **Two Unity importer traps found live and documented in the template header:** the default ImportViaMaterialDescription pipeline (which maps Stingray/Arnold materials to "Autodesk Interactive") never calls the legacy `OnAssignMaterialModel`, and it applies its description mapping AFTER `OnPostprocessMaterial`, silently overwriting anything set there — the rewire must happen at `OnPostprocessModel` time, when the embedded materials are final sub-assets (all three hook strategies were run against a real batch Unity; only the third survives). New `test_shadow_plane_integration.py` (pattern of the lightmap integration test): real `ShadowRig.create` + `bake` + `run_export_preparers` in mayapy → shadow group + carrier exported → batch-Unity verifier asserts shader, bound texture, renderer flags, marker component, PNG pinning, and the baked keys arriving as an AnimationClip — 3/3 green. Gated by the shared `ImportAllowlist`; `deploy_templates` picks the new file up automatically (glob); `templates/README.md` updated (five-template set, `shadow_metadata` contract row).

- **2026-07-10 — Lightmap apply path is now compile- AND behavior-verified end-to-end (the template's last untested half).** `test_lightmap_metadata_integration.py` grew a real apply phase: the Maya side now writes a real solid-value float EXR (not an empty stand-in), the python side ships it into the Unity project beside the FBX, and the batch-Unity verifier instantiates the imported prefab into a fresh scene and calls the REAL `LightmapMetadataApplier.ApplySceneLightmaps()` — a direct compile-time reference, so the previously COMPILE-UNVERIFIED template is now compiled by the test on every run. Asserted: the texture lands in `LightmapSettings.lightmaps` (count 1, `NonDirectional`), the `Crate` renderer gets `lightmapIndex` 0 + identity `lightmapScaleOffset`, and `PinLightmapImportSettings` actually pinned the importer (Lightmap type, sRGB off, mipmaps on). 3/3 green on a licensed batch Unity (~34s). Template header updated to reflect verified status.

- **2026-07-09 `LightmapMetadataController.cs`: the apply half hardened — import settings pinned, atlas slots deduped, auto-bake clobber guard, color-space / uvIndex / duplicate-name warnings.** A contract review against the mayatk producer found the applier was the weak half of the bridge. **Import pinning**: `LoadLightmapTexture` previously set only `textureType = Lightmap` and trusted platform defaults for the rest — on the wrong defaults a linear HDR EXR lands sRGB and/or 8-bit. New `PinLightmapImportSettings` pins sRGB **off**, mipmaps **on** (the Maya bake dilates gutters for exactly this), and per-platform HDR block formats (Standalone → BC6H, Android → ASTC-HDR 4×4), reimporting only when something actually changed. **Atlas dedupe**: objects sharing one atlas EXR (mayatk `pack_atlas`) previously each got their own `LightmapData` entry pointing at the same texture; they now share a single slot (dictionary keyed by texture) and differ only by `lightmapScaleOffset` — the point of the atlas. **Clobber guard**: manually assigned `LightmapSettings.lightmaps` are wiped wholesale by any Unity lighting bake, so the applier now switches 'Auto Generate' (iterative GI workflow) to on-demand before binding, with a log line. **Warnings**: Gamma color-space projects (the maps are linear HDR irradiance — they decode wrong), records with `uvIndex != 1` (Unity's native lightmaps only sample uv2; the Maya side now publishes the *real* index instead of a hardcoded 1), and duplicate record names in `Rebuild()` (the join key is first-match-wins). **`intensity` is now documented as informational** — the Maya side applies a non-1.0 value into the texels at commit (Unity's `LightmapData` has no per-map multiplier; previously the field was parsed and silently ignored, a dead contract field). Template remains COMPILE-UNVERIFIED (no Unity license on the dev boxes); the deploy-set coupling tests and the full suite stay green. `templates/README.md`: applier row rewritten for the new behavior, and the Lightmaps DCC-requirements bullet now states the Linear-project requirement and the intensity semantics (default 1.0 = Maya-render parity; `math.pi` for Unity-native light parity — Arnold bakes radiance `albedo·E/π`).

- **2026-07-08 Templates README: cross-repo links made wheel-safe.** The four producer-spec links added the same day (`mayatk` `data_nodes.md`/`shot_export_unity.md`, `blendertk` `data_nodes.md`) were monorepo-relative (`../../../mayatk/...`) — but `templates/README.md` ships in the wheel (`*.md` package-data) and renders on the standalone GitHub repo, where all four 404. Now absolute GitHub URLs.

- **2026-07-08 `deploy_templates` — one-call deployment of the full compile-coupled C# template set; templates README now catalogs all four controllers + the import contract.** The four controller templates are compile-coupled (each calls the shared `ImportAllowlist` class that only `RenderOpacityController.cs` defines), so a partial copy leaves a Unity project with CS compile errors — yet `deploy_template` (singular) invited exactly that, and every integration test hand-rolled its own file list. New `deploy_templates(scripts_dir)` deploys every bundled `.cs` (never the per-project `maya_fbx_import_config.json` or the README); `deploy_template` keeps working for single-file overwrites and its docstring now warns about the coupling. New `test/test_deploy_templates.py` (4 tests) pins the deployment behavior **and the coupling contract itself** — exactly one template defines `ImportAllowlist`, every other one references it — the only compile-adjacent guard available without a licensed Unity on the dev box. `templates/README.md` was still describing a two-file package (opacity + audio): now catalogs all four controllers (incl. `ShotMetadataController` and `LightmapMetadataController`, shipped since earlier but undocumented), documents the **FBX user-property import contract** up front (the `data_export` carrier GameObject, the channel/format/consumer table, `OnPostprocessGameObjectWithUserProperties`, and the carrier-must-be-exported caveat with its stale-controller-removal behavior), links the producer-side specs (mayatk `data_nodes.md` / `shot_export_unity.md`, blendertk `data_nodes.md` — whose export path was fixed the same day, see blendertk's CHANGELOG), and gains Shots/Lightmaps rows under DCC-side requirements plus troubleshooting entries for missing metadata and partial-copy compile errors. `docs/README.md` (the pyproject landing) links the producer spec directly. **Also fixed: `test_audio_events_integration` was stale since mayatk's 2026-07 audio-taxonomy change** — its embedded mayapy script still asserted the pre-taxonomy contract (`audio_manifest` present on `data_internal`), so the whole module failed (and its Unity steps skipped) on any current mayatk; it now asserts the live contract (manifest is a plain channel on `data_export` and must NOT exist on `data_internal`, matching mayatk's own suite). Full suite after the fix: **95 tests, 0 failures, 1 deliberate skip** (the `<client>_MANUAL`-gated GUI test), incl. the live Maya + Unity batch integration tests. `docs/README.md` (the pyproject readme) was a single sentence; it now describes the package and links the C# `templates/README.md` catalog, the changelog, and `CLAUDE.md` — whose Nav now links the landing back. Part of the monorepo docs-standard pass.
- **2026-07-02 Docs: `audio_manifest` template comments synced with mayatk's plain-channel taxonomy fix.** No code change on the unitytk side — `unitytk/templates/AudioEventController.cs` and `unitytk/templates/README.md` comments updated to match mayatk's `AudioClips.prepare_for_export` taxonomy fix (see mayatk `CHANGELOG.md` same date): reworded "proxied from `data_internal`" to "baked/stamped as a plain string channel onto `data_export`" throughout, and noted that `prepare_for_export()` is now automatic once a track exists (creating an audio track self-registers a before-export hook) rather than something the consumer must call manually before every FBX export.
