- **2026-07-01 Test-suite audit — clean full run, no code changes needed.** Monorepo-wide test-cleanup pass (already applied to pythontk/uitk/mayatk/tentacle). Full run (`test/run_tests.py --all`, all 16 modules incl. the live Maya + Unity batch-mode integration tests): **91/91 tests, 0 failures, 1 deliberate skip** (`test_<client>_<client>_integration::test_03_gui_launch`, gated behind `<client>_MANUAL=1` for manual GUI inspection — not environmental). No Unity license-wall (rc 198) skips hit; ran against a real licensed Editor. Confirmed every `launch_editor` call site passes `-batchmode` (or is the opt-in manual GUI test against a scratch project the test itself created — no live-session attach). Swept `test/test_output/last_audio_events.log` (gitignored scratch). Fixed `CLAUDE.md`'s documented run command — it referenced a nonexistent `run_all_tests.py`; the actual runner is `test/run_tests.py --all`. Noted, not touched: `SceneBuilder` (unitytk/scene_builder.py) has no dedicated test file — pre-existing coverage gap, not introduced here; the `MAYAPY` mayapy.exe path constant is duplicated verbatim across 10 integration test files (DRY opportunity, no bug).
- **2026-06-19 `FileToUnityBridge` — DCC-free, file-driven Unity hand-off engine.** New `HandoffBridge` subclass in `asset_delivery` that copies an *already-exported* model file into a Unity project's `Assets/` via the same `CopyToAssetsDeliverer` Strategy the DCC `UnityBridge` engines use — the file-input sibling of mayatk/blendertk's selection-driven bridges. `requires_objects=False`; `send(model_path=, *, template=, mode=, params=)` wraps the picked path as the payload (`default_asset_name` = file stem). This is the engine behind the rebuilt standalone **Unity Workflow** panel (extapps) and the Blender "Unity Bridge" (export selection → FBX → hand the path here), so the marmoset/substance "one DCC-agnostic panel + thin DCC consumers" architecture now applies to Unity with the engine in a real package (no vendoring). `CopyToAssetsDeliverer.deliver` now **preserves the source file's extension** (was hardcoded `.fbx`) so a picked `.obj`/`.usd`/`.gltf` isn't mislabelled; DCC exports are `.fbx` so that path is unchanged. Exposed: `FileToUnityBridge`. Tests: `test_asset_delivery.py` (+6 → 24).
- **2026-06-19 `CopyToAssetsDeliverer` — launch *mode* (none / windowed / headless) replaces the on/off launch.** Field report: "Send to Unity" copied the FBX but Unity never opened — because the launch was a default-off boolean the user never set. `deliver` now reads a `LAUNCH_MODE` param (`""`/`"open"`/`"headless"`) via the new `_resolve_launch_mode` (the brief same-day `LAUNCH_EDITOR` boolean back-compat was dropped — no presets predate it, so only `LAUNCH_MODE` drives launching). `_launch_or_focus_editor` → renamed `_launch_editor(bridge, params, *, batch_mode=False)` (the old name promised a focus-existing-instance behavior it never had); `"headless"` runs `launch_editor(batch_mode=True, extra_args=["-quit"])` — the explicit `-quit` is required or the windowless batch process hangs (no `-executeMethod` to drive it). Honest caveat surfaced to the panel: headless needs a batch-capable Editor license (the rc-198 wall). Tests: `test_asset_delivery.py` (open=windowed, headless=batch, no-launch, legacy-bool ignored; version-forwarding + method-rename cases).

- **2026-06-19 `CopyToAssetsDeliverer` — version-aware editor launch.** `_launch_or_focus_editor` now honors an optional `UNITY_VERSION` param (a version string like `2022.3.4f1`, or an explicit `Unity.exe` path) so a host panel can pick which installed Editor opens the project; blank/absent auto-selects the newest install (prior behavior). Consumed by the Unity bridges' new "Unity Studio" delivery mode. Tests: `test_asset_delivery.py` (+1 → 15: version forwarded to the launcher's `executable_path`).
- **2026-06-19 `CopyToAssetsDeliverer` — copy-to-Assets hand-off Strategy.** New `asset_delivery` module: a `pythontk.Deliverer` Strategy that copies an exported FBX into a Unity project's `Assets/<subdir>/` (Unity ingests it on window focus — session-safe, no batch-license wall, never disturbs a running editor) and optionally launches the editor via `UnityLauncher`; plus `sanitize_asset_name`. Plugged in as the `deliverer` of **both** mayatk's and blendertk's `UnityBridge` (the copy + launch logic lives once, next to the launcher engine). The host bridge supplies `project_path` + a produced `Payload` (FBX + `default_asset_name`); this Strategy supplies the `preflight` + `deliver` hooks. The `ASSETS_SUBDIR` field is hardened against path traversal (normalizes either separator, drops `.`/`..`/empty components so the copy can't escape `Assets/`). Exposed: `CopyToAssetsDeliverer`, `sanitize_asset_name`. Tests: `test_asset_delivery.py` (14).
- **2026-06-09 Maya lightmap bridge — `LightmapMetadataController.cs` template + integration test.** Consumes the `lightmap_metadata` manifest mayatk's Lightmap Baker (lighting-only mode) publishes onto the shared `data_export` node — so an externally baked lightmap wires into Unity's native lightmap system with the **full PBR material kept** and no custom shader / per-object sidecar. New template [`templates/LightmapMetadataController.cs`](unitytk/unitytk/templates/LightmapMetadataController.cs): a runtime `LightmapMetadataController` (records `{name, map, uvIndex, intensity, scaleOffset}`), an editor `LightmapMetadataImporter : AssetPostprocessor` that reads the FBX user property, attaches the controller to the prefab root, and warns if Unity's lightmap-UV generation is on (Maya supplies the lightmap UVs on channel 1 — it doesn't silently override the import setting, since with no allowlist config every FBX is processed), and an editor menu `Tools ▸ Maya Lightmaps ▸ Apply Scene Lightmaps` (`LightmapMetadataApplier`) that binds the textures into `LightmapSettings` for the open scene's matching renderers. Mirrors the `ShotMetadataController.cs` pattern and reuses its `ImportAllowlist` gate (deploy both templates together). Integration test [`test/test_lightmap_metadata_integration.py`](unitytk/test/test_lightmap_metadata_integration.py): the Maya half (commit_lightmap → FBX with `data_export` → manifest round-trip, material kept) runs here; the Unity readback half gates on the batch-license wall. Template is compile-unverified (no Unity license on the dev boxes), consistent with the other `.cs` templates.
